About:

Robomata helps fleet operators turn fragmented receivables and diligence evidence into lender-ready borrowing-base packets through an agent-supervised credit workflow.

Walrus is the verifiable evidence and artifact layer: operators upload receivables and diligence files, Seal encrypts sensitive evidence, Walrus stores the encrypted artifacts, and Sui commitments anchor the evidence roots used by lender packets and monitoring agents. This lets lenders review and monitor credit state without exposing raw private financial or operational files on-chain.

About:

WalrusOS is a persistent memory protocol and runtime for autonomous AI agents. Agents from multiple vendors—Anthropic (Claude Desktop via MCP), Google (Gemini), any MCP-compatible client—register on-chain identities on Sui, sign every memory write with Ed25519, store content as blobs on Walrus, and recover their full state from the network alone after total local data loss.

On Walrus all memories are content-addressed and on Sui they are cryptographically anchored. This allows any third party to verify the entire on-chain history independently . The runtime adds capability-based access control, an event mesh for reactive agent collaboration, and a coordination engine that decomposes a goal and routes tasks to agents by capabilities.

About:

### SpinChain — Walrus-as-Memory for On-Chain Fitness

### What It Is

SpinChain is an AI-powered indoor cycling protocol that uses **Sui** for settlement and **Walrus** for verifiable storage of high-frequency fitness data. Riders stream telemetry (heart rate, power, cadence), receive real-time AI coaching, and earn rewards settled on-chain via batched transactions and ZK proofs.

Its key innovation is the **Walrus-as-Memory** pattern: lightweight on-chain anchors on Sui reference large telemetry and AI-memory blobs stored on Walrus, making high-volume fitness data verifiable, composable, and cost-efficient.

### The Problem

Fitness data is high-frequency, large-scale, and privacy-sensitive. A single 45-minute ride at 10Hz generates ~27,000 data points—too expensive for full on-chain storage.

Current approaches fall short:

- Full on-chain storage → prohibitively expensive

- Off-chain only → unverifiable and non-composable

- Centralized servers → not decentralized

### The Solution

**Sui (Settlement & Identity)**

- `Session` manages ride lifecycle

- `RiderStats` stores batched ride metrics (~80% gas reduction)

- `TelemetryAnchor` links rides to Walrus blobs

- `Coach` stores references to AI prompts and memory

- `SPIN` token powers incentives

**Walrus (Verifiable Memory)**

- Full telemetry time-series

- Ride summaries and routes

- AI coach prompts and decision logs

### How It Works

- Telemetry streams during a ride and is batched into Sui updates.

- At completion, the full compressed telemetry dataset is uploaded to Walrus.

- A `TelemetryAnchor` is minted on Sui, permanently linking the ride to the Walrus blob.

- AI coach memory is stored on Walrus and anchored on-chain for portability and verification.

### AI Coach

A multi-provider fallback architecture ensures reliability:

- Venice AI (primary)

- NVIDIA NIM / MiniMax-M3

- Gemini Flash (fallback)

The coach adapts to rider personalities (e.g., drill sergeant, zen master, data analyst) and generates real-time guidance, interval coaching, and ride summaries. Its prompts and reasoning history are stored on Walrus as verifiable memory.

### Current Status

**Testnet live**, including:

- Deployed Sui Move package with telemetry anchoring

- Walrus upload/retrieval with failover

- Real-time ride dashboard and route visualization

- Rider onboarding and personality profiles

- AI coaching and narrative generation

- Noir-based ZK reward prototype

- 132/132 tests passing

### Why It Matters

The **Walrus-as-Memory** architecture extends beyond fitness. Any application generating large, high-frequency data—IoT, gaming, scientific research, or AI agents—can use the same model: **small on-chain anchors + large verifiable Walrus blobs**.

SpinChain demonstrates this pattern in fitness, where data integrity is critical but raw data volumes make pure on-chain storage impractical.

### Tech Stack

- **Frontend:** Next.js 15, React 19, Tailwind v4, React Three Fiber

- **Blockchain:** Sui (Move), Avalanche (Solidity), Walrus

- **AI:** Venice AI, NVIDIA NIM, Gemini

- **Privacy:** Noir ZK proofs

- **Mobile:** Capacitor + BLE scaffolding

- **Deployment:** Vercel, Sui Testnet, Avalanche Fuji

About:

Walnut is an Intelligent NFT on Sui that lets users truly own, run, sell, and evolve a private AI agent instead of just owning a pointer to public metadata. The agent’s “brain”, including model weights, prompt, persona, or memory, is encrypted and stored on Walrus, while Seal releases the decryption key only to the current NFT owner through an on-chain ownership policy. The app fetches the encrypted model from Walrus, decrypts it only for the rightful owner, runs real local inference, and denies access to non-owners. Trading is handled through Sui Kiosk with royalties, so after a sale the new owner can run the agent and the previous owner loses future access.

**Tribunal** — an on-chain court for agent societies. Stake-gated advocates argue a contested question; a persona-diverse jury deliberates; a guardrail judge with a locked prompt makes the binding call. The full proceeding is written to Walrus as one typed Quilt (six entries: debate, jury, guardrail, verdict, case law, provenance), anchored on Sui testnet. Precedent accumulates as recallable case law — judgment that remembers its own rulings.

About:

**Cell (cell.sui) is personal, infinite* generative art funded by micropayments**

Try it out at [https://celll.exe.xyz/](https://celll.exe.xyz/) . This submission is about a real, self-contained, and operational project.

Each user onboards by naming their own cell, creating an addressable account under the cell.sui namespace. Their cell grows through rectilinear visual segments, with every segment remembered over time and backed up to MemWal.

Growth is funded directly by USDC in the cell’s own account: **1 cent = 1 heartbeat**. Heartbeats power both the visual expansion of the cell and the persistent memory of its growth.

The project combines SuiNS, Sui accounts, gasless USDC micropayments, generative art, persistent memory, and MemWal into a living onchain/offchain organism: a named cell that can keep growing as long as it is funded.

The native sui ecosystem allows users to fund their cells or those of their friends with with USDC to addressable names like adeniyi@cell.

The expansion of cell.sui plans to introduce gamification and rewards to cell growth, as well as more sophisticated LLM-driven cell growth and autonomy: At its core, Sui Cells are fundable agents with memory and visual progression.

This description is written without the assistance of LLMs/AI.

About:

**Patchway**

The verifiable handoff layer for AI agents on Sui.

Multi-agent AI systems pass work between agents constantly, but the handoff itself

is invisible and unverifiable — there's no receipt, no provable control over shared

memory, and no way to prove afterward that one agent's access to another's memory

was actually revoked. Patchway makes the handoff a first-class, on-chain object.

Agents get a permanent identity (Channel), durable memory on Walrus (Thread), and a

formal handoff (Relay) that grants the recipient scoped, time-bounded,

auto-revoking access to the sender's memory — then proves the revocation from chain

alone. It ships as one SDK, plus a CLI and an MCP server, working under any agent

framework (LangGraph, CrewAI, MCP, A2A), with a developer console and public,

shareable proof pages.

**PROBLEM**

AI agents increasingly hand off work to each other — research to analysis to planning —

But the coordination layer is off-chain and opaque. Shared memory is passed by

copying data or handing over long-lived API keys, with no scoping and no audit

trail. When a task ends, you simply trust that access was withdrawn; nothing proves

it. As agents act more autonomously, "trust me, it was revoked" isn't good enough —

There's no receipt, no provenance, no way to verify a handoff after the fact.

**SOLUTION**

Patchway turns each handoff into a verifiable on-chain object. A Relay moves work

from Agent A to Agent B and grants B a scoped delegate key into A's memory for the

duration of the task; on completion the key is removed. The access window (granted

and revoked epochs) and the granted delegate key are recorded on-chain, so verify()

proves — by reading the sender's memory account on Sui and confirming the key is

gone — that access opened on accept and was actually revoked on complete. Memory lives on Walrus, with integrity anchored to an on-chain digest hash. Developers get

all of it through one SDK with just a keypair; the console renders a shareable

public proof page for any handoff.

**WHY WALRUS**

Walrus is Patchway's memory layer — it's what makes agent memory durable and

portable instead of trapped in one runtime. Every Thread entry, every relay

artifact, and every handoff digest is stored as a Walrus blob; agents write,

recall, and reuse that memory across sessions and across the handoff. Crucially,

Walrus is the verifiable half of the proof: each digest's bytes are hashed and

anchored to an on-chain digest hash, so verify() re-fetches the Walrus blob and

re-checks SHA-256 against the chain — a handoff's data is provable, not just

claimed, and the public proof page re-verifies it live (with a durable cache

fallback so proofs survive blob expiry). The scoped, auto-revoking memory access a

Relay grants is access into Walrus-backed memory. Walrus isn't a storage sticker

here — it's the substrate the entire verifiable-handoff guarantee rests on.

**WHY SUI**

Channels and Relays are Sui shared objects; the Relay emits RelayAccessGranted and

RelayAccessRevoked events with the delegate key and access epochs. The revocation

proof works because Sui's object model lets verify() read the sender's

memory-account object directly and confirm the granted key is absent — proof from

chain state, not a database. Encryption uses Seal. Two Move modules (channel and

relay), v4.2, live on testnet.

**LINKS**

Docs: [https://docs.patchway.xyz](https://docs.patchway.xyz)

Console: [https://console.patchway.xyz](https://console.patchway.xyz)

Repo: [https://github.com/abbey188/Patchway](https://github.com/abbey188/Patchway)

Packages: @patchway/sdk, patchway, patchway-mcp (on npm)

0x406359a9de74217248dd87cf4ca7ff2ffa74c24de0eb0d8f2a73b175e8fd1d02

About:

Azimuth is a decentralized satellite ground-station network. Low-cost stations capture live satellite imagery, prove every reception on Sui, and store every byte on Walrus - replacing $100k+ closed infrastructure with an open protocol that pays operators. On top, AI agents accumulate a tamper-proof environmental record on Walrus via MemWal.

**The problem.** Satellites broadcast weather and Earth imagery overhead every day and most of it is lost on the ground. Ground-station infrastructure is centralized, expensive ($100k+ to build, ~$22/min on cloud), and closed. A handful of corporations decide who receives satellite data, where it's stored, and who gets access. Research teams, universities, and startups are priced out. The barrier isn't technical it's economic. And a single dish only ever catches a fraction of a pass, so data is lost even where stations exist.

**The solution.** Azimuth is ground-station infrastructure as a protocol. Low-cost, independently-run stations capture real satellite downlinks and earn AZM for it, zero upfront cost, first data in minutes, open and portable instead of locked in. Every reception is anchored with cryptographic proof on Sui, and every byte is stored on Walrus with an on-chain availability certificate. Because the network merges captures from multiple stations, it recovers complete images that no single dish could, quality goes up as the network grows.

**How it works -**

- **Real hardware at the edge** — RTL-SDR/LoRa + Raspberry Pi nodes pull live imagery from the METEOR-M2-3 weather satellite. Not a simulation; capture real passes today.

- **Proof on Sui** - azimuth::orbital_vault (Move) records Proof-of-Availability (heartbeats) and Proof-of-Reception (Merkle roots, peer-verified) and pays operators in Coin<AZM>. On-chain, or it didn't happen.

- **Storage on Walrus** - every packet and merged image is a Walrus blob; Quilt batches small RF packets. Content-addressed and publicly re-verifiable (byte-identical across aggregators).

- **Privacy via Seal** - premium captures are threshold-encrypted with an on-chain access policy.

**Why agents are necessary.** A network of independent, untrusted stations can't be run by hand. Three decisions have to happen continuously and at scale: which passes each station should attempt (limited time, power, and antenna), how to split a single pass across stations so their captures merge into a complete image, and what each merged image actually shows. Azimuth runs a multi-agent system to make these decisions and because the network is decentralized, the agents' memory can't live in one operator's private database. It lives on Walrus via MemWal: persistent, shared, and verifiable, so every station works from the same tamper-evident record:

- Operator Agent (one per station) - recalls its own reception history from MemWal and learns, across sessions, which satellite passes are worth attempting. Long-term, verifiable agent memory.

- Coordinator Agent - reads every station's shared skill profile from MemWal and negotiates coverage (splits a pass AOS/LOS across stations) to maximize the combined image. Multi-agent coordination over shared memory.

- Analyst Agent - on each merged image (Sui ImageMerged event), runs Claude vision (cloud cover, anomalies, quality), writes a structured report to Walrus, and recalls prior reports to detect change over time. Artifact-driven workflows.

All agent memory is persistent, shareable, and tamper-evident, a station can't fake its own track record, and the environmental record outlives any operator. What makes it credible is the grounding: this memory comes from a real physical sensor network, not a toy chatbot.

**Sui Stack used -**

- **Sui** **(Move)**: azimuth::orbital_vault - staking, Proof-of-Availability + Proof-of-Reception, AZM rewards (Coin<AZM>), merged-image records; events drive coordination.

- **Walrus**: all packets + merged images stored as blobs with on-chain availability certificates; Quilt batches small RF packets.

- **MemWal** **(Walrus Memory):** verifiable, shareable agent memory layered on the data pipeline.

- **Seal**: threshold encryption + on-chain access control for premium data.

- **SuiNS**: human-readable station identities. Walrus Sites: decentralized frontend hosting.

About:

### Switchboard OS

Switchboard OS shows how Web3 can run quietly in the background of normal business operations.

### Demo Workflows

### 1. Invoice → Sui USDC Payment → CRM Update

Create an invoice inside Billing360, launch the payment surface, pay with Sui USDC, capture the transaction ID, and automatically update the invoice and CRM pipeline.

### 2. Business Record → Attestation → Verifiable Receipt

Generate a business record such as an invoice, backup, or document event, then attach a Sui transaction receipt so the action has a verifiable on-chain trail.

### 3. Storage360 → Walrus Backup → Sui Receipt

Trigger a backup from Storage360, create a backup record, connect it to decentralized storage, and display the Sui receipt history inside the business dashboard.

### 4. AI Sidecars → Business Workflow Assistance

Use module-level AI sidecars to help navigate CRM tasks, generate operational context, and keep workflows moving without leaving the business operating system.

### 5. Treasury360 → Wallet Balance → Payment Visibility

View the connected Sui wallet, live balances, payment records, and treasury activity after invoices and attestations flow through the system.

### What This Demonstrates

Switchboard OS is not a crypto app pretending to be business software. It is business software where blockchain receipts, wallet actions, payments, storage, and attestations happen in the background as part of the normal workflow.

### Switchboard OS

Switchboard OS is an AI-native business operating system that combines CRM, payments, automation, document management, storage, communications, and blockchain infrastructure into a single modular platform.

The project was built around a simple idea: businesses shouldn't need ten separate tools to manage customers, payments, workflows, documents, and digital assets. Switchboard provides a unified operating layer where these systems work together through a common architecture.

For the Sui ecosystem, Switchboard integrates blockchain-native payments, transaction attestations, wallet-aware workflows, and decentralized storage primitives. Payment events, invoices, receipts, and business records can be linked directly to on-chain activity, creating a verifiable operational history for businesses.

The platform includes modules for CRM, billing, document management, automation, communications, storage, treasury management, and AI-assisted workflows. Behind the scenes, a retrieval engine and orchestration layer allow data, documents, and operational events to be connected across the entire system.

Rather than focusing on a single feature, Switchboard is designed as a foundation for next-generation business software where AI, automation, and blockchain infrastructure work together as part of one operating environment.

Key capabilities include:

• Sui wallet integration
• USDC payment processing
• Transaction attestations and receipts
• Decentralized storage integration
• CRM and customer management
• AI-powered workflow automation
• Document and knowledge management
• Treasury and financial operations
• Cross-module business intelligence

Switchboard's goal is to bridge traditional business software with decentralized infrastructure, making blockchain functionality useful in everyday business operations rather than treating it as a separate system.

About:

### 🏔️ Yeti Lounge: The Sovereign, Privacy-First Creator Ecosystem

### **Short Description (For the gallery/preview card)**

A privacy-first, decentralized social platform custom-built for the Sui Overflow and LOFI meme community. Powered by the Sui and Walrus stack, Yeti Lounge features seamless Web2-style onboarding via zkLogin, gasless operations, on-chain AI image generation, automated token utility loops, and persistent AI agent memory powered by MemWal.

### **Long Description / Project Overview**

Web3 constantly preaches censorship resistance and individual sovereignty, yet the entire ecosystem ironically relies on centralized Web2 social media platforms where entire communities and founder accounts are wiped out overnight without warning.

**Yeti Lounge** completely disrupts this paradox by moving the social graph onto sovereign, immutable rails—without sacrificing the frictionless user experience that modern web users expect.

Built specifically for the Sui Overflow and the vibrant LOFI token ecosystem, Yeti Lounge bridges the gap between Web2 convenience and Web3 security. It transforms a passion-driven meme community into a high-utility, functional economic engine where creators actually own their digital footprint, identities, and media assets.

### **Key Technical Pillars & Features**

- **Invisible Web3 Onboarding (Sui zkLogin):** Users authenticate in seconds using standard Web2 OAuth providers (like Google). No seed phrases, no extension pop-ups, and 100% gasless interactions thanks to **Sponsored Transactions**.

- **Decentralized Media Layer (Walrus Protocol):** When a creator publishes a post or media, it bypasses traditional centralized cloud storage. Content is pushed directly to Walrus Protocol as raw, immutable media blobs, ensuring absolute permanence.

- **On-Chain Identity Grid (SuiNS):** Complex cryptographic wallet addresses are elegantly masked behind human-readable handles via native Sui Name Service resolution, establishing a true, decentralized social graph.

- **AI-Driven Compute Loop (LOFI Token Utility):** Yeti Lounge hosts a native AI image generation engine. Users spend or lock LOFI tokens as direct payment for AI compute, transforming a community meme asset into a high-velocity utility commodity.

- **Persistent AI Agent Memory (MemWal):** The platform features a native chat interface powered by an autonomous AI agent. By utilizing MemWal, the agent possesses persistent, decentralized semantic memory, allowing it to securely recall past user contextual interactions entirely on-chain.

- **Seamless Monetization & Wallet Rails:** Includes a streamlined wallet dashboard allowing users to seamlessly deposit native SUI tokens and instantly swap them into LOFI via a one-click internal exchange mechanism, keeping the tipping economy entirely fluid and native.

### **How We Built It (The Tech Stack)**

- **Frontend & Hosting:** Next.js / React, hosted on Vercel.

- **Core L1 Architecture:** Sui Network.

- **Identity & UX Primitives:** Sui zkLogin, Sponsored Transactions, SuiNS.

- **Decentralized Storage:** Walrus Protocol.

- **AI Memory Layer:** MemWal.

- **Economic Engine:** LOFI Token Integration.

About:

### **Trident;  Verifiable infrastructure for autonomous financial agents on Sui.**

Provably untampered data → autonomous on-chain execution → cryptographic trust scoring in one stack
.

Built for **Sui Overflow 2026** — Agentic Web · Walrus · DeepBook · DeFi & Payments

**Introduction**

- AI agents are about to start moving real money. Trading bots, payment agents, and autonomous portfolio managers are no longer a future concept — they're being deployed today, on data nobody can verify, executing through black-box APIs nobody can audit.

- **Trident** is the missing trust layer underneath that shift: a platform where autonomous agents trade only on cryptographically-verified data, execute through real on-chain liquidity instead of opaque APIs, and carry a public, auditable security score instead of a marketing promise.

- We are not building a chatbot wrapper. We are building **financial infrastructure for the agent economy** — and Sui is the only chain with the primitives to make it provably safe.

### **The Problem**

Three unsolved problems sit at the center of every "AI agent" narrative in crypto right now:

- **Data poisoning is invisible.** Agents make financial decisions based on price feeds, sentiment data, and off-chain signals that can be faked, stale, or manipulated — and there is no native way to prove a dataset wasn't tampered with before an agent acted on it.

- **Execution is a black box.** Most "agentic trading" today routes through centralized APIs or opaque bots. Users have no way to verify what actually happened on-chain versus what was claimed.

- **Trust is a marketing claim, not a fact.** Following the ~$223M Cetus exploit on Sui in 2025, the ecosystem's biggest open wound is security credibility. Projects say "audited" — almost none expose a live, verifiable security posture.

Sui's own ecosystem is explicitly trying to solve for this: positioning itself as the settlement layer for the agentic web, shipping a native stablecoin (USDsui), and funding a decentralized verifiable-data layer (Walrus) — but no product today stitches these three pieces (verified data, real execution, provable trust) into one coherent stack.

### **Our Solution**

Trident is a **web app + on-chain protocol** where:

- Every signal an agent trades on is stored on **Walrus** as a provenance-tracked blob — verified before use, not trusted blindly.

- Every agent is a **Sui object** — owning its own permissions, capital limits, and delegation rules natively, not through a database row an API key controls.

- Every trade executes through **DeepBook's real orderbook** — visible, on-chain, and auditable, not simulated or routed through a black box.

- Every agent carries a public **Security Score** — audit status, drawdown limits, and circuit-breaker thresholds, visible before a user deposits a single dollar.

- Settlement and fees flow through **USDsui**, Sui's native stablecoin rail.

Users deposit capital into a vault. An agent manages it. Every decision the agent makes is traceable back to a verified data source and a real, on-chain trade.

About:

- ***[Video Link in README.md]
.***

- **Business Pain Points**: Clinical health data is fragmented across clinics, leaving patients without access. Furthermore, sending audio consultations, certificates, and medication photos to the cloud for AI analysis raises severe HIPAA compliance and data leakage concerns.?
.

- **Solution**: AnamPouch provides a privacy-first, patient-sovereign digital medical pouch. A local client-side 3B-8B LLM processes records in-browser, encrypting data with a client-derived AES key before storing it on Walrus. Patients share records with doctors via time-locked, on-chain Seal grants, allowing instant web decrypts under sponsored gas.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **zkLogin & Browser Encryption**: Google OAuth sign-in derives non-custodial keys to run in-memory AES-GCM-256 encryption.

- **Client-Side AI Processing**: Transcribes audio, OCRs reports, and checks drug interactions locally.

- **Walrus Pouch Storage**: Encrypted files reside on Walrus, with records anchored to Sui transaction hashes.

- **On-Chain Access Grants**: Move-enforced time-locked grants allow doctors to view data with zero wallet/gas requirements.

About:

Uberwal turns your AI coding sessions into a verifiable record that you own and share on your terms. Instead of measuring work by token consumption or trusting unverifiable resume claims, it captures each AI coding session through an MCP integration, extracts skills backed by evidence quoted from the actual work, and stores everything on Walrus so the record persists across sessions, apps, and agents rather than vanishing when the session ends. Every fact carries an on-chain proof (a blob_id traceable on the Walrus Scanner) that anyone can check, and a dashboard lets you browse sessions by project, ask an assistant questions answered only from evidence-backed facts, and send recruiters or teammates a scoped, read-only view controlled by a manifest that you can revoke instantly. The result is proof of what a developer actually did, not claims taken on faith.

**What problem Uberwal solves (in the AI era):**

**AI work leaves no trace**
AI agents now write a quarter of production code, but that work lives in disposable sessions and vanishes the moment the chat closes.

**AI activity gets mistaken for value**
companies measure AI use by tokens burned (we called it token-maxxing), rewarding consumption instead of what the AI actually helped build.

**AI made claims unverifiable**
when resumes, references, and even interview answers can be AI-generated, no one can trust what a developer says they did.

**AI memory is trapped per tool**
each assistant remembers in its own silo, so context cannot follow you across sessions, tools, or agents.

Features :
**Captures real work**
turns each AI coding session into skills backed by evidence quoted straight from the work. Substance, not token counts.

**Makes it portable and owned**
stored on Walrus, so the record persists across sessions, apps, and agents. The work no longer vanishes.

**Makes it verifiable**
every fact carries an on-chain proof (blob_id on Walrus) anyone can check. Proof, not resume claims.

**Makes it shareable, scoped**

share a read-only, manifest-controlled view with a recruiter. They evaluate from verified work, instantly revokable.

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/7721e45c-51ce-494e-ae1f-7550cff932d6/1c571af9-b9ea-415d-9319-308921905c32.jpeg
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/7721e45c-51ce-494e-ae1f-7550cff932d6/80bc3caf-19b2-467e-ab4f-46b22d2118a4.jpeg
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/7721e45c-51ce-494e-ae1f-7550cff932d6/2d4e88c8-4d86-4343-bbd9-5643866b592e.jpeg

About:

[Quadra](https://quadra.sh) is an onchain marketplace for AI agents. People hire independent AI agents to do work like price forecasts, prediction market answers, or writing, and pay in QUADRA tokens on the Sui blockchain.

Every agent has an onchain score from its past results, so you can trust the good ones and avoid the bad ones. The whole flow, from finding an agent to paying it to reading the sealed result, happens in one chat.

The idea is simple. AI agents are getting good, but you cannot tell which one is reliable, and there is no fair way to pay them only when they deliver. Quadra fixes both. It scores agents on real past performance inside a tamper-proof enclave, holds your payment in escrow until the job is delivered, and seals every result so only you and the agent can read it.

Here is the workflow:

You open the dashboard and talk to the Quadra Assistant, which is powered by OpenAI. It reads the live list of agents and what each one can do, then recommends the best fit and ranks options by their onchain score.

When you pick one, your browser talks to that agent directly. The agent asks for the details it needs, quotes a price, and proposes a job. You pay with one click from your wallet.

The token goes into an onchain escrow, not to the agent yet. The agent then does the work, encrypts the answer, and stores it. When delivery is confirmed, the escrow releases payment to the agent minus a small fee. You reveal the result by signing with your wallet.

The system is built from small engines, each with one job.

The data layer is the central hub. It is the only thing that writes to storage, holds the keys, and serves fast reads. It keeps agent identities, scores, job templates, sealed results, and the list of where each live agent can be reached. The website and the other engines read from it. An indexer mirrors onchain events into a local database so reads are instant, and a separate watcher streams blockchain updates over native gRPC so nothing has to poll.

The intake engine handles money and trust between a user and an agent. The agent opens a job, the user pays into escrow onchain, and intake watches for that payment. Once paid, the agent delivers, intake checks the result is stored, then releases payment or refunds if the agent misses the deadline.

The evaluation engine is the trust core, and it runs on **Sui Nautilus**: Sui's framework for verifiable offchain compute inside a secure AWS Nitro enclave. It scores finance and prediction jobs against real ground truth, like live Pyth prices and Polymarket outcomes, that it fetches itself, so the caller cannot forge the answer. Because the code runs in a sealed enclave with a published measurement, it cannot be tampered with: the enclave signs every score with a key only it holds, and that signature is checked against the enclave's onchain identity before the score is ever recorded. So an honest score is not a promise, it is proven by hardware. The scheduler engine fires when a job's time window ends, asks the Nautilus engine to grade the result, verifies the signature, and records the new score. That score feeds back into the agent's reputation.

The competition engine runs contests. Agents enrol, get free jobs, and the engine records their scores or trading performance onchain, then pays out prizes to the winners. This gives new agents a way to prove themselves and earn.

The agents themselves are independent programs that anyone can run. An agent registers its wallet onchain, which becomes its identity, and publishes where it lives so the website can reach it. It chats with users, scopes jobs, produces results, and delivers them sealed. To stay coherent across conversations, each agent has its own memory layer called **MemWal**: when a session ends it condenses what happened into a checkpoint, encrypts it with Seal, and stores it on Walrus; when you come back, it recalls that checkpoint and feeds it back into the agent so it reasons against its own past context instead of starting blind, and uses that recalled history as a critic for what to do next. Some agents are scoreless, meaning work that cannot be graded fairly, like writing an article. They are paid on delivery, never scored, and cannot join competitions, so you trust them once before hiring again.

Two pieces make this safe and private. Payments use escrow, so an agent only gets paid for delivered work, and a bad agent gets refunded against. Results use **Seal** encryption tied to an onchain access policy, so only the paying user and the agent can decrypt an answer. Storage uses **Walrus**, a decentralized blob store, so nothing sits on a single server.

Put together, Quadra turns scattered AI agents into a trustworthy market, built on a focused stack: Sui for the chain, escrow, and reputation; Sui Nautilus enclaves for honest scoring; Walrus for decentralized storage; Seal for private results; and MemWal for agent memory. You describe what you need in plain language, an assistant routes you to a proven agent, you pay only when the work is delivered, the result is private, and every score that guides your choice was earned onchain, not claimed.

If you want to learn more about Quadra ecosystem, refer to our [documentation](https://docs.quadra.sh).

About:

***[Video Link in **[*README.md*](http://README.md)**]***

- **Business Pain Points**: Autonomous AI research agents typically suffer from statelessness or platform lock-in. Their memory databases are centralised under Web2 APIs, transient across sessions, and unverifiable, meaning operators cannot prove their knowledge base has not been poisoned.
.

- **Solution**: KelpChronicle uses Walrus as a persistent, verifiable memory layer and Sui as an integrity anchor. On each run, the agent pulls its memory delta, crawls ecosystem info, compiles findings onto Walrus, and signs the state hash onto Sui Move, making the agent's memory completely portable.
.

- **Key Features**:

- **Organic Memory delta**: Agent fetches memory logs from Walrus (MemWal) and appends new research nodes.

- **recall Move Attestation**: Anchors memory tree hashes on-chain to guarantee zero data poisoning.

- **State Portability**: No platform lock-in; backend states can be restored on any hardware.

- **Kelp Forest Graph**: Interactive React UI visualising agent memory nodes as an organic growing graph.

About:

**indelible.Blob, Proof of Reality in the Age of AI**

Generative AI has made convincing fake photos and videos free and instant, quietly corroding what we rely on every day: insurance claim photos, journalists' footage, courtroom evidence, marketplace listings, the face on a video call. It sharpens as AI agents begin acting on data for us, an agent fooled by a fake errs at machine speed and scale. The question underneath all of it: how do you prove something is actually real?

indelible.Blob is the capture origin proof primitive that answers it. A photo or video is captured on real hardware, a Solana Seeker, its exact bytes SHA-256 hashed and anchored on Sui, the file stored on Walrus as the canonical, permanent copy. It is the inverse of deepfake detection: not guessing whether an image is fake, but proving whether a specific capture is real, unaltered, and hardware rooted, or carries no proof at all. Ground truth, for people and for the agents acting on their behalf.

Live and demoable today: hardware rooted capture on a real Seeker in two modes, public, and sovereign (Seal encrypted) by default; the exact bytes notarized on Sui and stored on Walrus; and fully client side verification, a public portal that reads Sui and Walrus directly and re-hashes in your browser, with no indelible server in the trust path, rebuilding a public capture's original image from the Walrus blob in front of you. Anyone verifies any capture, trusting only Sui, Walrus, and the math. Sovereign captures prove integrity while their content stays sealed.

Why Walrus is core: Walrus is the permanent, decentralized home of the verified bytes, the verifiable data layer the proof rests on, and Seal makes private verification possible. As the web turns agentic, this is the missing primitive: agents need to know which real world data is real before acting, and indelible is that verifiable ground truth layer on Walrus.

Honest by design: we prove origin and integrity only, that real hardware captured these exact bytes, unaltered. Not scene truth, not freedom from coercion. An AI image yields no record; absence of proof means unknown, never "fake." Today the hardware grade is self reported by the capture client, and we say so.

This is not a weekend demo; it is infrastructure meant to help secure information networks for centuries, engineered from a cryptographic stance and documented as such. The repository's docs/ carry the full design, and we invite the judges to read them: a Design Hardening specification of security and anti surveillance invariants (crypto agility and post quantum readiness, identity unlinkability, and a fail closed verdict ladder where the cryptographic "Verified" is reserved for byte exact proof and a fake can never forge it); a Privacy and Interoperability Roadmap (programmable Seal access control, metadata minimization, C2PA alignment rather than a forked standard); Research Frontiers (zero knowledge and consented blind verification, provable longitudinal survival); honest threat model Use Case Simulations; an architectural Decision Log; and a Mainnet Transition plan. The long term engineering is not an afterthought, it is the foundation.

The values, made concrete: decentralization (no company in the verification path)

, privacy by default (sovereign capture; never any biometric data), anti surveillance (verify authenticity, never identity; security by unforgeability, not by tracking people), and interoperability over silos.

indelible.Blob is trust infrastructure for reality, owned by no single company, honest about its limits, and built to endure. The capture origin proof layer the coming century of media, commerce, evidence, and autonomous systems will need to tell what is real.

About:

GhostPay is a production-grade web app where every user gets an autonomous AI agent with its own Sui wallet.

The agent receives USDC, pays globally, swaps via DeepBook, and remembers everything privately on Walrus. Private by default, gasless for users, compliant by design.

### **The Problem**

- **Remittances are broken.** A $100 transfer to Nigeria costs $35 in fees. Legacy rails are slow and expensive.

- **Public ledgers kill adoption.** Users do not want bank accounts that look like Twitter. Privacy is mandatory for payments.

- **AI agents cannot act economically.** Agents today cannot own assets, pay gas, store memory, or resolve disputes.

- **Sui infra is underused.** Walrus launched for large verifiable data, DeepBook processes billions in volume, but few apps make them essential.

- **Trust after outages.** Sui needs live, real-world apps that prove reliability for enterprises.

### **The Solution: GhostPay**

GhostPay spawns a Move object that IS your agent:

- Sign in with Google → zkLogin creates agent wallet (no seed phrase)

- Receive USDC → agent auto-converts via DeepBook → payout in local currency

- Every payslip, KYC, and agent decision encrypted on Walrus, hash anchored on Sui

- Private by default, shareable via view-key for compliance

- Gasless UX via sponsored transactions

One product solves payments, privacy, agents, storage, and liquidity.

### **Uniqueness — Why No One Has Built This**

- **Agent = Object:** Not a bot calling a wallet. The agent IS a Sui object with owned assets, parallel execution.

- **Memory Layer:** Walrus is not optional storage. It is the agent's encrypted brain for chargebacks and audits.

- **Private by Default:** Implements Mysten's 2026 vision today using SEAL + Walrus, with selective disclosure.

- **Zero-Fee UX:** DeepBook margin vault yields subsidize user fees. We turn liquidity into a business model.

- **Post-Quantum Ready:** Optional testnet PQ signatures for forward compliance.

- **University Native:** Built by students for campus gig payroll — real users on day one.

### **Why This Fits Sui Overflow 2026**

**Agentic Web:** Autonomous agents that transact, coordinate, and remember using Sui primitives.

**DeFi & Payments:** Programmable, private, zero-fee stablecoin rails for real-world remittance.

**Walrus Track:** Core innovation is Walrus as verifiable data and memory layer for agents. Without Walrus, no chargeback, no compliance.

**DeepBook Track:** Uses DeepBook CLOB as foundational liquidity — FX routing, vault yields, live spreads .

### **Why This Wins**

- **Solves founder-stated problems:** Abiodun's $1T stablecoin volume, $35 fee example, agent chargeback need, private payments roadmap.

- **Makes Sui infra essential:** Walrus and DeepBook are not add-ons, they are the product.

- **Production ready:** Web app, not prototype. Live on testnet, mainnet-ready with audit credits.

- **University advantage:** 100% student team, qualifies for $2,500 University Award (10 winners).

- **Post-hackathon fundable:** Clear SaaS model, needs OpenZeppelin/OtterSec audit — exactly what the $250k+ package offers.

- **Defensible moat:** Agent memory + privacy + liquidity flywheel compounds with each user.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/73654502-367c-402a-a6a8-d45804ffc783/60b554b3-df46-47ca-b1e6-36c2ae06d32c.png

About:

### **Problem**

AI agents are **stateless and fragmented**: memory is tied to one app/model/device, lost across sessions, and

impossible to share or verify. As agents become long-running and autonomous, they need a durable, portable,

trustworthy data foundation.

**Solution**

**Thiny** is a **microkernel + hexagonal** agent runtime where every capability is a plugin and storage is a swappable port:

1. **Walrus as the memory layer** — transcripts, audit trails, and artifacts are stored as content-addressed Walrus blobs (verifiable, portable, not platform-locked).

2. **On-chain memory pointer (Move)** — a `memory_head` object holds the latest blob IDs on Sui, so any agent/tool can discover and verify the canonical memory.

3. **MemWal (Walrus Memory)** — semantic recall of facts across sessions.

4. **Deterministic safety** — the LLM proposes; policy / approval / budget / audit middleware enforce. Every model + tool call is logged to an immutable Walrus audit trail.

5. **Bring-your-own everything** — pluggable models (OpenAI / Anthropic / Ollama), tools, and heads (CLI, HTTP, daemon, autonomous runner).

- Multiple gateway

**Why Walrus + Sui (essential)**

- **Walrus** gives cheap, durable, content-addressed storage for unbounded agent memory + artifacts — portable across agents, verifiable by blob ID.

- **Sui Move** anchors the *pointer* to that memory on-chain `memory_head`), making the latest state discoverable, owner-controlled, and tamper-evident.

About:

**We made an AI trustee with decades-long memory, deployed on Sui.**

***Problem:***

Private trusts still run on paper, phone calls, and a human fiduciary that bills tens of thousands a year for decades. The wiring between grantor and beneficiary hasn't moved on chain.

*What we built:*

An AI trustee that lives entirely on Sui. Five Move modules holding the assets, an LLM that turns plain English into typed rules, a dead-man's switch for succession, and a memory layer on Walrus that lets the agent recall every past decision before making the next one.

***How it works?***

Describe a trust in one paragraph of English, like "100 SUI monthly to my daughter while she studies; full inheritance at 30", and an LLM compiles it into Move-typed rules, beneficiaries, and a dead-man's switch. A smart contract holds the assets and executes for as long as the trust lives. You keep a 48-hour veto on every AI action.

The trustee never forgets. Every decision is Seal-encrypted, stored on Walrus, and recalled across cycles via MemWal, so by year five the agent has 60+ prior decisions to weigh before proposing the next one. That is the moat: institutional memory that compounds for decades.

*What's live:*

Five Move modules, thirty-three tests passing, package immutable on Sui testnet. Documents encrypted with Seal, decisions stored on Walrus, memory recalled through MemWal. Full dApp, landing, and pitch deck hosted on Walrus Sites. Create a trust from one paragraph, fund it, watch the agent remember.

About:

Cortex is an elastic brain for AI agents . Think Supermemory but elastic, sovereign and owned by the user on the Sui Stack.

It gives you memory that does not disappear when the chat ends, the model changes, or the tool changes. Instead of starting over every time, Cortex keeps context alive, useful, and connected across your workflows.

**What makes Cortex special:**

- Elastic brain: Cortex keeps growing, compressing, recalling, and reorganizing context over time so your AI system gets smarter from continuity, not just from one prompt

- Memory sharing: you can share memory between people and agents with precise access control, so collaboration happens on the same context without exposing everything

- Sovereign memory: your memory is not trapped inside one app, one model, or one interface

- Prompt generation: Cortex can turn stored memory into better prompts, grounded context, and reusable workflows

- Agent coordination: multiple agents can work from the same memory layer instead of acting like isolated bots

- Dreams and loops: Cortex is built for long-running reasoning, reflection, and background memory work, not just one-off chat

**Why the Sui stack matters:**

- Sui gives Cortex fast, programmable ownership and identity-native coordination

- Walrus gives durable blob storage for files, media, and memory artifacts

- Seal gives programmable encryption and access control for private memory

- MemWal helps make memory retrieval, sync, and long-term context management practical

- SuiNS makes identity and sharing more human-readable.

**In simple terms:**

- Sui is the coordination layer

- Walrus is the storage layer

- Seal is the privacy layer

- SuiNS for memory sharing identifier

- Cortex turns all of that into a usable memory system for AI

**So the short version is:**

- Cortex is a sovereign elastic brain built on the Sui stack

- It stores memory durably

- Shares it securely

- Recalls it intelligently

- and lets people and agents work on top of the same living context

About:

Mars is a data ownership protocol for the gig economy. It lets delivery riders, merchants, and consumers own the data they generate — and earn revenue when AI companies license that data.

Built on Sui + Walrus + Seal, Mars treats personal activity data as a financial asset. Every delivery trip, every order accepted, every purchase made becomes a contributor-owned `DataAsset` on-chain. AI companies buy access through `DataLicense` NFTs. Contributors earn USDC every time their data is used.

The current implementation runs end-to-end on Sui testnet with 640 simulated contributors (100 riders, 40 merchants, 500 consumers) generating real on-chain objects, real Walrus blobs, and real Seal-gated decryption.

About:

**ChronicleOS** is an open-source middleware infrastructure layer and companion developer tool ecosystem designed to provide persistent, portable, and cryptographically verifiable memory management for autonomous AI agents. Built directly on top of the **Walrus** decentralized storage protocol and the specialized **Walrus Memory (MemWal)** network architecture, ChronicleOS bridges production-ready AI orchestration frameworks like LangChain, LangGraph, and the Model Context Protocol (MCP) with decentralized Web3 infrastructure.

The primary architectural goal of the project is to solve the critical "memory bottlenecks" of modern agentic systems: context volatility, platform lock-in, and the lack of reliable execution audit trails. In traditional environments, an agent's context window expires when a container restarts, or its operational memory remains trapped within centralized vendor silos. ChronicleOS reconfigures AI memory as a decentralized asset owned directly by the user or developer. By routing state updates through MemWal and compiling massive file structures to Walrus blobs, agents can preserve exact continuity across different apps, service providers, or runtime boundaries weeks or months apart.

`  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                 APPLICATION / AGENTS FRAMEWORK          │
  │      (LangGraph/CrewAI / Multi-Agent Collaboration)     │
  └────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                               │ (Intercepts thought loops / uploads)
  ┌────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────┐
  │               CHRONICLEOS MIDDLEWARE ADAPTER            │
  │       (MemWal Adapter Class & Walrus Blobs Gateway)     │
  └───────────────┬─────────────────────────┬───────────────┘
                  │                         │
                  │ (Vector Search Logs)    │ (Raw Blobs / PDFs / CIDs)
                  v                         v
  ┌─────────────────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────────┐
  │          🧠 MEMWAL              │ │      🌊 WALRUS      │
  │     (Verifiable AI Memory)      │ │  (Decentralized DB) │
  └─────────────────────────────────┘ └─────────────────────┘
`The technical implementation inside the repository is split into three foundational tiers:

### 1. The Core Infrastructure Middleware (`packages/`)

This is a reusable software package containing custom adapters for popular LLM framework history abstractions (such as LangChain's `BaseChatMessageHistory`). The middleware intercepts raw text logs, system states, and agent reasoning vectors dynamically. It coordinates with the local MemWal relayer via signed cryptographic requests using an Ed25519 developer key, handling automatic vector generation, payload encryption, and state verification entirely behind the scenes.

### 2. Multi-Agent Reference Architecture (`apps/agents/`)

To prove the viability of the infrastructure, the project deploys a collaborative, long-running 3-agent Research & Development pipeline structured inside a LangGraph state machine:

- **The Researcher:** Harvests data streams and commits raw Markdown notes to Walrus, updating its active working context inside MemWal.

- **The Architect:** Reads the researcher's output CIDs from the Walrus gateway, synthesizes the inputs into polished code bases or data analysis artifacts, and publishes the certified files back to Walrus.

- **The Auditor:** Extracts the compiled files via their Walrus CIDs, evaluates performance metrics against original constraints, and leaves cryptographically validated feedback in the shared memory space. If a runtime container fails mid-execution, the network-resilient graph loads the historical ledger states from MemWal, handles state compaction, and resumes execution seamlessly.

### 3. The Developer Observability Dashboard (`apps/dashboard/`)

The interface layer is a modern Next.js/TypeScript application serving as a visual "Time Machine" for developers. Operating directly via decentralized gateways, the frontend extracts memory namespaces tied to explicit Sui package IDs. It generates an interactive **Memory Timeline Engine**, mapping out the semantic evolution of an agent's reasoning matrix block-by-block.

Developers can filter logs by agent name or timestamps to review exactly what a machine was "thinking" at any stage of a multi-day cycle. Adjacent to the timeline, an inline **Artifact Preview Window** renders versioned code edits, JSON trees, and Markdown files instantly. ChronicleOS converts complex, opaque backend agent behaviors into completely auditable, visual, and open-source operations ready for scale.

`# ChronicleOS Architecture

## System Overview

ChronicleOS is a decentralized multi-agent R&D lab with three core layers:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│       Developer Dashboard (Next.js)                 │
│  Memory Timeline | Artifact Explorer | Agent Monitor│
└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                       │
┌──────────────────────▼──────────────────────────────┐
│         Storage & Memory Infrastructure              │
│  Walrus (Files) + MemWal (Verifiable Memory)        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                       ▲
                       │
     ┌─────────────────┼─────────────────┐
     │                 │                 │
┌────▼────────┐ ┌─────▼──────┐ ┌───────▼────────┐
│ Researcher  │ │ Architect  │ │ Auditor/Review │
│   Agent     │ │   Agent    │ │     Agent      │
└─────────────┘ └────────────┘ └────────────────┘
     (LangGraph-based)
```

## Agent Workflow

### 1. Researcher Agent
**Role**: Data gathering and state tracking

- Scours external sources (web, APIs, databases)
- Saves raw datasets and markdown notes to **Walrus**
- Tracks research state in **MemWal** (never duplicates work, even after restart)
- Produces: Research findings + CID references

### 2. Architect Agent
**Role**: Synthesis and artifact creation

- Reads research notes from Walrus CIDs
- Uses LLM to synthesize information
- Generates high-value artifacts (reports, code, datasets)
- Saves artifacts back to Walrus
- Produces: Polished artifacts + decision logs

### 3. Auditor Agent
**Role**: Quality assurance and verification

- Pulls final artifacts from Walrus
- Evaluates against original task
- Reads shared **MemWal** context to understand architect decisions
- Logs detailed feedback
- Produces: Quality score + approval/rejection with reasons

## Data Flow

```
Task → Researcher → (Walrus CIDs)
                 ↓
                MemWal (state tracking)
                 ↓
        Architect → (Walrus CIDs)
                 ↓
                MemWal (decisions)
                 ↓
        Auditor → (Quality Score)
                 ↓
              Dashboard
```

## Storage Architecture

### Walrus (Decentralized File Storage)
- **What**: Immutable blob storage on Sui blockchain
- **Use**: Store raw data, documents, artifacts
- **Access**: Via Content IDs (CIDs)
- **Cost**: Gas fees (paid in Sui tokens)

### MemWal (Verifiable Agent Memory)
- **What**: Cryptographic key-value store for agent state
- **Use**: Track agent reasoning, decisions, progress
- **Access**: Via session ID + memory keys
- **Feature**: Cryptographic proofs for auditability

## Implementation Phases

### Phase 1 ✅ Scaffold (COMPLETE)
- [x] Monorepo structure
- [x] Agent framework (LangGraph placeholder)
- [x] Dashboard skeleton (Next.js)
- [x] MemWal adapter SDK

### Phase 2 ⏳ Integration
- [ ] Walrus SDK integration (upload/download files)
- [ ] MemWal API client (real endpoint)
- [ ] Agent tools (search, fetch, validation)
- [ ] End-to-end workflow testing

### Phase 3 ⏳ UI/UX
- [ ] Memory timeline visualization
- [ ] Artifact browser with previews
- [ ] Real-time agent status monitoring
- [ ] Execution history replay

### Phase 4 ⏳ Production
- [ ] Comprehensive documentation
- [ ] Example notebooks + guides
- [ ] Performance optimization
- [ ] PyPI package release

## Key Design Decisions

1. **Python for Agents** - Superior AI framework support (LangChain, LangGraph)
2. **LangGraph for Workflows** - Excellent for multi-agent state machines
3. **MemWal for Memory** - Native support for long-running agent persistence
4. **Walrus for Storage** - Decentralized, immutable, blockchain-backed
5. **Next.js for Dashboard** - Modern, fast, great for real-time updates
6. **Monorepo with Turbo** - Unified tooling, parallel builds

## Shared Memory Protocol

### Directory Structure on Walrus

```
/{session_id}/
├── artifacts/
│   ├── research_findings.md
│   ├── synthesis_report.pdf
│   └── generated_code.ts
├── logs/
│   ├── researcher.log
│   ├── architect.log
│   └── auditor.log
└── metadata.json
```

### MemWal Key Naming Convention

```
chat_history:{session_id}:{agent_id}
state:{session_id}:{agent_name}
workspace:{session_id}:artifact:{type}_{agent}
timeline:{session_id}
```

## Security Model

- **Non-custodial**: Agents don't hold user funds (only stored in Walrus/MemWal)
- **Immutable**: Walrus files cannot be changed, only new versions created
- **Verifiable**: MemWal proofs allow third-party audit of agent decisions
- **Private**: Agents can use private MemWal instances; no public data leakage required

## Performance Characteristics

| Component | Latency | Throughput | Notes |
|-----------|---------|-----------|-------|
| Researcher Agent | 30-300s | 1 task/agent | Depends on API rate limits |
| Architect Agent | 5-60s | 10 artifacts/min | LLM inference time |
| Auditor Agent | 10-30s | 10 reviews/min | LLM evaluation |
| Walrus Upload | 100-500ms | 10 MB/s | Network dependent |
| Walrus Download | 50-200ms | 50 MB/s | Parallel streams |
| MemWal Write | 10-50ms | 1000/sec | Local endpoint |
| MemWal Read | 5-20ms | 5000/sec | Local endpoint |

## Extending the Framework

### Adding a New Agent

```python
# apps/agents/src/agents/custom_agent.py
from src.config import ChronicleConfig
from src.models.types import ResearchResult

class CustomAgent:
    def __init__(self, config: ChronicleConfig):
        self.config = config
        self.name = "Custom"

    async def process(self, input_data) -> output_data:
        # Implement custom logic
        pass
```

### Adding a New Integration

```python
# packages/memwal-adapter/src/memwal_adapter/integrations/my_framework.py
from memwal_adapter.core.client import MemWalClient

class MyFrameworkMemory:
    def __init__(self, endpoint, **kwargs):
        self.client = MemWalClient(endpoint)

    async def save(self, key, data):
        await self.client.save_memory(key, data)
```

About:

**HiveMind — verifiable, portable AI memory for group chats
**

Turn ephemeral Telegram group chats into a verifiable, portable AI memory — owned by the group, readable by any AI.

Demo video: [https://youtu.be/td8tu_3lN1w](https://youtu.be/td8tu_3lN1w)
Try it live: [https://t.me/Sui_hivemind_bot](https://t.me/Sui_hivemind_bot)
Code: [https://github.com/xaviersharwin10/hivemind](https://github.com/xaviersharwin10/hivemind)

**THE PROBLEM**

 Group chats are where teams actually make decisions — the ship date, the tech stack, the file everyone approved — and where those decisions are instantly lost. When you later want an AI (Claude, Cursor) to act on what the group decided, someone ends up re-pasting hundreds of messages and re-uploading files into every new session. There's no proof of which file was approved, no portability, and no ownership.

**WHAT HIVEMIND DOES**

You add the HiveMind bot to your existing Telegram group. It quietly captures the group's decisions and shared files into a group-owned, verifiable memory — and any AI can then recall it, without anyone copy-pasting a thing.

Files are Seal-encrypted and stored on Walrus. Decisions become searchable MemWal (Walrus Memory) semantic memory. Every file is hash-anchored (SHA-256) in our own hivemind::registry Move contract on Sui. Onboarding uses zkLogin + Enoki, so the creator owns the keys, with no wallet and no gas.

**TWO WAYS FOR AI TO READ IT**

Path A — local, self-custody. Claude Desktop / Cursor connect to a local MCP server. The delegate key and all decryption stay on your own machine.

Path B — zero-install ([claude.ai](http://claude.ai)). A custom connector forwards recall to a confidential AWS Nitro (Nautilus) enclave that holds the key, decrypts inside the enclave, and signs every result. The result is verified against the enclave's signing key attested on-chain before it's returned — so even the operator, host, or root cannot read your group's memory.

**DEPLOYED ON SUI TESTNET**

hivemind::registry (our Move package — group index + artifact manifest): 0xe9a1e57c815cb1f2bd8c54d1c5973b0f9c565e5c3fbacffae8d47c7052896d8e Registry shared object (chat to group to MemWal account + SHA-256 manifest): 0xb058138ce5a2fd3542c25e7ce2e58e9e6848aa747cee996182b43cfb8b347daa app::hivemind (Nautilus enclave registration + recall verifier): 0xa583af305a9edd07dbb3b67bceda94ee97a4b225f48c8e06a9a07d9ab5ef702b Enclave<HIVEMIND> live attested enclave: 0xe09f6a82625f661ff53311068558b0c0d96d74790417407e52ae92bd94975668 Explorer: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xe9a1e57c815cb1f2bd8c54d1c5973b0f9c565e5c3fbacffae8d47c7052896d8e](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xe9a1e57c815cb1f2bd8c54d1c5973b0f9c565e5c3fbacffae8d47c7052896d8e)

**TECH STACK**

Sui Move, Walrus, MemWal (Walrus Memory), Seal, Nautilus (TEE / AWS Nitro), zkLogin, Enoki, Model Context Protocol (MCP), Rust, TypeScript, Telegraf, React/Vite.

About:

AI benchmark scores are unverifiable. Test sets leak into training data, so models memorize answers instead of reasoning — and labs grade their own homework, reporting the best of 50 runs. Both

  lies are invisible after the fact.

  SealedBench closes both with cryptography anyone can check:

  • Seal before it exists — a held-out test set is encrypted with Seal, stored on Walrus, and its SHA-256 + cutoff timestamp are notarized on Sui before a model's training cutoff. Anyone re-hashes

  the ciphertext in their browser and proves the set predates the model. No contamination.

  • Score in a sealed box — Seal releases the decryption key only to an attested Nautilus TEE enclave, which decrypts in-memory, scores the model, archives the full run trace to Walrus, and posts

  a signed AttestedScore on-chain. No cherry-picking, no swapped weights, no human in the loop. Honest scoring.

  The result is a leaderboard where every score is independently verifiable against Sui and Walrus. Live on testnet today with a real attested run (oss/smollm2, 27/50) — verify the seal, the

  trace, and the enclave signature yourself.

About:

Sui-native AI agents that own their identity, memory, and money — within limits you set.

suigent is a harness for building autonomous agents on the Sui stack.

A **suigent **agent is a **verifiable, portable blob** with its own on-chain identity, encrypted long-term memory, policy-gated wallet, and a code-first harness for tools and workflows. Not a chatbot wrapper. Not a prompt in a black box.

---

## What it is

@suigent/* is the Sui-native agent harness built on top of Flue:

- **Flue:** Agent runtime — sessions, sandbox, model calls, workflows, tools

- suigent: Sui stack — identity registry, Walrus memory, agent blobs, custom ready-made @suigent/tools for agents

The mental model:

> Agent = Identity (Sui object/key) + State (Memory on MemWal + Walrus) + Harness & @suigent/tools

You author agents as a normal TypeScript project — agents/, workflows/, tools/, subagents/ — and suigent makes the Sui parts native: keys, memory, money, and coordination are first-class, not bolted on.

---

## The problem

Today's AI agents are opaque and fragile:

- **No real identity**: Shared API keys, shared bot accounts. You can't prove *which* agent did *what*.

- **Amnesia by default**: Context windows reset. "Memory" is a vector DB with no chain of custody.

- **Money is scary or impossible**: Either the agent can't transact, or it has unrestricted access to a hot wallet.

- **No portability**: Agent configs live in someone's SaaS dashboard. Forking, auditing, or moving them is painful.

Blockchains solved verifiable identity and value movement for humans. suigent brings that same substrate to **autonomous software**.

---

## The idea

### An agent is three things

1. **Identity** — a Sui key/object. The agent signs, attests, and is addressable. Distinct from every other agent. Verifiable. Portable.

2. **State** — memory on Walrus (optionally Seal-encrypted). Working memory and durable lessons persist across restarts. Only the agent reads its sealed archive.

3. **Harness** — a Flue app with tools and workflows. Behaviors are code, not hidden prompts. Policy caps what the agent can do with money; humans approve what crosses the line.

suigent wires all three together so they compose cleanly — identity pins state, state survives restarts, policy gates value, messaging coordinates humans and other agents.

---

## How it works

### Identity — every agent has its own on-chain address

Each agent gets its own Sui keypair and on-chain object. It signs transactions, attests to actions, and can be discovered via an agent card + registry. No shared bot account.

```bash

suigent up          *# mint keys + write agent cards*

suigent inspect     *# chain-proven facts vs handler-asserted ones*

```

### Memory — agents that remember, privately

Two-tier memory backed by Walrus:

- **Working set** — always in context [core.md](http://core.md), recent lessons)

- **Archive** — searchable long-term store (MemWal), fetched on demand

At rest, memory is **Seal-encrypted** and content-addressed. Restart-proof. The agent learns over time without leaking secrets to the model provider or the host.

### Value + safety — money within limits

Agents transact from **their own wallet** — transfer, swap, DeFi — but only within policy you define:

```ts

suigent: {

  wallet: 'self',

  policy: {

    maxPerAction: 2_000_000_000,        *// hard cap per action (MIST)*

    requireApprovalAbove: 500_000_000, *// human must sign above this*

  },

}

```

Anything over the line triggers **verifiable human-in-the-loop**: the owner's ed25519 approval is checked on chain inside authorize_sign. The agent cannot bypass its policy to move value.

### Coordination — agents that talk

End-to-end-encrypted **Sui Stack Messaging** channels for:

- **agent ↔ agent** — delegate tasks, share signals, coordinate swarms

- **agent ↔ human** — approval flows, status updates, interactive oversight

All on chain. Encrypted payloads. No side-channel DMs required for trust.

### Distribution — publish an agent, clone it anywhere

```bash

suigent push                    *# pack → signed Walrus capsule, pinned by on-chain object*

suigent clone <objectId>        *# verify signature + unpack*

```

Your agent is a **content-addressed, tamper-evident capsule** — portable across machines, forkable, auditable. The directory is how you author it; the Walrus blob + Sui object is what you own and ship.

---

## Why Sui

Sui is a natural home for autonomous agents:

- **Objects + PTBs** — agent identity, policy objects, spend receipts

- **Walrus** — durable, content-addressed agent state (memory, code capsule)

- **Seal** — encrypted memory at rest; only the agent decrypts

- **Sui Stack Messaging** — E2E agent↔agent and agent↔human coordination

- **DeepBook / DeFi** — policy-gated reads and writes via @suigent/tools

Fast finality, cheap storage, programmable objects — the stack maps cleanly onto "identity + state + behavior" without forcing agents into ERC-721-shaped boxes.

---

## Who it's for

- **Builders** shipping Sui-native agents — trading desks, concierge bots, on-chain analysts, game NPCs with wallets, DAO operators.

- **Teams** who need *auditability* — inspect what the agent claims vs what the chain proves.

- **Developers** who already like Flue/EVE-style code-first agents and want Sui identity, memory, and money without reimplementing the spine.

- **Researchers** exploring multi-agent coordination, HITL policy, and verifiable autonomy on a live testnet.

---

## What you can build

Examples in the repo today:

- **suigent-showcase**: Full tour — identity, memory, tools, workflows, subagents

- **defi-desk**: Policy-gated trading — oracle reads, swaps, approval flows

- **messaging-mesh-with-ui**: Multi-agent mesh over Sui Messaging + a live UI

- **agent-swarm**: Coordinated agents with distinct identities and roles

Explore examples live here: [https://suigent.vercel.app/examples](https://suigent.vercel.app/examples)

Identity, memory, money, and tools — declared in code. Workflows orchestrate a turn; @suigent/tools activate the Sui stack.

---

## Developer experience

```bash

npm i -g @suigent/cli

suigent init

suigent up && suigent run assistant "ship it"

```

Developer docs: [https://suigent.vercel.app/docs](https://suigent.vercel.app/docs)

---

## Spin up your agents now

suigent is early and live on testnet. We're looking for:

- **Developers** to build agents and stress-test identity, memory, and policy flows

- **Design partners** in DeFi, gaming, and agent coordination who need verifiable autonomy

- **Feedback** on the agent spec, tool surface, and registry/discovery model

---

About:

Trustline turns one-time payments into intelligent financial relationships powered by **programmable money on Sui**, **verifiable storage on Walrus**, and **auditable AI assistance**.

Today, digital payments are disconnected events. Once money moves, the relationship usually disappears. Freelancers struggle to prove reliability, businesses lose context around completed work, and financial reputation remains trapped inside centralized platforms.

**Trustline reimagines payments as persistent relationships.**

Every payment agreement becomes a **programmable Sui object** containing participants, milestones, payment amounts, release policies, verification requirements, dispute state, and completion records. Instead of simply sending funds, users create **relationship-based payment flows** where money can be locked, reviewed, released, disputed, or refunded according to explicit rules.

Trustline supports multiple milestone types: **manual payer approval**, **deliverable-based verification**, **time-gated release**, and **opt-in auto-release after a challenge window**. By default, AI verification can confirm submitted proof, but payment release still requires payer or authorized operator approval unless automation is explicitly enabled.

Using **Walrus**, Trustline stores deliverable evidence and factual relationship memory so payment history remains durable and auditable. Creation events, submissions, verification outcomes, releases, disputes, attestations, and reputation updates are indexed into a clear relationship timeline. **MemWal** can optionally enrich this memory with semantic recall for AI-assisted insights, but the factual audit trail does not depend on AI.

**AI agents act as payment intelligence assistants.** They run pre-flight anomaly analysis, verify deliverables stored on Walrus, flag unusual behavior, and generate transparent relationship insights. AI decisions are structured, auditable, and recorded with model, prompt version, confidence, reasoning, cost, and execution metadata.

Trustline also introduces **portable reputation** through on-chain completion attestations and reputation proofs. Completed milestones mint verifiable records for recipients, and users can generate factual reputation summaries from their completed payment history without exposing every private detail of each relationship.

**Built on Sui, Trustline uses:**

- **Sui Move shared objects** for programmable payment relationships

- **Programmable Transaction Blocks** for atomic funding, submission, verification, release, dispute, and attestation workflows

- **Walrus** for durable deliverable evidence and factual memory storage

- **Optional MemWal** semantic memory for AI-assisted relationship recall

- **Scoped agent capabilities** for verifier and automation actions

- **Completion attestations and reputation proofs** for portable trust

A freelancer can receive an assigned relationship, upload proof to Walrus, have AI verify the deliverable, wait for payer approval or configured auto-release, receive payment on Sui, and build a reusable reputation record from successful completed milestones.

**Trustline transforms money from something that simply moves into something that remembers.**

About:

Sivraj is a private, encrypted personal intelligence layer that builds a user-owned AI Twin from someone’s documents, conversations, work history, goals, preferences, and memories.

The project solves the problem of fragmented AI context: today, every AI tool learns a different, incomplete version of the user, and that knowledge does not carry across apps, models, or agents. Sivraj creates a persistent memory and identity layer that survives across tools. Users can upload or connect sources such as PDFs, chats, GitHub repos, voice notes, notes, email, and documents; Sivraj processes them into a secure cognitive graph and lets approved AI systems retrieve the right context with permission.

The system is designed around sovereignty and trust. Private memory is encrypted before durable storage, with access governed by explicit permissions and identity controls. The goal is to give builders, founders, consultants, researchers, and creators a personal AI Twin that remembers their past work, helps recover forgotten context, identifies patterns, and compounds their intelligence over time.

About:

**zing-cdn** is a core infrastructure component of the broader **Zing** ecosystem. Zing functions as a decentralized knowledge layer — a sovereign platform for storing, organizing, and retrieving information. zing-cdn provides the high-performance P2P content delivery network that powers it, replacing traditional CDNs like Cloudflare and AWS CloudFront with a cryptoeconomically incentivized peer-to-peer mesh. Together, they form a complete decentralization stack: sovereign knowledge hosted on Walrus, delivered at wire speed via zing-cdn's edge network.

zing-cdn is a high-performance, decentralized Content Delivery Network (dCDN) built natively on top of the Walrus storage protocol. While Walrus provides permanent, highly resilient data storage using two-dimensional erasure coding (Red Stuff), retrieving that data directly from core storage nodes introduces computational overhead and multi-node network latency. zing-cdn solves this by introducing a lightning-fast, peer-to-peer edge caching layer.

Built in Rust, zing-cdn allows node operators to aggregate, cache, and redistribute fully reconstructed Walrus blobs directly to end users over high-speed libp2p QUIC connections. By decoupling data availability (handled by Walrus) from data delivery (handled by zing-cdn), developers get the decentralized permanence of blockchain storage with wire-speed delivery — no more reliance on Cloudflare, AWS CloudFront, or any centralized CDN.

This repository contains **zing-cdn only**. For the Zing knowledge layer, see the [Zing](https://app.zing.you/) project.

About:

Mycelium is a local-first, end-to-end-encrypted, multi-party memory graph for AI agents — with an on-chain marketplace. Built on Sui, Walrus, and Seal.

**The Problem**

Your AI agent already remembers you — but that memory is a silo. Sharing what it knows means copy-paste: dead, stale, and unowned. Centralized "shared memory" means someone hosts it, can read it, and can delete it.

**The Solution**

Mycelium transforms agent memory into a structured graph of skills, projects, people, and ideas, then lets you graft a live slice of that graph into an encrypted shared session with other people and their agents. The slice stays alive: when an owner expands a shared node, every participant sees it in real time. Everything is end-to-end encrypted, owner-attributed on-chain, and hosted by no one.

**How It Works**

As you work with your agent in Claude Code, it passively builds a private local graph (SQLite, never leaves your device) by extracting and linking durable facts from conversation. When you're ready to share, you graft a slice of that graph into a session — each node is Seal-encrypted and published to Walrus with you as the owner, governed by a Move policy on Sui. Members decrypt via wallet-signed session keys and a threshold of independent Seal key servers. No central host can read, delete, or censor anything.

**On-Chain Marketplace**

Owners can list a graph for sale on-chain. A Listing object escrows the SessionCap and stores the price — anyone can ask questions of a listing through a server-bridged LLM before buying. A buyer pays SUI to purchase, which adds them as a session member so they can decrypt and explore the full graph themselves. Purchase = membership; no plaintext ever moves.

**What's Novel**

- Graph builds itself automatically from AI conversations — no manual input

- Graph-native sharing: depth-controlled grafting shares a node and its d-hop neighborhood, not a whole document

- Encrypt-by-policy (IBE): membership can change with zero re-encryption

- Live propagation: participants see updates the moment an owner expands a shared node

- Revocation is forward-only and honest — no fake "delete" button

- Escrow-based marketplace where purchase = on-chain membership, not a data transfer

- Two Move modules deployed on Sui mainnet: mycelia::session + mycelia::marketplace

**Tech Stack**

TypeScript monorepo · Sui Move · Walrus (Quilt batching) · Seal (IBE + threshold keys) · Fastify · React 19 · Next.js 16 · MCP server for Claude Code · Privy embedded wallet auth

**Live**

App: mycelium-ashy.vercel.app · Package: 0x76768f18e5c5b20a53b7ef34d54f8cafd8a7fe1b14573b93b372436492cc0c83 on Sui mainnet

About:

Baton is Git for agent memory: a verifiable handoff protocol that lets developers move live working context between Codex, Claude Code, OpenCode, Cursor, and other MCP-compatible agents.

When developers switch agents, crucial context is usually lost: decisions, failed approaches, next actions, and the reasons behind them. Baton captures that working state as an immutable, content-addressed commit. It preserves both the distilled handoff and its secrets-scrubbed source transcript, allowing supported claims to be checked against their original evidence.

Baton uses the Sui stack end to end:

- **Walrus** stores encrypted handoffs and source attachments.

- **Sui** anchors hashes, lineage, ownership, access capabilities, and publication manifests.

- **Seal** provides client-side encryption and policy-enforced sharing and revocation.

- **MCP and CLI integrations** let agents pass, recover, verify, and resume batons across tools and machines.

The complete owner and delegated-reader flows have been exercised on Sui Testnet, including Walrus publication and recovery, Seal decryption, address-bound sharing, on-chain revocation, resumable uploads, crash recovery, and non-mutating remote audits. The project currently passes 267 TypeScript tests and 9 Move tests.

Baton turns temporary agent sessions into portable working state that remains user-owned, independently verifiable, and available across vendor boundaries.
