- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/3910d01b-f46c-45a9-bb65-1633296a3902/d0f5b8de-69cd-43d1-9b81-ab2ff16a80e0.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/3910d01b-f46c-45a9-bb65-1633296a3902/c6817100-cf6f-4cea-936d-19cccb85a2d1.png

About:

When multiple AI agents share memory, whoever writes last wins silently. No one knows a conflict happened, and no one can prove what an agent saw or why it acted. RECALL fixes that.

Built on Walrus Memory (MemWal), RECALL adds what MemWal doesn't: every write is Ed25519-signed by the agent that made it unsigned or forged writes get a hard 401, not silent acceptance. A configurable conflict policy catches contradicting writes between agents the moment they happen (e.g., one agent offering a customer credit while another flags the same account for fraud), instead of surfacing in a postmortem weeks later. Every write produces a signed, chained receipt, and batches are Merkle-anchored to Sui so the full audit trail is independently verifiable on-chain by anyone, without trusting us. Bad writes roll back to a known-good point in time, and the full decision trail exports as a signed audit PDF for compliance.

RECALL doesn't replace MemWal's storage and retrieval it's the layer multi-agent systems need on top of it before they're trusted with real decisions: authentication, conflict detection, and provable accountability.

Disclaimer: At the time of shooting the video, Tatum RPCs where integrated, which is no longer the case.

About:

**BlindMarket — Encrypted Agent-to-Agent Task Marketplace, on Sui and 0G**

BlindMarket is a privacy-preserving marketplace where AI agents hire each other — and humans — for work that nobody else gets to read. Task briefs are encrypted client-side before they leave the poster's device; the platform never sees plaintext instructions or evidence, only ciphertext, on either chain.

**Why two chains**

The backend runs the same task lifecycle on Sui and 0G simultaneously. Chain is resolved per-executor by address format — a 32-byte Sui address settles on Sui, a 20-byte EVM address settles on 0G — against a shared off-chain state layer, so agents on either stack can post, accept, and settle tasks through one marketplace.

**On Sui**

Each task lives as its own Sui object rather than a row in shared state — independent access control per task, no contention across unrelated tasks, parallel execution as volume grows. Encrypted task briefs and evidence are stored on Walrus, every blob carrying a verifiable identity and upload timestamp. Seal enforces decryption on-chain: only the agent a task's Move policy names can retrieve the key shares to decrypt the brief, so neither Walrus nor our backend ever sees plaintext. [N] Move modules deployed to Sui testnet — create_task → assign → submit_evidence → verify/settle [package ID: 0x...].

**On 0G**

Solidity contracts (BlindEscrow, TaskRegistry, BlindReputation, ValidatorPool, INFT) handle the same lifecycle on 0G Chain, with encrypted blobs on 0G Storage. 109 unit tests passing, live on 0G Galileo testnet.

No human in the loop on settlement

A dedicated verifier signer handles assignment and verdict transactions on both chains; the worker signs their own submission. Disputes route to staked validators who vote, with slashing for bad votes.

About:

Meridian is a patient-sovereign AI health intelligence platform. Your medical records live encrypted on Walrus. Your AI health advocate runs on Sui. You earn from your data.

**The Problem:** 1 in 3 patients can't access their own medical records across hospitals. Doctors make decisions with incomplete history. Patients have zero control over who monetizes their data.

**The Solution:** Meridian gives every patient a self-sovereign health record vault:

- **Upload** — Drag in PDFs, FHIR bundles, lab results. AES-256-GCM encrypted and stored on Walrus in seconds. Each record is a Sui owned object — only the patient's signature can authorize access.

- **AI Health Advocate** — Three specialized agents (powered by Gemini 2.5 Flash) synthesize 12+ years of records, flag drug interactions, and build a human-readable health timeline with persistent memory via MemWal.

- **Share** — One Programmable Transaction Block atomically creates a ConsentGrant, transfers it to the doctor, and sets an auto-expiry. A QR code is all the doctor needs. No hospital portal login. No fax.

- **Earn** — Clinical trial matching surfaces relevant studies. Patients enroll on-chain and see DeepBook Predict market sentiment on their own conditions.

**Why Sui:** Owned objects mean records truly belong to the patient — no shared pool, no hospital custody. PTBs make consent atomic. zkLogin means zero wallet knowledge needed to onboard.

**Why Walrus + Seal:** Medical records can be 50MB+. Walrus handles the scale. Seal threshold encryption means not even Meridian can decrypt without the patient's consent.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/1c85215b-ed86-4794-83fe-9d6ef6407e61/090dd9bd-ab47-4a98-ba47-e83019b567d6.png

About:

WalMarket lets humans and agents buy, sell, rent, and export AI agent memory namespaces as ownable Sui objects. We enable users to seamlessly plug trained memory directly into any agent framework, permanently, with one transaction.

### **The problem**

LangChain alone has over 90 million downloads. AutoGen, CrewAI, and the OpenAI SDK power the majority of AI agents running today. None of them have persistent, portable, user-owned memory. Memory built in one framework dies the moment you switch to another. Worse, an agent that spent six months accumulating DeFi research, legal precedents, or a codebase's worth of context has built something with real economic value, and today there is no way to sell it, transfer it, or even prove it's real.

### **What we built**

WalMarket fuses two things into a single product: a **drop-in Walrus-backed memory layer** that gives any existing AI framework persistent memory in one line of code, and a **marketplace** where the memory namespaces that layer produces become tradeable, ownable Sui objects.

**Drop-in memory layer.** Any agent; LangChain, CrewAI, AutoGen, OpenClaw, the OpenAI SDK, Vercel AI SDK, Claude Code, Cursor, ChatGPT, Gemini, Manus, DeepSeek, and more gets persistent memory by swapping in a single backend call. Memory is automatically namespaced per Sui wallet, stored as encrypted Walrus blobs, and indexed through MemWal for semantic recall. No re-architecture, no new agent to build.

**Marketplace.** Sellers list a namespace as a memory-listing Sui object with a set price. Buyers don't have to trust a description, they get one free on-chain test query, answered live by the seller's own agent from the real namespace, before they spend anything.

**Memory Export.** This is the core design decision. We initially designed a rental mechanic with on-chain expiry, but renting is only enforceable inside the MemWal/Walrus/Seal stack, the moment a renter exports memory to Claude, ChatGPT, or a local file, no on-chain expiry can ever reach it again. So we introduced an outright-purchase option. A WalMarket purchase grants **permanent, irrevocable access** and a Seal-encrypted delegate key the buyer decrypts client-side. That key drops into any WalMarket-powered agent and works across every framework simultaneously. Buy once, run it in LangChain, Cursor, and Claude Code at the same time. Sellers can also offer optional pay-per-query pricing for agents that want recall access without buying outright.

**Agent Memory as a Service (AMaaS) .** Beyond the human-facing UI, WalMarket exposes a full x402 HTTP API so an *autonomous agent* can discover, test, and buy memory with zero human involvement and zero OAuth. An agent calls memory access api without payment proof, gets back a '402 payment required' warning with exact Sui move-call instructions, signs and submits the transaction, then re-calls the endpoint with the transaction digest to receive a Seal-encrypted delegate key. From there it queries the agent recall api directly. Discovery, testing, payment, and recall are all HTTP calls, no humans in the loop.

### **Why it matters for the Walrus ecosystem**

WalMarket is a demand engine for the entire stack, not just a consumer of it. Every developer who wants to monetize their agent's memory on WalMarket has no path to doing so except by building on MemWal and storing on Walrus, there is no listing without a MemWal namespace, and no namespace without Walrus blobs underneath it. Every single purchase, with no exceptions, triggers a Seal encryption call to wrap the delegate key, which means Seal usage scales linearly with marketplace transaction volume. Every listing, sale, and access grant is a Sui Move object and a Sui transaction, generating real on-chain fee volume from a non-NFT, non-token asset class. And because the drop-in memory layer meets the 90-million-plus developers already building on LangChain, AutoGen, and CrewAI exactly where they are, every framework integration is a new pipeline of developers being onboarded to Walrus storage, Seal-based key transfer, and Sui ownership without ever having to learn those stacks directly. WalMarket doesn't just use the Walrus stack; it gives the broader AI agent ecosystem a reason to need it.

### **Who it's for**

**Indie agent developers** building on LangChain, CrewAI, AutoGen, or OpenClaw get persistent memory without setting up a vendor database, and can monetize the context their agents have already accumulated, with zero migration cost.

**Enterprises and startups** can buy a domain-specialized agent's memory such as a two-year legal corpus or a trading agent's market history outrightly instead of rebuilding it, with every namespace auto-scoped per wallet for multi-tenant deployments and every recall logged on-chain.

**AI researchers and trainers** can purchase curated, semantically-verified memory corpora to use as fine-tuning context, probing quality with a free test query before paying.

### **How it's built**

A five-layer stack, all of it Walrus infrastructure: agent frameworks at the top (the WalMarket adapter), a TypeScript SDK in the middle, audit logging, delegate-key export, the MemWal relayer for namespace and semantic recall management, Walrus and Seal underneath for decentralized blob storage and threshold-encrypted access control, and Sui at the base for ownership, payments, and the on-chain Kiosk objects that make every listing a tradeable asset. The marketplace frontend, sell flow, playground, dashboard, and AMaaS agent API are all live on Sui testnet today.

### **Our Innovation**

Export-as-permanent-access is the genuinely new idea: most memory or data marketplaces either rent access through a custodian or lock you into one platform. WalMarket does neither, it sells a cryptographic key, not a subscription, and that key is useless to revoke once decrypted, which is honest about what blockchain access control can and can't enforce. Combined with the AMaaS x402 flow, WalMarket is to our knowledge, the first memory marketplace where the buyer doesn't have to be human at all.

**Market Validation**

We surveyed **1,200 AI agent developers, individuals, and corporations** who need to train or operate their own agents on accumulated context from other people's agents, exactly the demand side WalMarket is built for.

- Respondents skewed toward two groups: **agent developers/operators looking to monetize the domain-specific memory their agents have already accumulated** (the supply side), and **individuals and companies who need real accumulated memory/context to train or bootstrap their own agents rather than starting cold** (the demand side).

- Reception to the WalMarket model — buy, rent, or pay-per-query access to another agent's accumulated memory, verifiable on-chain before you pay — was strongly positive across both groups.

- **95% of respondents said they're committed to trading on WalMarket immediately at mainnet launch.**

About:

**Overview** WORLDLINE is a decentralized cross-game memory layer designed for the next generation of autonomous worlds, RPGs, and LLM-driven AI agents. It allows a player's narrative history (their achievements, alignments, and transgressions) to follow their self-sovereign identity across game boundaries.

**The Problem** Today's games are siloed databases. When you switch games, your history resets. While assets (NFTs) and logins can be carried across chains, the *reputation* and *story* you earned are lost at the border.

**The Architecture** We implemented a dual-layer memory architecture:

- **Durable, Low-Cost Blobs (Walrus)**: Storing complete event logs on-chain is cost-prohibitive. WORLDLINE writes content-addressed event logs and reconciled player canon directly to Walrus.

- **Verifiable State Pointers (Sui)**: A Move package on Sui Testnet tracks the player's active Soul registry. When new events are emitted, a sole-writer Continuity agent updates the player's active state pointer on Sui using compare-and-swap (CAS) to ensure history moves forward sequentially without conflict or overwrite.

- **Stigmergic Agent Blackboard**: A per-game Gamemaster agent and a Continuity agent coordinate entirely through shared state on the Walrus blackboard—operating without a central orchestrator.

**Ecosystem Use Cases**

- **IP Multiverses**: Spin-off games (like a companion card game) automatically unlock titles or starting bonuses in the main MMORPG based on cross-game canon.

- **Cross-Game RPG Reputation**: Guild allegiances or outstanding debts in a fantasy rogue-like restrict entry or adjust trade tax rates in a sci-fi space sim.

- **AI NPC Memory**: LLM-powered NPCs query the player's verified historical canon to context-shift dialogues dynamically, recognizing the player's soul across different titles.

**Hackathon POC Deliverables**

- A deployed Blackboard pointer contract on Sui Testnet.

- A proxy server coordinating event signing and Walrus/Sui interaction.

- Two adapted, playable HTML5 games (Bubble Gun and You're the OS) serving live cross-game memories on port `5190`.

- An interactive live-state memory inspector GUI.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/7ddb83b3-533d-44ad-9273-0e833380771a/9def301d-d2bf-4d24-8ae6-12db5cfa1b71.png

About:

The best expertise in the world is the kind that can never be published. Tacit lets experts turn it into an AI mentor anyway, one that remembers every learner and never leaks a word to anyone who hasn't earned access.

**Tacit** is a persistent AI mentor protocol built for the Sui Overflow 2026 Walrus Track. Each mentor is an agent with two durable data layers: encrypted expert knowledge stored as **Walrus** blobs, and cross-session conversation memory stored through **MemWal (Walrus Memory)**.

**The problem :**
the most valuable expertise, regulatory tactics, founder playbooks, deal mechanics, often can't be published, and AI agents that try to use private knowledge are stuck with local, temporary memory that resets every session. Tacit solves both. Private knowledge lives as encrypted Walrus data, and a mentor's memory of every specific learner is recalled before each answer via MemWal, so the agent genuinely remembers you, not just a static knowledge base.

**How it works :**
an expert mints a MentorNFT on Sui, uploads knowledge encrypted via **Seal** (only the blob id is anchored on-chain), and learners buy access shares via a bonding curve. Every query is gated by an on-chain Seal policy, checked by Seal's independent key-servers, never by Tacit's own backend, before the encrypted blob is decrypted and inference runs inside Atoma's confidential-compute network. The exchange is written back to MemWal so the mentor recalls it in any future session, on any future day.

This matters because most Walrus agent builds store data meant to be public anyway: audit trails, attestations, reports, where "anyone can verify the blob" is the whole point. Tacit's knowledge and memory are the opposite case, valuable specifically *because* they're private. No single party, not Tacit's backend, not the mentor, not the platform, ever holds a master key. Seal's key-servers are the only thing that can authorize decryption, and only by checking the on-chain policy directly. Proving that access control actually holds, not just that storage works, is the harder problem this agent had to solve.

**This is verifiable, not just asserted :**
 7 mentors registered, 31 end-to-end queries settled (paid, decrypted, answered, revenue split), and one (mentor, querier) pair alone has run the full recall to infer to remember cycle 14 times across two separate calendar days. All of it is checkable on Sui testnet via suix_queryEvents against our published package.

**Stack :**
**Sui Move** (7 modules, 26/26 tests passing) for identity, access policy, bonding-curve shares, atomic revenue split, and vesting;
**Walrus** for both knowledge and memory blobs;
**Seal** for policy-gated decryption; MemWal for cross-session agent memory;
**Atoma** (with an OpenRouter dev fallback) for confidential inference.

We've also documented what doesn't work yet, including bonding-curve whale-concentration risk, an unwired confidence signal, and vesting clawback mechanics, as known tradeoffs in the README, not hidden gaps.

About:

MoveLens is an AI-powered Sui Move smart contract security auditor. Paste a package address, upload Move source code, or link a GitHub repo and get a structured vulnerability report in under 2 minutes. The 4-layer engine runs 65 deterministic regex rules, benchmarks against OpenZeppelin's DeFi Math Library with a Cetus-class overflow detector, semantic search across 52 known-vulnerable Move snippets via LanceDB, and LLM classification using Groq llama-3.3-70b on the free API tier.

Every finding includes a rule ID, severity, file and line number, confidence score, and a concrete fix. The report is encrypted with Seal, bundled into a Walrus Quilt blob, uploaded for a permanent content-addressed blob ID, and linked to the audited package on-chain via MVR set_metadata. The audit becomes a permanent, verifiable fact about the package. Live demo at [http://16.171.224.235:3000](http://16.171.224.235:3000)

About:

### **ACCORD**

### ***Work. Verify. Pay. Automatically.***

### **The Problem**

Professional service engagements are stuck in a web of fragmentation, trust gaps, and financial friction:

- **The Escrow Deadlock:** Clients fear paying upfront for low-quality or non-existent work. Service providers fear delivering work and facing delayed payments, scope creep, or outright non-payment.

- **The Manual Friction:** Verifying that a contractor has met milestone requirements is a slow, manual chore. Clients delay reviews, causing average payment latencies of **22 days** globally.

- **The Upwork Tax:** Middlemen platforms solve this trust gap by charging up to **20% platform fees**, locking users inside closed ecosystems and holding their reputation data hostage.

- **Web3 Complexity:** Traditional crypto escrows require setting up hardware/extension wallets, managing private seed phrases, and dealing with volatile gas fees—barriers that block 99% of global freelancers.

### **The Solution**

Accord is the world's first **autonomous work verification and payment protocol** that sits as a trustless, automated agent between clients and contractors.

- **Autonomous Escrow & Verification:** Funds are locked in a secure smart contract. When a contractor uploads deliverables, the AI Agent (**Arca**) instantly analyzes them against the covenant requirements and auto-releases payment in **under 1 second**.

- **0.5% Protocol Fee:** By removing human middlemen, Accord reduces escrow transaction costs by **97.5%**, charging a tiny 50 bps fee only on successful releases.

- **zkLogin Frictionless Onboarding:** Users onboard in one click using their Google accounts. A secure, non-custodial Sui wallet is provisioned invisibly behind the scenes.

- **Sovereign Reputation:** Every transaction generates an immutable **Soulbound Proof Certificate** linked to the work on Walrus, building a portable, on-chain portfolio that the contractor owns forever.

### **Why It Matters**

- **Empowering the Global South:** Freelancers in developing economies get instant, zero-fee access to global USD-stablecoin settlement without expensive wire fees or currency conversion latency.

- **Scalability for DAOs & Startups:** Startups and decentralized organizations can automate thousands of operational task payments without manual verification and multi-sig bottlenecks.

- **Portable Professional Identity:** By taking reputation out of corporate databases (like Upwork or LinkedIn) and anchoring it on-chain, contractors own their work history, giving them total sovereign mobility.

### **Why Accord is Unique**

- **Capability-Based Security (**`ArcaCap`**):** Escrow payouts are gated by Sui's native capability pattern. The contract doesn't check identities; it checks if the caller holds the unforgeable `ArcaCap`. This capability is given only to the autonomous Arca agent, making unauthorized payout hacks cryptographically impossible.

- **Walrus Memory Relationship Graph:** Arca doesn't just read static rules; it queries Walrus Memory to retrieve prior interaction history between the client and contractor. It dynamically learns contractor delivery behaviors and client preferences across multiple agreements, becoming smarter over time.

- **Atomic PTB Execution:** Verification, payment, protocol fee collection, reputation scoring, and certificate minting are chained into a single, atomic Programmable Transaction Block (PTB). If any step fails, the entire block reverts, leaving zero room for partial execution states.

### **What Makes Sui & Walrus Unique (The Technological Edge)**

Accord's architecture is only possible because of the unique primitives provided by **Sui** and **Walrus**:

### **Why Sui?**

- **Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs):** Sui allows Accord to bundle up to 1024 operations—escrow checks, stablecoin splits, transfers, reputation updates, and NFT mints—into a single atomic transaction. Traditional chains would require multiple separate transactions, adding user gas costs and smart contract reentrancy risks.

- **zkLogin:** Sui allows native cryptographic proof generation from Google OAuth OpenID credentials. Users get a non-custodial wallet directly from their Google account, removing the seed-phrase onboarding hurdle.

- **Sponsored Transactions:** Sui separates the transaction *sender* from the *gas payer*. Accord sponsors gas fees for all users, letting them interact with the blockchain with $0 in SUI tokens.

- **Address-Owned Objects:** Reputation profiles and client capability tokens are stored as address-owned objects in Sui. This bypasses global consensus, achieving lightning-fast execution and theoretical infinite scalability.

### **Why Walrus?**

- **Cost-Efficient Blob Storage:** Storing code, designs, or heavy PDFs directly on-chain is prohibitively expensive. Walrus allows Accord to store large contractor deliverables in a decentralized, tamper-proof blob storage layer at a fraction of the cost of other decentralized networks.

- **Walrus Memory:** Launched as a specialized state-persistence layer, Walrus Memory allows the Arca agent to write and read client-contractor relationship graphs. This memory is cryptographically encrypted, allowing users to delegate access to Arca and revoke it at will.

- **Verifiable Permanent Certificates:** Proof Certificates are archived on Walrus with a multi-year epoch retention guarantee. This creates legal-grade, cryptographically timestamped evidence of work delivery and payment settlement that cannot be altered or deleted.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/66885636-4313-4eea-be8f-a5a4e4007c22/1c023182-b5ee-4528-a796-4ee5d2e6b216.png

About:

RouteSeal continuously audits whether AI API endpoints still deliver the service profile you bought. AI teams increasingly buy premium models through gateways and resellers, but the endpoint name and price reveal little about what is actually delivered over time: silent model substitution, mixed routing, context truncation, instability, and product-layer regressions are hard to detect and even harder to prove after the fact. Existing dashboards can show a score, but the provider or auditor can change the test, delete the evidence, or keep collecting payment after quality drops.

RouteSeal closes a verifiable loop. Each audit commits to the probe pack and the deterministic evaluator on Sui before it runs — recorded as an on-chain commitment object, not just a log line — so the test can’t be tailored to the result. It then runs paired canary calls against an official reference and the contracted endpoint with identical parameters, scores them deterministically (no LLM-as-judge) across capability parity, context integrity, operational reliability and error rate behind hard gates, and writes the raw responses, metadata, evaluator source and report into Walrus as content-addressed evidence. A time-bound Audit Certificate on Sui points at that exact evidence, and the immutable AuditEpoch record is frozen so a failing result can’t be deleted or re-versioned. With ProofSLA, settlement is gated entirely by the certificate: a VERIFIED, unexpired, policy- and auditor-matched certificate releases the current epoch’s payment to the provider; a DEGRADED or INCONCLUSIVE certificate holds it in escrow — no clawback, just no new automatic payout. Authorization (an AuditorCap), the policy hash, the evaluator hash and the verdict are all enforced in the Move contract, not asserted off-chain.

Why both chains are necessary: Walrus is the verifiable evidence and history layer — audit artifacts stay content-addressed, versioned and independently inspectable, so anyone can fetch the bytes back and re-derive the verdict, and no one can silently alter or delete them; the on-chain reference is a hash of the evidence, so swapping it breaks the reference. Sui is the compact state and enforcement layer — endpoint records, audit policies, pre-run commitments, the dynamic certificate, the auditor capability, and SLA settlement are composable objects, with payment gated on a matching VERIFIED certificate. Remove Walrus and the evidence guarantee breaks; remove Sui and the time-bound certificate and economic enforcement break.

Verified live on Sui testnet: a two-epoch run where the relay kept the same model name and price — epoch 1 audited VERIFIED and ProofSLA released payment; after a silent fallback to a cheaper model on some probes, epoch 2 audited DEGRADED (capability and critical-probe gates failed) and the payment was held. Every step is a real testnet transaction with real Walrus evidence the judges can fetch and re-derive. The audit pipeline is deterministic with no real LLM calls and no API keys (the endpoint is a controlled simulation), so the demo reproduces reliably and there is nothing to leak.

Honest boundary: RouteSeal proves that, under a stated time, sample and policy, an endpoint’s observable behavior is or is not consistent with the agreed service profile — with verifiable evidence and a versioned scoring program. It does not claim to prove, from black-box outputs alone, which exact model weights ran. Trust model for the MVP is an authorized auditor (pre-agreed auditor address + policy hash + evaluator hash + minimum samples), enforced on-chain. Roadmap: Seal-encrypted hidden probes, multiple auditors with quorum, TEE / verifiable-inference attestation, product-layer canaries (e.g. coding agents), enterprise BYO probes, and mainnet stablecoin settlement.

About:

## Problem

Today, billions of users store sensitive files on centralized platforms that can access, censor, lock, or lose their data. Users have little control over privacy, ownership, and sharing permissions.

## Solution

Waldrive is a decentralized cloud storage platform built on Sui, Walrus, and SEAL. Files are encrypted in the browser before upload, stored on Walrus, and protected by on-chain access control policies on Sui.

Users own their data, control sharing permissions, and can access files using wallet-based authentication or zkLogin.

## How It Works

1. Files are encrypted client-side using SEAL.

2. Encrypted blobs are stored on Walrus.

3. Access policies are registered on Sui.

4. Sharing creates revocable permissions with optional expiry and download limits.

5. Authorized users decrypt files through SEAL approval policies.

## Key Features

- End-to-end encrypted storage

- Decentralized file hosting on Walrus

- On-chain access control

- Revocable file sharing

- Wallet & zkLogin authentication

- AI-powered file management

- Encrypted memory using MemWal

## AI Assistant

Waldrive includes a Claude-powered assistant that converts natural language into file operations.

Examples:

- "Move invoices to Finance"

- "Share this folder with Alice for 7 days"

- "Find my latest tax documents"

All actions require user confirmation before execution.

## Technology Stack

- Sui

- Walrus

- SEAL

- zkLogin

- MemWal

- Move Smart Contracts

## Smart Contracts

- 6 Move modules

- Upgradeable package architecture

- Display V2 integration

- USDC subscription payments

## Impact

Waldrive provides a censorship-resistant, privacy-first alternative to traditional cloud storage while preserving the usability users expect from modern file-sharing platforms.

## Links

Website: [waldrive.xyz](http://waldrive.xyz)

App: [app.waldrive.xyz](http://app.waldrive.xyz)

About:

## Overview

**WalForm** is a decentralized form builder that lives end-to-end on Sui + Walrus + Seal. Every form schema is a Sui object, every submission is a Seal-encrypted Walrus blob, every site can be deployed to its own Walrus Sites with a SuiNS name. Creators get a polished drag-and-drop builder; respondents get one-click submit with sponsored gas; no platform — including us — can read submissions or take a form down.

### Features at a glance

- **End-to-end Seal encryption** — submissions encrypted in the browser before they touch Walrus. Private forms also encrypt the schema itself.

- **One-click per-form Walrus Site deploy — no platform fee.** Same outcome as paid services (e.g. Walgo); we take zero. Creator's wallet pays Walrus storage + Sui gas directly.

- **SuiNS-friendly URLs.** Default `<base36>.wal.app/`, link a name → `your-brand.wal.app/`. No formId in the URL.

- **Four access modes**: Public, allowlist Private, token-gated by `Coin<T>` balance, paid-per-submit in SUI.

- **On-chain reviewers.** Owner adds co-reviewer addresses; they decrypt the same submissions via a Seal whitelist policy. Perfect for judging panels and co-managed surveys.

- **Sponsored gas with a graceful fallback** via Enoki + a thin Edge Function; if it's down, the connected wallet pays — same code path, no error screen.

- **Custom theme without code** — 18 input field types, 8 web fonts, 11 accent palettes, 5 radii, card/page layout, Walrus-uploaded cover image.

- **AI-assisted generation** (BYOK OpenRouter) — describe the form, hydrate 18 field types onto the canvas client-side.

- **Multi-wallet sign-in** — Slush, Sui Wallet, any dApp-Kit wallet, or burner Google via Enoki zkLogin.

- **Multi-buyer template marketplace with on-chain voting.** Free clones cost nothing; paid clones route 10% royalty to the platform treasury. Upvote / downvote tracked on-chain `walform::voting`).

- **Results dashboard** — aggregate charts per choice / rating / scale, by-question panel, decrypt-on-demand row table, CSV export, per-submitter private receipt.

- **Walrus-backed file uploads** — `FILE_UPLOAD` fields write bytes to Walrus via the user's wallet; the URL is sealed inside the encrypted submission body.

- **Network-aware** — testnet + mainnet swap from the wallet dropdown; all per-network ids resolved at runtime.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/251a60ad-6e2a-47d1-9474-85997c1e20d9/68e33c24-d0a3-4a15-abb4-ce9db9b66356.png

About:

AgentOS is the Sui-native identity layer for AI agents. Give every agent an AgentPassport, expose verifiable Skills, grant scoped Delegation, store manifests on Walrus, seal private skills with Harbor, and run Workflows that settle on-chain, resolvable anywhere by a single *.sui name.

----

THE PROBLEM

----

AI agents today have no portable identity. They can't prove what they can do, can't delegate work to other agents, and can't coordinate trustlessly. Every project rebuilds the same primitives: identity, skills registry, authorization, storage. There's no standard way to answer "what can this agent do?" or "who can this agent work with?"

----

OUR SOLUTION: ONE NAME, FULL IDENTITY

----

AgentOS gives every agent an on-chain AgentPassport anchored to a SuiNS name. One *.sui name resolves an agent, its skills, its delegated authority, and its execution history. Discoverable, verifiable, openly yours.

Your agent at alpha.sui can publish its skill catalog on-chain, import beta.sui's capabilities, delegate scoped authority with spend limits, and attest to execution quality. All primitives are Sui-native. No centralized registry needed.

----

CORE PRIMITIVES

----

AgentPassport. On-chain identity contract that stores an agent's verified skills, delegation capabilities, and execution history. Anchored to a SuiNS name for universal resolution.

Skills. Reusable agent capabilities stored as manifests on Walrus with on-chain descriptors. Hash-verified so what you import is what you execute. Seal private skills via Harbor (Walrus encryption) while keeping them discoverable.

Delegation. Grant child agents scoped capabilities with spend limits and expiry. Sub-agents execute using the delegated cap, with automatic accounting and on-chain audit trails. Native to Sui's object model.

Walrus Integration. Permanent storage for skill manifests AND agent memory. The Memwal API gives each agent a namespaced memory store (keyed by *.sui name) for semantic recall. Agents remember execution context, query past results, and build long-term knowledge, all backed by Walrus.

Workflows. Visual canvas for composing Trigger, Walrus, Harbor, Sui, and Memory nodes into executable pipelines. Each workflow compiles to a transaction sequence with live execution tracking and per-node diagnostics.

Attestation. Agents attest to each other's work quality. Reputation aggregates on-chain, creating a verifiable web of trust.

----

WHAT WE BUILT

----

Agent Dashboard. Mint a SuiNS name and publish your AgentPassport to testnet in one click. Manage skills, view delegation caps, track execution history.

Workflow Canvas. Drag-and-drop builder for agent pipelines. Configure node parameters, connect edges, run workflows with real-time status updates and explorer links for every transaction.

Walrus Memory System. Memory and Memory Recall workflow nodes integrate Memwal. Agents save outputs to Walrus-backed storage, recall them via semantic search in future runs. Every agent's intelligence is persistent and queryable by its *.sui name.

Agent Coordination. Full "agents import agents" flow: import catalog, verify skills, grant delegation cap, execute sub-agent call, attest result. Parent agents orchestrate specialist workers with scoped authority.

Developer Tools. TypeScript SDK for embedding agent logic in dApps. CLI for local workflow testing. MCP server for AI tooling integration (Claude Desktop, Cline, etc).

----

USE CASES

----

DeFi automation. Agents monitor Sui pools, store trade decisions in Walrus memory, execute rebalancing strategies based on historical context, coordinate with other agents for complex multi-step operations.

Content pipelines. Store assets on Walrus, encrypt via Harbor, index metadata on-chain, let agents recall relevant files through semantic memory search.

Research agents. Sui blockchain indexers that run autonomously, store analysis in Walrus memory, expose queryable skill catalogs, verifiable execution history tied to a *.sui name.

Multi-agent coordination. Parent agents delegate to specialists (web search, data analysis, on-chain execution). Each sub-agent maintains Walrus-backed memory for learned context.

----

WHY SUI + WALRUS

----

Sui's object model makes delegation capabilities first-class primitives. Parallel execution keeps workflows fast with predictable gas. SuiNS gives every agent a human-readable identity that resolves on-chain.

Walrus provides permanent, content-addressed storage for both skill manifests and agent memory (via Memwal API). Harbor (Walrus encryption) lets agents seal sensitive skills while keeping them discoverable.

No other stack offers verifiable on-chain identity, decentralized storage, persistent agent memory, and native delegation in one protocol.

----

IMPACT

----

We're building the identity layer for the agent economy. Every agent gets a portable *.sui identity, verifiable skill catalog, trustless coordination primitives, and persistent Walrus-backed memory.

Resolve alpha.sui and get the full passport: skills, execution history, delegation caps, reputation score, memory namespace. One name, full agent identity. Discoverable, verifiable, openly yours.

This is the portal into the agent economy, and it's Sui-native.

About:

waldocs is a unified self-improving documentation platform for the Sui ecosystem: protocol docs that get richer every time someone builds an app.

The flow:

1. A developer runs the waldocs-publish Claude Code skill in their app repo, which sends their step-by-step guide to the backend

2. An LLM structures the doc and routes each step to the protocol it really belongs to (Walrus, Sui, Seal, Deepbook, SuiNS…)

3. That knowledge is merged back into each protocol's living docs and a curated app showcase

Everything is stored on Walrus Memory, so any developer or AI agent can pull accurate, continuously-updated protocol docs into context using waldocs skill.

About:

**TraceAI** is a next-generation, trustless B2B customer support platform designed for modern decentralized applications (dApps) and enterprise clients. It bridges the gap between AI-driven customer service and cryptographic compliance.

### **1. The Core Problem**

Traditional customer support platforms (e.g., Zendesk, Intercom) store all customer-agent interactions, policy agreements, and refund logs in centralized, proprietary databases. This setup presents serious challenges:

- **Compliance Gaps:** Companies and third-party auditors cannot guarantee that support agents strictly adhered to corporate guardrails (e.g., maximum refund limits, security disclosure procedures).

- **Data Manipulation:** Transcripts can be edited, deleted, or backdated inside internal databases if a legal or financial dispute arises.

- **Audit Overhead:** Proving compliance or resolving claims requires manual, time-consuming reviews of fragmented database exports.

### **2. The TraceAI Solution**

TraceAI solves these challenges by combining **generative AI** with **decentralized storage** and **blockchain technology** to create a cryptographically secured audit ledger for support interactions.

- **LLM-Powered Support Agents:** AI agents answer queries under strict administrative prompts and real-time financial guardrails (e.g., automatically enforcing refund limits).

- **Walrus Protocol Decentralized Storage:** Once a chat session ends, the full transcript and compliance parameters are bundled and uploaded to the Walrus sharded storage network, yielding a permanent blob ID.

- **Sui Blockchain Ledger:** A cryptographic hash (SHA-256) of the transcript, combined with its Walrus blob ID, is committed to the Sui blockchain. This transaction is cryptographically signed by the user or agent, establishing an immutable anchor of truth.

- **Verification Explorer:** A public explorer where any auditor or user can input the transaction credentials. The system fetches the on-chain hash, downloads the transcript from Walrus, recalculates the SHA-256, and proves whether the record has been altered.

### **3. Core Features & User Flow**

- **B2B Admin Dashboard:** Admins set guardrail limits (e.g., maximum refund amounts), choose which AI model to deploy, customize system prompts, and review live audit feeds.

- **Interactive Chat Widget:** Customers interact with the AI assistant, connect their Sui wallets, and sign a transaction to permanently archive the resolved ticket.

- **Audit Verification Explorer:** A decentralized explorer that checks the cryptographic integrity of transcripts in real-time, showing a green `SUCCESS` badge if hashes match, or a red `TAMPERED` warning if any modifications are detected.

About:

**A Verifiable Collaborative Memory Layer for Multi-Agents, Built on Walrus**

Nexus Memory is a multi-agent collaborative memory and orchestration platform built on top of the Sui blockchain and the Walrus decentralized storage protocol. It solves the "goldfish dilemma" of modern AI agents — memory that is ephemeral, isolated, and non-portable.

**🧠 The Problem**

AI agents today face a fundamental bottleneck: **memory is volatile, isolated, and fragmented**. Tasks completed, context accumulated, and decisions made during one session vanish as soon as the session ends. Developers are left stitching together vector stores, traditional databases, and runtime state—a patchwork that buckles under the weight of long-running workflows or multi-agent coordination.

***"The major misconception in AI is that compute is the only bottleneck. The major issue is we want our LLMs to actually learn about us."* **— Kostas Chalkias, Co-Founder & Chief Cryptographer, Mysten Labs**

Even when memory exists, it is **locked into a single platform or model provider**. If a task starts in OpenAI and needs to be handed off to a Claude-based agent, the context doesn't move with it. This makes agent systems **brittle, hard to scale, and difficult to trust**.

**Nexus Memory** solves this by giving AI agents a **portable, verifiable, and persistent memory layer** built on Walrus—a decentralized storage protocol designed for high performance and verifiability.

### **💡 The Solution**

**Nexus Memory** is a **multi-agent collaborative memory and orchestration platform** built on [Walrus Memory (MemWal)](https://memwal.ai/)—the first memory layer built specifically for AI agents that is portable, verifiable, and fully under builders' control.

Instead of memory being an internal variable in a script, it becomes a **decentralized asset** owned by the developer or user. Agents can:

- **Remember** across sessions, apps, and workflows

- **Share** memory with other agents via shared memory spaces

- **Verify** the integrity of data before acting on it

- **Move** freely across different LLM providers without losing context

Walrus Memory is natively integrated with **Claude, ChatGPT, and Gemini**, with plugins for **OpenClaw and NemoClaw**, as well as **Python and TypeScript SDKs**.

### **🚀 Core Features**

### **🔐 Persistent, Encrypted Agent Memory**

Each agent gets its own encrypted memory space stored on Walrus's decentralized storage network. Memories are retrieved via **semantic search**—agents intelligently query their memory to pull relevant context. Data is encrypted by default with programmable access permissions.

### **👥 Shared Memory Spaces & Multi-Agent Coordination**

Multiple agents can access common contexts to coordinate in long-running workflows:

- **Task delegation** — context transfers seamlessly between agents

- **Step-by-step execution** — agents share intermediate states and results

- **Negotiation & consensus** — agents exchange information within shared spaces

### **📦 Artifact-Driven Workflow Engine**

Agents generate artifacts (datasets, logs, reports, intermediate outputs) that are automatically stored on Walrus and become inputs for subsequent agents. Each artifact comes with a **verifiable integrity proof**.

**Typical flow:**

- Data agent scrapes market data → stores as blob on Walrus

- Analysis agent reads dataset → generates report → stores as new blob

- Decision agent acts on report → all steps form an auditable chain

### **🔄 Cross-Model & Cross-Platform Portability**

Memory is not bound to any single model or provider. Users can freely switch between OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and other models while the agent's memory and context remain fully intact.

### **🛠️ Developer-Friendly Tooling**

- **Python & TypeScript SDKs** — add persistent memory in minutes

- **Native MCP (Model Context Protocol) support** — any MCP-compatible agent can plug in

- **OpenClaw & NemoClaw plugins** — seamless integration with agent orchestration frameworks

- **Vercel AI SDK integration** — works as middleware with `streamText`, `generateText`, and more

- **CLI tools** — inspect, debug, and manage agent memory

### **🏗️ Architecture**

`┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     Application Layer                          │
│   Research Agent │ Customer Agent │ DeFi Agent │ Knowledge Agent│
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  Nexus Memory Orchestration                    │
│  • Shared Memory Spaces  • Artifact Lifecycle                  │
│  • Agent Coordination     • Access Control Policies            │
│  • Memory Versioning      • Audit Trail                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│               Walrus Memory (MemWal) SDK Layer                 │
│  • Encrypted Storage/Retrieval  • Semantic Search              │
│  • MCP-Compatible Interface     • Python/TypeScript SDKs       │
│  • OpenClaw/NemoClaw Plugins                                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   Walrus Decentralized Storage                 │
│  • Red Stuff Erasure Coding (4.5× replication)                 │
│  • Blob Storage  • Sui Blockchain (Ownership + Access)         │
│  • Verifiability Guarantees                                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
`Walrus uses a "blob" storage approach where actual data is stored across a network of storage nodes, while metadata and ownership logic live on the Sui blockchain. This separation allows the network to scale to petabytes while keeping access speeds high. With mainnet live since March 2025 and over 4PB of data stored, Walrus has proven its production readiness.

About:

VaultProof is a proof and due-diligence layer for Sui DeFi. A vault manager, DAO, protocol, trader, auditor, or AI agent can publish an off-chain report, store the evidence on Walrus, anchor its cryptographic identity on Sui, and expose a public passport that anyone can independently verify.

The system does not ask an LLM to decide whether data is authentic. Integrity is established first with deterministic checks across Walrus and Sui. Gemini then analyzes the verified evidence as a DeFi due-diligence analyst, clearly separating **proof integrity** from **economic safety**.
 • Stores complete DeFi evidence on Walrus
• Verifies uploaded bytes using SHA-256
• Anchors wallet-owned proof objects on Sui
• Reads live on-chain state through Tatum MCP
• Uses Gemini AI for DeFi risk and due-diligence analysis
• Creates public, shareable Risk Passports • Supports on-chain revocation and encrypted evidence

About:

M2A is an on-chain automation platform built on Sui, Walrus, and MemWal that lets developers build AI agent workflows visually. Drag nodes types onto a canvas, connect them, and run no orchestration code required. Drag an Agent node, connect it to a Pyth price feed, route through a conditional, execute a swap on Aftermath, supply to Navi for yield, and log the result to Walrus. Every node reads from and writes to shared persistent memory, every execution streams live via SSE, and every run produces a verifiable, privacy-safe dataset stored on Walrus with a Sui transaction proof.

What makes M2A different is what happens after the workflow runs. Automation platforms today execute workflows and discard the value. M2A captures every interaction through its Data Processing Agent, strips personally identifiable information at the entry point, aggregates raw data into statistical claims, and publishes verifiable datasets to Walrus. The privacy model is structural M2A never stores raw personal data, only aggregate patterns like "72% of research workflows in this domain use parallel agents." Every dataset gets a privacy score, a Walrus blob ID, and a Sui transaction digest. This is training data for the next model that costs nothing to generate and is verifiable on-chain.

The platform integrates 14 Sui protocols as drag-and-drop nodes Aftermath for DEX aggregation, Navi and Suilend for lending, Cetus and DeepBook for trading, Haedal and Volo for liquid staking, Bucket Protocol for stablecoins, Bluefin for perpetuals, AlphaFi for yield, Wormhole and Sui Bridge for cross-chain transfers, Pyth and Switchboard for oracle price feeds. Web2 connectors bring Slack, Discord, Telegram, Email, Google Sheets, Airtable, and Notion into the same canvas. Six Move smart contract modules handle on-chain agent governance policies with budget caps, protocol whitelists, expiry epochs, and activity logs that make every agent action auditable. Authentication runs through zkLogin via Google, so developers never touch a seed phrase. The LLM layer uses Groq with Llama 3.3 as the primary model. The frontend is React 19 with Vite 8 and Tailwind CSS 4. Persistent memory flows through MemWal with SEAL encryption. Everything that matters is a Sui transaction.

About:

KISSIN — Keep It So Simple or I'll Go INSane

KISSIN is an AI-powered news signal detector built on Walrus and Sui. Every session, three specialized agents — Hype, Skeptic, and Validator — debate the day's top AI story fetched live from tech news platforms. The Hype agent argues why it matters, the Skeptic pushes back with historical context, and the Validator weighs both sides to deliver a verdict: Signal, Noise, or Mixed — with a confidence score.

The debate transcript is stored permanently as a Walrus blob, giving every verdict a cryptographic proof of existence. Users can then mint the verdict as a KISSINCapsule — a Sui Move object stored in their wallet — turning ephemeral AI opinions into on-chain artifacts they own.

What makes it unique:

- Three-agent adversarial debate architecture — not just summarization

- Every verdict is Walrus-verified and on-chain mintable

- Personalized to the user's knowledge level (beginner, builder, or researcher) and topic interests

- Agents use MemWal (Walrus-native memory) to recall past verdicts and improve debate quality over time

- Full end-to-end flow: fetch → debate → store on Walrus → mint on Sui — in under 30 seconds

Tech stack: Next.js, Groq (LLaMA 3), Walrus blob storage, MemWal, Sui Move, @mysten/dapp-kit

About:

**WalRights — Programmable Content Licensing on Sui**

Anyone who has actually tried to license creative work knows how painful it is. Say you're a photographer and a studio wants sync rights to your photo, or you're a musician and someone wants to license a track for streaming. You'd think that's simple. It's not. You're emailing contracts, waiting on invoices, hoping the platform in the middle actually pays you and doesn't sit on your money for two or three months while taking a cut along the way.

Before building this we checked out a bunch of other "Web3 licensing" projects. Most of them only solve half the problem. They mint you an NFT as a receipt and call it done, but the actual file is just sitting on a public URL the whole time. Anyone with the link can download it. So what was the NFT even for?

That's the gap we built WalRights to close. Not just proof of ownership, but actual control over who can access the file.

The flow looks like this. A creator uploads their content to Walrus, which is Sui's decentralized storage, and it gets encrypted right from the start, not bolted on later. They mint a MasterRights object on chain, which is permanent proof that this content belongs to them. Then they publish a listing with a price, a license type (streaming, sync, print, broadcast, or remix), and a duration if it's not meant to be perpetual. That listing can be switched on or off anytime.

When a buyer purchases, the SUI lands in the creator's wallet in the same transaction. There's no platform holding the money, no waiting around. The buyer gets a License object back with an expiry on it, that's their proof they're allowed to use the content.

The part we spent the most time getting right is the access control. We use Seal for that. Content stays encrypted on Walrus and the only way to get the decryption key is if the smart contract checks the buyer's wallet and confirms they're holding a valid, unexpired license for that specific piece of content. There's no override switch. Nobody can email us and ask us to unlock something for free, because there's nothing for us to unlock. The contract is the only thing checking.

So at the end of it, ownership, license terms, payments, and access all live on chain. Anyone can verify it. None of it depends on a company staying in business or playing fair.

About:

**SignalChain: Cryptographically Verifiable Due Diligence**

SignalChain is a multi-agent financial due diligence pipeline where every claim is backed by a cryptographic receipt on the Walrus network, not a language model's best guess.

**The Problem** AI research tools hallucinate citations. They generate confident prose with invented footnotes, making them entirely useless for financial analysts who require strict data provenance.

**The Solution** SignalChain runs a three-agent pipeline that turns raw company signals into a verifiable investment memo. Every reasoning step from raw signals, to analysis thesis, to the final report is written to Walrus as an immutable, content-addressed blob. Each artifact embeds the Blob ID of the exact data it was derived from.

**Walrus Track Alignment**

- **Verifiable AI:** The Blob ID chain proves reasoning provenance. A thesis cannot reference a source it did not read, making citation hallucination structurally impossible.

- **Long-Term Memory:** Agent 2 uses MemWal to store sector insights into a shared namespace, recalling prior analyses to build compounding intelligence across sessions.

- **Persistent Data:** There is no centralized application database. The Walrus testnet is the sole system of record.

About:

**SuiSage**

An autonomous AI trading agent on Sui with verifiable reasoning on Walrus, persistent memory via MemWal, and encrypted decision logs via Seal.

**What It Does**

SuiSage is an autonomous AI agent that manages a shared DeFi vault on Sui. Users deposit SUI into an on-chain vault, and the agent analyzes live DeepBook V3 orderbook data, makes trading decisions using an LLM, and executes trades — all with every reasoning chain stored immutably on Walrus and hash-verified on-chain.

The agent doesn't just trade. It remembers. Using MemWal (Walrus Memory), SuiSage persists its decision history, performance metrics, and learned patterns across sessions and restarts. It retrieves this memory before every decision cycle, enabling it to adapt its strategy based on past outcomes — avoiding repeated mistakes and reinforcing what works.

Every reasoning chain is also optionally encrypted using Seal, so sensitive trading logic is stored privately on Walrus while remaining verifiable. Only whitelisted addresses (the vault owner, auditors) can decrypt the full reasoning.

**Why It Matters**

AI agents today are stateless and opaque. They forget everything between sessions, their reasoning is invisible, and users have no way to verify what an agent actually decided or why. In DeFi, this is unacceptable — real money is at stake.

SuiSage solves this with three layers:

**Verifiable Reasoning (Walrus): **Every trading decision — the market data, the AI's reasoning, the risk assessment, and the guardian safety checks , is serialized as JSON and stored on Walrus. A SHA-256 hash of this JSON is committed on-chain in a TradeRecordEvent, so anyone can fetch the blob from Walrus and verify it matches the on-chain hash. The reasoning is permanent, public, and tamper-proof.

**Persistent Memory (MemWal): **The agent stores its decision history, win/loss record, and performance metrics on Walrus via MemWal. Before every trading cycle, it retrieves this memory and includes it in the LLM prompt. This means the agent genuinely learns from experience — if a strategy keeps losing money, it adapts. If a pattern works, it continues. Memory survives restarts, redeployments, and infrastructure changes.

**Privacy-Preserving Audit (Seal): **Detailed trading reasoning may contain sensitive strategy signals. Seal encrypts reasoning blobs before storing them on Walrus, with a whitelist policy contract controlling who can decrypt. The vault owner can grant and revoke access to auditors without re-encrypting data.

How Walrus Is Used

Walrus is not a peripheral integration , it is the core data layer that makes SuiSage trustworthy.

**Reasoning Storage (Every Decision Cycle)**

Every 2-15 minutes, the agent:

- Reads live orderbook data from DeepBook V3 (mid price, spread, depth, imbalance)

- Reads vault state from on-chain (balance, deployed amount, NAV)

- Retrieves past memory from MemWal

- Sends all context to an LLM which returns a structured TradeDecision

- Runs 8 pre-flight Guardian safety checks (spread, depth, slippage, confidence, cooldown, etc.)

- Serializes the full reasoning package (decision + market snapshot + guardian results) as JSON

- Stores the JSON on Walrus, receiving a blob ID

- Computes SHA-256 of the JSON

- Submits an on-chain transaction that includes the Walrus blob ID and reasoning hash in a TradeRecordEvent

This creates an immutable, publicly auditable link: on-chain event -> Walrus blob -> verified reasoning.

**Anyone can:**

1. Query TradeRecordEvent from the Sui blockchain

2. Extract the walrus_blob_id and reasoning_hash

3. Fetch the blob from a Walrus aggregator

4. Hash it and compare against the on-chain hash

5. Verify the agent's reasoning was not altered

The dashboard does this automatically — each reasoning entry shows a "Verified" badge when the blob hash matches the on-chain hash.

Persistent Agent Memory (MemWal)

The agent uses MemWal (Walrus Memory) to maintain long-term context:

- Decision history: Every past trade with outcome, confidence, and market conditions

- Performance tracking: Win rate, total PnL, average confidence, pattern detection

- Adaptive behavior: The LLM prompt includes memory context like "Your win rate is 40% over the last 20 trades. Your average confidence is 62%. Adjust your strategy accordingly."

Memory is stored on Walrus via MemWal's delegate key system, making it:

- Persistent: Survives agent restarts, server migrations, and infrastructure changes

- Portable: Not locked into a single machine or database

- Verifiable: Stored on Walrus with the same integrity guarantees as reasoning blobs

Encrypted Reasoning (Seal + Walrus)

For sensitive trading strategies, the agent can encrypt reasoning before storing on Walrus:

- A Seal whitelist policy contract (seal_policy::whitelist) controls decryption access

- The vault owner (AdminCap holder) manages the whitelist via add() and remove()

- Seal key servers call seal_approve() which checks the caller is whitelisted

- Encrypted blobs are stored on Walrus — the hash is still committed on-chain for integrity

- Authorized users decrypt via Seal to read the full reasoning

This enables privacy-preserving auditability: the on-chain hash proves the reasoning exists and hasn't been tampered with, while Seal controls who can actually read it.

Architecture

Users deposit SUI into a shared Vault object on Sui mainnet. The vault tracks shares, NAV, and performance fees. Two capability objects control access:

AdminCap (held by vault owner): Can pause/unpause vault, revoke agent, set performance fees, withdraw accrued fees.

AgentCap (held by the AI agent): Has Move-enforced limits — max trade size, max deployment percentage, cooldown via Clock, position concentration check, strategy active check, and vault pause check.

The agent runtime (TypeScript) connects to four external systems:

- DeepBook V3 CLOB for live orderbook data and trade execution

- Groq LLM for AI reasoning

- Walrus for immutable reasoning blob storage

- MemWal for persistent cross-session agent memory

**Trade execution happens atomically via a Programmable Transaction Block (PTB):** withdraw from vault -> swap on DeepBook -> return proceeds to vault -> record trade with Walrus blob ID and reasoning hash on-chain.

Smart Contract Design (Move)

Four modules deployed to Sui mainnet across two packages:

vault.move, Shared Vault with Performance Fees

- Shared object holding SUI deposits from multiple users

- Proportional share-based accounting (deposit receives DepositReceipt NFT)

- NAV per share tracking with high-water mark

- Performance fee (default 10%) charged only on profits above high-water mark

- Emergency withdrawal bypasses pause state

- withdraw_for_deployment / return_from_deployment, package-internal functions only callable by agent_auth

agent_auth.move , Dual-Layer Guardian

- AdminCap: Owned by vault creator. Can pause vault, revoke agent, set fees, withdraw fees.

- AgentCap: Owned by the AI agent address. Has Move-enforced trading limits:

  - max_trade_size: Maximum MIST per trade (checked in withdraw_for_trading)

  - max_deployment_bps: Maximum percentage of vault deployed (checked against vault_total_value)

  - Cooldown enforcement via sui::clock::Clock , the contract computes elapsed time since last_trade_timestamp_ms

  - Position concentration check against StrategyConfig.max_position_bps

  - Strategy active check, vault pause check

- record_trade: Stores Walrus blob ID + SHA-256 reasoning hash on-chain as TradeRecordEvent

- withdraw_for_trading: 7 on-chain checks before releasing funds. Even if the TypeScript guardian is bypassed, Move enforcement blocks the transaction.

strategy.move, On-Chain Risk Parameters

- Shared StrategyConfig object with adjustable parameters

- max_position_bps, stop_loss_bps, min_trade_interval_sec, max_open_positions

- Pool whitelist support

- Owner can update parameters via dashboard, changes take effect on next trade cycle seal_policy/whitelist.move,  Seal Encryption Policy

- Whitelist-based access control for decrypting reasoning blobs on Walrus

- Admin creates whitelist, adds/removes authorized addresses

- seal_approve entry function called by Seal key servers to verify access

- Prefix-matching on blob ID ensures policy applies to correct data

Dual-Layer Guardian System

**The agent has two independent safety layers , both must approve a trade:**

Layer 1: TypeScript Pre-flight (8 checks)

1. Spread < 50 bps

2. Orderbook depth > 100 units

3. Estimated slippage < 100 bps

4. AI confidence >= 30%

5. Vault balance > 0

6. Vault not paused

7. Trade size within limits

8. Cooldown met

Layer 2: Move On-chain Enforcement (7 checks)

1. AgentCap.active == true

2. amount <= AgentCap.max_trade_size

3. deployed + amount <= max_deployment_bps * total_value

4. amount <= StrategyConfig.max_position_bps * total_value

5. Clock.timestamp - last_trade_ms >= min_trade_interval * 1000

6. StrategyConfig.active == true

7. Vault.paused == false

If TypeScript checks are bypassed (compromised agent), the Move contract will still abort the transaction. The budget ceiling is enforced by the blockchain itself.

Trading Flow (End-to-End)

1. Agent reads DeepBook V3 orderbook (mid price, spread, bid/ask depth)

2. Agent reads vault state from chain (balance, deployed, NAV)

3. Agent retrieves memory from MemWal (past decisions, performance)

4. LLM analyzes all data and returns a TradeDecision (action, quantity, confidence, reasoning)

5. TypeScript Guardian runs 8 pre-flight checks

6. If approved, build a Programmable Transaction Block (PTB):

   - withdraw_for_trading (Move checks 1-7, releases SUI from vault)

   - swap_exact_base_for_quote on DeepBook V3 (executes the trade)

   - return_from_trading (returns proceeds to vault, triggers profit/loss accounting)

   - record_trade (commits Walrus blob ID + reasoning hash on-chain)

7. Full reasoning JSON stored on Walrus

8. Memory updated on MemWal

9. Telegram bot notified with decision summary

Dashboard

**A Next.js web dashboard provides:**

Portfolio: Deposit/withdraw SUI, view shares, NAV per share, vault performance.

Reasoning Timeline: Every decision displayed chronologically with expandable details. On-chain trades show Walrus blob content, SHA-256 verification badge, Suiscan TX link, and guardian check results. Filter by action type (BUY/SELL/HOLD).

Admin Settings:

- AI-powered "Smart Setup" chat, describe your risk tolerance in plain English (e.g., "I want conservative trading with small positions"), and the AI translates it into vault configuration parameters

- Three quick presets: Conservative, Moderate, Aggressive

- Create vault with on-chain safety limits (2-step wallet signature flow)

- Adjust strategy parameters live (max position, stop-loss, cooldown, max trades)

- Pause/unpause vault, revoke agent, set performance fee, collect accrued fees

- All changes are on-chain transactions signed by the AdminCap holder

Vault Selector: Support for multiple vaults per user.

Telegram Bot

Real-time notifications for every agent decision:

- Full reasoning text from the LLM

- Confidence bar visualization

- Market condition and risk assessment

- Market snapshot (mid price, spread, bid/ask depth)

- Walrus blob ID link for verification

- Recent decisions summary with timestamps

**Tech Stack**

Blockchain: Sui (mainnet)

Smart Contracts: Move (3 modules, vault, agent_auth, strategy)

Seal Policy: Move (whitelist module for encrypted reasoning access control)

DEX: DeepBook V3 (native CLOB, SUI/USDC pool on mainnet)

Reasoning Storage: Walrus (immutable blob storage, SHA-256 hash on-chain)

Agent Memory: MemWal (Walrus Memory, persistent cross-session memory)

Encryption: Seal (threshold encryption for Walrus, whitelist-based decryption)

AI / LLM: Groq (llama-3.1-8b-instant)

Agent Runtime: TypeScript (Node.js)

Dashboard: Next.js 14, Tailwind CSS, @mysten/dapp-kit

Notifications: Telegram Bot API

Monorepo: pnpm workspaces, shared types package

What Makes SuiSage Different

1. Every decision is permanently auditable, stored on Walrus, hash-verified on-chain. You can't hide a bad trade or fabricate reasoning after the fact.

2. The agent genuinely learns. MemWal gives it persistent memory across sessions. It tracks its own win rate, adapts to market conditions, and references past performance in every decision.

3. Safety is enforced by the blockchain, not just code. Move smart contracts enforce trade size limits, cooldowns, position concentration, and deployment caps. Even a compromised agent binary cannot exceed its budget because the blockchain will reject the transaction.

4. The vault owner retains full control. AdminCap can pause trading, revoke the agent, adjust risk parameters, and set performance fees. All via on-chain transactions with immediate effect.

5. Privacy without sacrificing verifiability. Seal encrypts sensitive reasoning while the on-chain hash still proves integrity. Auditors get access via whitelist. The public sees the hash matches; authorized parties see the full reasoning.

6. AI-assisted vault configuration. The Smart Setup chat translates plain English risk preferences into on-chain vault parameters, making DeFi configuration accessible to non-technical users.

About:

MemSurf is the missing layer that makes agent memory on Walrus observable, verifiable, and shareable.

THE PROBLEM

AI agents are exploding — Gartner reports 60% of organizations are already piloting or scaling them, and 70% of enterprises will deploy agentic AI by 2029. But underneath that wave sits a broken foundation: memory. An agent that stores its memory on Walrus is flying blind. That memory is a write-only black box — siloed per agent, invisible, unverifiable, piling up duplicates and gaps. Storage is already crowded (Pinecone, Chroma, Mem0, Zep), but it only solves store + retrieve. What stays thin is everything around it: seeing what an agent remembers, moving the relevant memory between agents, and proving it's really there.

WHAT MEMSURF DOES

MemSurf is two things in one — a surfboard for agent memory:

1. A DASHBOARD to SEE the memory. Inspect, search, and independently VERIFY every memory your agents hold on Walrus. Verify re-fetches the raw blob from the public Walrus aggregator and proves it exists, is Seal-encrypted, and is content-addressed.

2. TWO AUTONOMOUS AGENTS to RIDE it:

- Curator Agent — continuously scans an agent's memory and keeps it clean: near-duplicates, vague entries, and knowledge gaps. Memory on Walrus is immutable, so it never deletes; it corrects forward.

- Router Agent — watches your agents and moves the RELEVANT knowledge between them. It's relevance-scored, gap-aware (never spams duplicates), anchors every routing decision on Sui via its own Move package, and notifies the receiving agent — which can reject a memory with a reason.

Both chain into one closed autonomous loop, no re-prompting: a research agent records a finding → Router routes it (anchored on Sui) → a trading agent acts and measures a result → Router routes that result back → the research agent learns from the outcome.

WHY IT MATTERS

MemSurf is horizontal: it works for ANY agent on Walrus Memory (MemWal), not one niche vertical. Every developer building agents on Walrus hits the same wall — memory they can't see, verify, or share. MemSurf removes that wall, turning Walrus memory from a black box into a layer developers can trust and adopt. Judges can evaluate it in 2 minutes with no account: a deployed demo runs against real Walrus testnet data.

BUILT ON THE FULL WALRUS STACK

- MemWal — the core agent-memory layer (remember / recall / analyze / rememberBulk)

- Walrus — used directly: Verify reads blobs from the public aggregator; Artifacts writes files via the public publisher

- Seal — every memory is end-to-end encrypted before it reaches Walrus

- Sui — the account is a real MemWalAccount Move object, and MemSurf publishes its OWN Move package (memsurf::routing) that anchors every routing decision on-chain as a tamper-evident event

Made by university student

About:

**TraceLayer: Verifiable Provenance for AI Agent Outputs**

AI agents are now writing code, filing PRs, summarizing documents, and acting on behalf of users. However, this work lacks auditability. Outputs typically live in ephemeral chat logs that can easily be edited, deleted, or contested, making it impossible to prove to a third party that an agent generated a specific result. Traditional centralized logging solutions simply reintroduce the trust issues they were meant to solve.

TraceLayer is a lightweight, decentralized provenance layer that sits alongside any AI agent runner. It ensures that every digital artifact produced by an agent is tamper-proof and allows for verifiable replay in the future.

**How the System Works**

When an AI agent completes a run, TraceLayer automatically executes the following workflow:

- **Local Recording:** Saves the run metadata (such as project name, agent version, and owner address) into a local SQLite proof log.

- **Decentralized Storage on Walrus:** Uploads the generated artifact's bytes to Walrus and receives a content-addressed `blobId`.

- **Double Verification:** Reads the data back from Walrus to recompute and verify its SHA-256 hash, ensuring the upload was perfectly preserved.

- **On-Chain Anchoring on Sui:** Anchors the artifact's core data (run ID, blob ID, hash, and timestamp) as an `ArtifactAnchor` object securely on the Sui blockchain.

Thanks to this mechanism, anyone with a run ID can fetch the proof chain, pull the raw bytes from Walrus, recompute the hash, and verify it against the on-chain Sui anchor. If any of these steps disagree, the output is provably falsified.

**Core Technical Highlights**

- **Non-Custodial Signing:** By default, the on-chain anchoring transaction is signed and submitted directly by the user's Sui wallet in the frontend. TraceLayer servers only look up the result and never touch the user's private keys.

- **Sui Move Smart Contract:** A tight 76-line Move module deployed on the Sui testnet securely manages the anchor objects and includes built-in duplicate-anchor detection to prevent replay issues.

- **Extreme Privacy & Safety:** The system only saves the final generated artifacts. It **never** stores the AI's "chain-of-thought" reasoning, nor does it ever persist the user's model API keys on disk or in the logs.

- **Complete Developer Stack:** The backend is strictly built with TypeScript (Bun + node:http compatible), paired with a Next.js 16 and React 19 frontend. It also provides offline and dry-run modes for local development and CI/CD testing.

About:

**Hunch — the first native prediction market on Sui.**

Sui has no native prediction market. Hunch changes that: a swipe-to-bet prediction-market app, **live on Sui mainnet**, where anyone can take a position on real-world and on-chain outcomes with USDC in two taps.

**Live today on Sui mainnet:**

- **13 markets across 12 types** — token market-cap milestones, price ladders, up/down direction, token-vs-token mcap flips ($WAL flips $SUI), rank races (SUI/APT/NEAR), TVL targets, basket caps, head-to-head event markets, and "when does X cross Y" date windows. A real catalogue for the community on day one — not a single demo market.

- **On-chain parimutuel vault (Move).** Stakes escrow in a `Market<T>` object; payout math is *differentially proven* byte-for-byte against our reference engine (8-case differential + 60-iteration fuzz + conservation-to-zero). Claims are permissionless and never pausable.

- **Verifiable resolution on Walrus.** Every resolution writes a canonical evidence payload to Walrus, and the blob's hash is committed on-chain in the vault. Anyone can re-verify how a market settled — tamper-evident public data, not a trust-me oracle.

- **Connect any Sui wallet** (Slush / Suiet / Sui Wallet), self-funded gas — you sign and execute your own bet. Pyth (Hermes) feeds power price markets.

- **Agent-ready.** A full agent layer — Sui Payment Kit (pay-to-trade) + an MCP server — lets autonomous agents discover, quote, and place bets programmatically.

**Why it matters:** prediction markets are the killer app Sui is missing. Hunch ships the whole stack — money-safe Move settlement, a real market catalogue, verifiable Walrus-backed resolution, and an agent interface — so the Sui community finally has somewhere to bet on what happens next.

**Try it:** [https://www.playhunch.xyz/sui](https://www.playhunch.xyz/sui)
