About:

**HireMe lets clients hire the work of specialized AI Agents through MCP while creators keep the private Harness encrypted, verifiable, and hidden behind a protected execution boundary.**

Demo: [https://youtu.be/pD1bTG_P_E0?si=dQoCHVaKhVzpCPE7](https://youtu.be/pD1bTG_P_E0?si=dQoCHVaKhVzpCPE7)

**Problem**

The better an AI Agent gets, the harder it is to share or sell.

A useful Agent is not just a model call. Its real value is usually hidden in the private Harness around it: prompts, skills, examples, rubrics, tools, memory rules, and workflow habits that make the Agent reliable. If a creator gives the full Harness to a client, the client can copy the know-how. If the creator keeps the Harness private, the client cannot use the Agent where the work actually happens.

That leaves both sides stuck: creators cannot earn from specialized Agent expertise without leaking it, and clients cannot safely hire expert Agents inside tools like Codex, Claude, or other MCP clients. Project memory also gets scattered across sessions and apps, so even useful Agents behave like one-off calls instead of repeatable specialists.

**Solution**

HireMe is protected Agent hiring, not a prompt marketplace.

Clients hire an Agent from HireMe and call it through the `hireme` MCP server from their existing workflow. The client sends a task, receives the Agent's result, and never receives the creator's private prompts, examples, rubrics, skills, or `AGENTS.md` files. Clients hire the result; creators keep the recipe.

Creators publish protected Agents from the HireMe app or the local `hireme-creator` Codex plugin. A protected Agent package can include `AGENTS.md`, `skills/**`, private examples, scoring rubrics, workflow rules, tools, and memory behavior. HireMe archives and encrypts that Harness, stores it as a protected Walrus artifact, and registers only the public metadata needed for discovery and access: the Agent card, public MCP contract, version, protected asset classes, price, and artifact digest.

**How It Works**

- Client task -> Codex MCP call -> HireMe gateway -> protected Agent execution -> safe result

- Creator Harness -> encrypted Walrus artifact -> public Sui metadata -> controlled access

- Client context -> MemWal memory -> second-session recall

The HireMe gateway verifies access, loads the protected Harness only inside the execution boundary, runs the Agent, checks that private Harness content is not returned, records safe usage metadata, and sends the result back to the client.

This is different from a prompt marketplace. A prompt marketplace sells something the client can copy. HireMe sells the work of a protected Agent, so the client gets the output and the creator keeps the operating knowledge.

**Why Walrus And MemWal Matter**

Walrus is not just storage for HireMe. It changes the trust model.

The private Harness becomes a durable, versioned, verifiable artifact that can be referenced without handing plaintext files to the client. A creator can prove which Agent package was published without revealing the package itself. A client can call a registered Agent through MCP without receiving the private Harness. HireMe connects public metadata, artifact digests, and access-policy references to a protected execution flow.

MemWal gives hired Agents continuity across the client's work. Memory belongs to the client context, so the client can carry goals, constraints, decisions, and project context across multiple sessions and multiple hired Agents. The creator's private Harness stays separate; MemWal is the shared client memory layer, not the creator's private workflow.

**Technical Proof**

The judge-facing proof is the full protected loop: creator publish, encrypted Walrus artifact, Sui public metadata, client MCP call, safe Agent result, and MemWal recall in a later session. The key thing to verify is that the client can use the Agent's work while the private Harness files remain hidden.

**What makes HireMe Special**

HireMe addresses a real bottleneck in the emerging Agent economy: useful Agents are valuable because of private operating knowledge, but that same private knowledge makes them hard to distribute, monetize, or trust.

The product loop is concrete: a creator publishes a protected Agent, a client hires it from an MCP workflow, HireMe returns the Agent's work without exposing the private Harness, and MemWal lets the relationship continue across sessions. Walrus is central to that loop because it provides the protected artifact layer, not just a place to upload files.

HireMe makes specialized AI labor portable without making it copyable.

**Future Roadmap**

The current version proves the core loop: protected Agent packaging, client-side MCP use, non-leak result delivery, Walrus-backed artifacts, Sui metadata, and MemWal continuity.

Next, HireMe can strengthen the trust boundary with richer access policies, usage receipts, verifiable execution environments, and stronger protection for creator Harnesses, client inputs, and Agent results.

About:

### **OVERVIEW**

TRACE Protocol is a media intelligence and authenticity network built on Sui, Walrus, and MemWal.

Today, every media file shared online is effectively anonymous. Once it begins spreading across platforms, it becomes difficult to verify its origin, whether it has been altered, how it has evolved, and whether it can be trusted.

TRACE combines cryptographic provenance, collective memory, and autonomous AI agents to create a verifiable trust layer for digital media.

Instead of asking:

"Is this media fake?"

TRACE answers:

"Where did this media come from, how has it evolved, who modified it, and can its authenticity be proven?"

By combining Sui's object model, Walrus' decentralized storage, and MemWal's persistent AI memory, TRACE transforms media verification from a one-time action into a persistent intelligence network.

### **PROBLEM**

The internet has no native trust layer for media.

As AI-generated content, deepfakes, manipulated content, and misinformation continue to grow, it becomes increasingly difficult to determine:

- Who originally created a piece of media

- When it first appeared

- Whether it has been modified

- How it has spread across platforms

- Which version is authentic

Current solutions focus primarily on AI detection, moderation, or content takedowns.

These approaches are reactive and often fail once content has already spread.

Additionally, AI agents today suffer from fragmented memory. They lose context between sessions, cannot share intelligence effectively, and may repeatedly investigate the same content from scratch.

The result is an internet where media lacks provenance, and agents lack memory.

### **SOLUTION**

TRACE creates a permanent, verifiable provenance layer for digital media.

When media is uploaded:

- The media is stored on Walrus.

- Cryptographic and perceptual fingerprints are generated.

- A MediaRecord is created on Sui.

- Provenance metadata is permanently anchored on-chain.

- AI agents continuously monitor and investigate derivatives.

- Browser extension agents sight media on every webpage, and store them in the Collective Memory Bank powered by MemWal.

This allows users, organizations, and AI agents to verify: Origin, Ownership, Integrity, Edit history, Distribution history, Trust score, AI involvement

TRACE does not merely detect manipulated content. TRACE makes authenticity provable.

### **KEY FEATURES**

- Provenance Registry: Every piece of media receives a permanent on-chain identity containing (SHA-256 hash, Perceptual hash, Creator identity, Timestamp, Walrus storage proof, Integrity status, AI generation score, Parent-child relationships).

- Provenance Graph: A visual lineage graph showing how media evolves over time. Tracks:

- Transform type (ORIGINAL, TRIM, CROP, MERGE, TRANSLATE, SUBTITLE, AUDIO_REPLACEMENT, VOICE_CLONE, FACE_SWAP, AI_REMIX, SYNTHETIC_GENERATION).

- Intent Type (JOURNALISM, EDUCATION, ENTERTAINMENT, SATIRE, COMMERCIAL, MISINFORMATION).

- Trust Level (VERIFIED_CREATOR, VERIFIED_ORGANIZATION, COMMUNITY_VERIFIED, UNVERIFIED).

- AI Involvement (NONE, AI_ASSISTED, AI_GENERATED, FULLY_SYNTHETIC).

- Collective Memory Bank: Powered by MemWal.
Stores: First-seen records, Verification history, Derivative discoveries, Trust signals, Spread intelligence, Investigation results.
Unlike traditional systems, TRACE agents never start from zero. Every observation strengthens the network.

- Multi-Agent Intelligence Layer

- Sentinel Agent (Discovers derivatives and manipulated content).

- Verification Agent (Validates provenance and authenticity).

- Spread Analysis Agent (Tracks how media propagates across platforms).

- Source Trust Agent (Builds reputation profiles for creators and publishers).

- Research Agent (Generates misinformation and media intelligence reports).

- Legal Evidence Agent (Produces court-ready certificate).

- Browser Extension: Provides real-time verification while browsing.
Possible results: 🟢 Verified Original, 🟡 Verified Derivative, 🔴 Unverified, 🟣 AI Generated, ⚪ Unknown.
Users can instantly inspect (Origin, Provenance chain, Trust score, Edit history, Distribution history).

- Authenticity Certificates: Creators can generate verifiable certificates proving:

- Ownership

- Timestamp

- Provenance

- Registration status

- Storage proof

- Other features like API, Organization access, and an Explorer.

### **USE CASES**

- Journalism: News organizations can authenticate and verify original footage before publication.

- Deepfake Detection: Users can identify manipulated derivatives and trace them back to original sources.

- Creator Protection: Creators can prove ownership and detect unauthorized modifications.

- Legal Evidence: Generate verifiable chains of custody for courts, investigators, and law enforcement.

- Social media Ai content verification: Social media companies can integrate trace protocol to display Ai watermark and an authenticity certificate as directed by the new EU act. Also, help creators automatically verify their content before it goes online.

### **WHY SUI + WALRUS + MEMWAL**

### **Why Sui?**

Sui's object-centric architecture is ideal for provenance. Every media asset, edit record, and provenance relationship becomes a first-class on-chain object with ownership and history.

Benefits: Object ownership, Immutable provenance, Fast execution, Sponsored transactions, zkLogin onboarding.

**Why Walrus?**

Walrus provides decentralized, verifiable storage for media.

Benefits: Blob certification, Verifiable retrieval, Cost-efficient large-file storage, Permanent media archival.

Every uploaded file receives a cryptographic storage proof.

### **Why MemWal?**

MemWal powers TRACE's Collective Memory Bank.

Benefits: Persistent memory, Cross-agent collaboration, Long-running investigations, Shared intelligence.

With MemWal, the network continuously learns and would never have to start from scratch.

### **PROOF OF DEPLOYMENT AND TRANSACTIONS**

**Sui Smart Contracts - testnet**
All contracts are modules within a single deployed package:
**Package Address:** `0xf1acdf7d36c4816d91ebe39f0887f163155a08bb0d435e7ea8f737b981637bdb`

### **PROGRESS DURING THE HACKATHON**

During the hackathon, we successfully:

- Designed the TRACE Protocol architecture

- Built the MediaRecord smart contract on Sui

- Integrated Walrus for decentralized media storage

- Implemented provenance registration workflows

- Built the Provenance Graph visualization

- Integrated MemWal for persistent agent memory

- Developed the browser extension prototype

- Created the Verification API

- Implemented autonomous agent workflows

- Designed the Collective Memory Bank architecture

- Created a demo video

- Wrote the documentation

**

CHALLENGES WE RAN INTO**

- Persistent Agent Memory: Creating a shared memory layer that multiple agents could continuously update and retrieve from required careful integration with MemWal.

- Media Similarity Detection: Detecting derivatives without relying solely on exact hashes required combining cryptographic fingerprints with perceptual similarity techniques.

- Balancing Transparency and Privacy: Some provenance information should be public while other metadata may require privacy-preserving controls. This led us to explore Seal integration for future encrypted workflows.

### **FUTURE PLANS**

- Mainnet deployment: Deploy all contracts mainnet

- Advanced Deepfake Detection: Integrate multimodal AI analysis for audio, image, and video manipulation detection.

- Organization Verification: Support delegated trust networks for newsrooms, governments, and enterprises.

- Expanding beyond browser extension: Enable users to contribute media sightings and enrich the Collective Memory Bank from mobile and messaging environments through native iOS and Android applications, Mobile keyboard integrations, Share Sheet integrations, TRACE SDK for third-party developers, WhatsApp and Telegram bots, Additional social and messaging platform integrations

- Trust Reputation Layer: Develop dynamic reputation scoring for creators, organizations, and publishers.

- Community-Powered Truth Mining: Launch the TRACE browser network where users contribute sightings and earn rewards for strengthening the Collective Memory Bank.

- TRACE Token Ecosystem: the $TRACE token will incentivize the ecosystem of browser extension installers and the broader community (Truth Mining rewards, Governance, Protocol services, Staking, and reputation systems.

**TECH STACK**

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/fc69ed40-e95d-430f-8d55-238632d30636/948b7d3a-af87-4f1a-b4da-303fb55abb44.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/fc69ed40-e95d-430f-8d55-238632d30636/77ac841d-7406-4c86-b5ab-cae2095225d3.png

About:

Callisto is a verifiable, local-first memory infrastructure for AI agents that brings Git-like guarantees to agent memory. It transforms every saved insight into cryptographically provable, tamper-evident knowledge anchored on Sui and Walrus, while making that knowledge recoverable from the blockchain even after complete local data loss.

Modern AI agents have become increasingly capable, autonomous, and intelligent, but memory remains one of the weakest layers in the stack. Most existing memory systems rely on vector databases and transient context windows that cannot prove the authenticity of stored information. These systems depend on centralized providers that cannot be independently audited, making them vulnerable to memory poisoning, silent data deletion, and loss of critical knowledge. If a device is lost or corrupted, the accumulated intelligence and context are often unrecoverable. Organizations investing heavily in AI systems also lack a reliable way to demonstrate that token expenditure resulted in measurable, verifiable work.

Callisto addresses these limitations by treating memory as financial-grade infrastructure. Every memory stored by an agent becomes a signed, encrypted, and content-addressed record that can later be verified on any machine. The system enables complete reconstruction of an agent's knowledge base from blockchain-backed storage even when the original local environment no longer exists.

At the core of the architecture, memories are signed using Ed25519, encrypted using AES-256-GCM, and content-addressed through SHA-256 hashing. Data is written immediately to a local SQLite database, providing zero-latency operation and full offline support. Rather than storing every memory directly on-chain, Callisto periodically creates checkpoints by anchoring a single Merkle root that represents an entire batch of memories. This approach preserves cryptographic verifiability for individual memories while dramatically reducing blockchain storage and transaction costs. Any memory that has been tampered with or maliciously injected is mathematically detected and excluded from an agent's active context, preventing poisoned data from being silently loaded. Even after total local loss, the system can reconstruct an entire session from Walrus storage, functioning as a Git clone mechanism for agent cognition.

The broader Callisto ecosystem is built around a shared memory engine that provides instant, private, and signed memory storage. Semantic recall capabilities allow agents to retrieve memories by meaning rather than simple keyword matching. This functionality is powered by on-device embeddings using all-MiniLM-L6-v2 through Transformers.js, operating completely offline and without ongoing API costs. A lexical fallback ensures that recall remains available even when semantic retrieval is unavailable.

Callisto's recovery system enables the reconstruction of wiped sessions from a single Walrus blob. During recovery, the system retrieves a checkpoint, rebuilds encrypted memory envelopes into local SQLite storage, and decrypts them using locally held keys. As a result, knowledge survives beyond any individual device and can be restored whenever required.

Another major component is the Verifiable Productivity Ledger, which records the actual work performed by AI systems. The ledger captures token usage, language model calls, files modified, commits created, tests passed, and other measurable outputs. A SHA-256 hash of the canonical productivity record is anchored on Walrus and Sui, allowing organizations to prove that a specific token expenditure produced specific verifiable work. Any modification to a recorded metric invalidates verification, providing strong guarantees against manipulation.

Callisto integrates with the broader AI ecosystem through a harness-agnostic MCP server. Clients such as Claude Code, Goose, Cursor, and other MCP-compatible tools can access verifiable memory without requiring custom forks or modifications. The MCP layer exposes tools for memory saving, recall, restoration, listing, checkpointing, verification, deletion, recovery, and productivity recording.

A live web console provides visibility into the entire memory system through server-sent events. The interface includes dedicated views for dashboards, memories, recall operations, active context, recovery workflows, productivity records, transactions, usage analytics, audit logs, and API key management. Because the console is built directly on top of the same core engine used by the CLI, its behavior and security guarantees remain consistent with the underlying infrastructure.

The command-line interface offers direct access to all core capabilities, including memory management, checkpoint creation, verification, recovery, productivity tracking, and on-chain delegation workflows. This allows developers and agents to interact with the system entirely from local environments while maintaining cryptographic integrity and blockchain-backed durability.

Checkpoint verification is performed by anchoring Merkle roots as Walrus blobs and Sui Blob objects. During verification, Callisto retrieves the anchored blob, reconstructs the Merkle root, and validates every cryptographic link in the chain, including signatures, content hashes, Merkle proofs, and blockchain anchors. Verification succeeds only when the entire chain of evidence remains intact.

Recovery functionality has also been validated through independent testing. A fully wiped session was successfully reconstructed end-to-end from a Walrus blob without exposing any plaintext content in storage. Similarly, productivity records have been anchored and verified on both Walrus and Sui, with verification failing immediately whenever any recorded value is altered.

The security model is designed around privacy by default and provability on demand. No plaintext data is stored on-chain. Blockchain records reveal only hashes, timestamps, and cryptographic commitments, while memory content is stored as randomized ciphertext so that identical plaintext never produces identical encrypted output.

The platform is built using the Sui and Walrus testnets together with the @mysten/sui and @mysten/walrus SDKs. Smart contracts are written in Move through the memwal_acl::acl package. The core platform is implemented in TypeScript using ECMAScript modules, Node.js, and SQLite operating in WAL mode with encryption. Cryptographic primitives include Ed25519 signatures, AES-256-GCM encryption, HKDF key derivation, SHA-256 hashing, and Merkle trees. AI integration is delivered through the Model Context Protocol SDK and Transformers.js, while developer tooling includes Commander CLI, Vitest, and dependency-free Node.js HTTP and Server-Sent Events infrastructure.

About:

***Arktion** is an ownership layer for the $1.26B web-novel and webcomic market — a creator-owned platform for manga, manhwa, manhua, web novels, and fanfiction, built on Sui.

The problem it solves is ownership: creators in this space routinely lose years of audience-building overnight when a Patreon or Ko-fi account is banned, and payment rails work poorly in the regions where most of this community lives. Arktion replaces the platform-as-landlord model with smart contracts — the creator becomes the owner, enforced by Sui rather than a terms-of-service promise.

Highlights:

- **Invisible wallets** — Google sign-in via zkLogin creates a real Sui wallet with no seed phrase or extension; the backend sponsors gas, so users never touch crypto mechanics.

- **Direct, gasless payments** — USDC tips go wallet-to-wallet with no platform cut and ~400ms confirmation; readers supply their own USDC and never need to hold SUI.

- **ArktionPassport** — a soul-bound (non-transferable at the Move type level) NFT that is the reader's portable on-chain identity, accumulating level, lifetime INK, chapters read, and badges. Its image is a live SVG that updates with the reader's progress, and stats can be anchored on-chain via an admin-attested, user-signed transaction.

- **INK token** — earned only by reading, never purchasable, giving the community real governance weight without letting anyone buy in.

- **Community-curated catalog** — readers propose new series and vote with INK-weighted DAO governance; approvals reward the submitter and mint a Contributor badge atomically.

- **Permanent storage** — chapters, badge metadata, and reading-history snapshots live on Walrus, content-addressed and durable beyond Arktion's own servers.

Built on Sui. Owned by creators.

About:

**MemZap gives your Slack workspace a memory that travels with you, into Claude and beyond.**

Anything you tell MemZap in Slack becomes portable, verifiable memory that your AI assistants can recall later. Tag the bot with something worth remembering, and it's captured the moment you send it — no copying context between tools, no re-explaining yourself in every new conversation.

**How it works**

Mention the bot in any channel with what you want to keep:

`@memzap remember the launch is targeting June 2026`

MemZap captures the message along with its origin — the channel, the author, and a link back to the exact message, and writes it to your encrypted memory space. A ✓ reaction confirms it's stored.

From then on, any connected AI assistant can recall it. Ask Claude "what launch date did I save?" and it answers from the memory you wrote in Slack — because both apps read from the same memory you own, not from each other.

**What you can do with it**

- Capture decisions, dates, and context as they happen in Slack, without leaving the conversation

- Recall that context later in Claude, Cursor, or any MCP-aware assistant — no manual hand-off

- Keep a shared team memory that every connected tool can read

- See exactly where each memory came from, with a link back to its original Slack message

- Stay in control: memories are encrypted, owned by your keys, and portable across tools

**Built on owned, verifiable infrastructure**

MemZap stores memories on Walrus, the decentralized storage network from the team behind Sui. Every memory is Seal-encrypted before it's stored, with ownership and access enforced on-chain, so your memory is private, durable, and provably yours, never locked inside a single vendor's database.

**Getting started**

Install MemZap, invite the bot to a channel, and tag it with anything you want to remember. Connect Claude (or any MCP client) to the same memory space to recall it anywhere.

About:

**WalrusStreamKit — ownable, monetizable video infrastructure on Walrus + Sui.**

A developer-first video SDK — think "Mux/Agora for Web3" — that turns video into an on-chain asset the creator owns, prices, and gets paid for directly. One install does upload → store → deliver → own → monetize → gate access, with no vendor lock-in.

**The problem:**

Web2 video (Mux, Cloudflare, Agora) gives great delivery but total lock-in: ownership, paywall, and payments are proprietary. Web3 gives decentralized *pieces* (storage, transcode) but no glue — no control plane, no SDK. Nobody unifies production-grade delivery with on-chain ownership, payments, and access control.

**Our solution — Walrus as the data plane, Sui as the control plane:**

- **Walrus** stores the bytes. HLS segments are packed as **Quilt** blobs (≈106–420× cheaper per segment, ~660 patches/quilt, byte-range readable), served through a cached, non-custodial relayer. Storage costs ≈ AWS S3 (~$0.28/raw-GiB/yr). Immutable blobs + an on-chain Merkle integrity root give verifiable provenance.

- **Sui (Move)** holds the rarely-changing, high-value state: `Channel`, `VideoAsset`, `AccessPolicy`, `AccessGrant`, `Subscription`. It is **never queried per segment** — only when a right is bought or verified once at playback start.

- **Seal (threshold IBE)** gates paid content: each segment is AES-encrypted and the key is released only after the chain confirms the viewer's `AccessGrant` via an on-chain `seal_approve` dry-run.

**What it does:**

- public VOD

- paid/encrypted VOD (Seal)

- pseudo-live (~6–12s) that finalizes into a durable on-chain VOD reusing the same Walrus blobs — no re-upload.

- The creator — not the platform — owns the asset and is paid directly to their wallet.

**Shipped:**

- 3 npm packages: [https://www.npmjs.com/settings/walrus-streamkit/packages](https://www.npmjs.com/settings/walrus-streamkit/packages)

- Dockerized hosted relayer: [walrus-streamkit-relayer.up.railway.app](http://walrus-streamkit-relayer.up.railway.app)

- Move contracts live on Sui testnet: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xa66a311c2a1fdfa4bb2debbdd86d9f32f851f5888bce28aebe8beb248993b21d/tx-blocks](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xa66a311c2a1fdfa4bb2debbdd86d9f32f851f5888bce28aebe8beb248993b21d/tx-blocks)

- Walrus streamkit landing page: [https://walrus-streamkit.up.railway.app](https://walrus-streamkit.up.railway.app/)

- Examples that uploads, sells, and Seal-streams an encrypted video end-to-end: [https://walrus-streamkit.up.railway.app/app](https://walrus-streamkit.up.railway.app/app)

- Walrus streamkit docs: [https://walrus-streamkit-docs.up.railway.app/docs](https://walrus-streamkit-docs.up.railway.app/docs)

- github: https://github.com/agadape/SUI_FairDrop
- website: https://sui-fairdrop.vercel.app/

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/f798a27e-c4eb-4231-bc81-baf845075292/f4275184-a5df-4967-83ca-2179b40ebe4c.png

About:

The product is a primitive: a recoverable, on-chain-policy-gated secret that no server can read. Encrypt a secret on-chain today and one lost device means it is gone forever, because no server is allowed to hold the key. We removed that rule and prove it across two live policies on Sui.

The first is FairDrop, a sealed-bid fair-launch protocol built on Sui. Users register with their Google account through zkLogin, then submit a blind bid by committing only a hash (`sha3_256(amount || nonce)`) on-chain. The actual bid amount remains hidden until the reveal phase. Once bidding ends, the auction clears at the lowest price that fully satisfies the available supply. If multiple bids tie at the margin, the winner is selected using `sui::random`, leveraging Sui’s validator DKG randomness at `0x8` for a fully on-chain, auditable, and unbiased result. Winners claim their allocation, losers reclaim their funds, and creators withdraw proceeds through on-chain transactions. No administrator can seize funds or block withdrawals.

FairDrop’s strongest differentiator is recoverability. The nonce required to reveal a bid is threshold-encrypted using Seal and stored as a Walrus blob, with the blob ID referenced directly inside the on-chain Commitment object. If a user loses their device, clears local storage, or returns from a fresh browser, the bid can still be recovered. Walrus serves the encrypted blob, while an on-chain `fairdrop::seal_policy::seal_approve` policy authorizes decryption exclusively to the owner of the Entry. Through zkLogin, the same wallet can be deterministically re-derived from the same Google account. At no point does a server, administrator, or the FairDrop team gain access to the secret.

The key insight behind FairDrop is that the auction itself is only one application of a broader primitive: a recoverable, on-chain-policy-gated, custodian-blind secret. The auction policy simply states, “the Entry owner may decrypt during the reveal window.” By replacing that policy, the exact same architecture—Seal, Walrus, zkLogin, and on-chain authorization—can power entirely different applications such as sealed voting systems, confidential RFP processes, or decentralized time-lock mechanisms. Rather than presenting this as a theoretical platform, we demonstrate the primitive in production code and validate its generality with a second working application.

That second application is Umbra, a confidential and MEV-resistant settlement layer built on the same commit-reveal, Seal, Walrus, and `sui::random` engine. On traditional DEXs, orders become public as soon as they enter the mempool, allowing sophisticated bots to front-run or sandwich traders. Umbra prevents this by keeping orders hidden behind a 32-byte commitment hash until the reveal phase. Once the commit window closes, all orders are batch-settled at a single fair clearing price and executed in randomized order using `sui::random`, preventing selective reordering and front-running. The order nonce is encrypted and recoverable through Seal and Walrus exactly like a FairDrop bid, providing the same recovery guarantees under a different policy. The Umbra demo showcases a documented sandwich attack from existing Sui DEX activity alongside a live on-chain Umbra order, allowing users to directly observe the protection rather than simply trust a claim. Like FairDrop, Umbra operates without a backend, privileged operator, or admin key.

Together, FairDrop and Umbra demonstrate a broader thesis: FairDrop serves as the user-facing product that solves a real problem and earns trust through utility, while Umbra proves that the underlying primitive is reusable across entirely different domains. Both applications are powered by the same engine, governed entirely on-chain, and built using Sui-native primitives as intended. zkLogin provides walletless onboarding and raises Sybil costs through auction-specific nullifiers, without claiming proof-of-personhood. Commit-reveal ensures bids and orders remain hidden until the appropriate reveal phase, eliminating last-second sniping and reducing MEV exposure through temporary blinding. `sui::random` provides verifiable and bias-resistant randomness for winner selection in FairDrop and execution ordering in Umbra. Supporting these systems are Walrus for encrypted blob storage, Seal for threshold encryption and policy-based access control, and zkLogin via Enoki for seamless authentication. The entire stack runs without centralized infrastructure: all state lives on-chain, cryptographic proofs are generated client-side, FairDrop’s Move contract stays under 500 lines with 25/25 tests passing, Umbra adds another 25/25, and both are deployed on Sui testnet.

About:

The purpose of ContextMeM is scrape any web content into durable, recallable agent memory on Walrus.

Problem

AI agents need web content as context include in docs, sites, pages but the way they get it today is broken:

`• Scraped context is throwaway. An agent fetches a page, uses it once, and it's` `gone when the session ends. Nothing is saved, nothing is reusable.`

`• The same pages get re-scraped forever. Every agent, every run, re-fetches and` `re-cleans the same content from scratch so is slow, wasteful, and inconsistent.`

`• Raw web is noise. Nav bars, ads, llms.txt boilerplate and messy HTML bury the` `actual signal an agent needs.`

`• No shared memory. There's no common store where one agent's scraped knowledge becomes available to the next agent or to the same agent tomorrow.`

`• Walrus content is hard to consume. Sites deployed on Walrus + Sui can't easily` `be turned into clean, structured context an agent can read.`

THE IDEA BEHIND CONTEXTMEM (aka MEMWA  for when you're feeling contextful )

ContextMeM is a Walrus-native web context engine: point it at any URL, Walrus site, or Sui object, and it turns the content into clean, agent-ready context then stores that context permanently on Walrus so any agent can recall it later, across sessions and across tools. Think of it as long-term web memory for agents, living on decentralized storage.

HOW IT WORKS (Resolve → Package → Remember)

`• Resolve: takes any target, a normal web URL, a wal.app site, or a Suiobject ID, and finds the real content behind it.`

`• Package: scrapes and cleans it into an agent-ready bundle: markdown, llms.txt,a manifest, design tokens, screenshots, and a knowledge graph. The noise isstripped; only the signal stays.`

`• Remember: stores the bundle's bytes on Walrus and writes asemantic pointer into Walrus Memory. The pointer is the lightweight index; the real content lives on Walrus, content-addressed so recall returns exactly whatwas saved.`

=>  Web content becomes persistent, shareable memory instead of disposable scrape output.

About:

Project Name

  • Content Passport

  One-line Summary

  • A blockchain-driven Web2.5 identity, media forensics, and co-creation royalty distribution ecosystem for creators and autonomous AI agents.

  Problem Statement

  • The rise of generative AI has escalated unauthorized media manipulation, deepfakes, and copyright attribution disputes.

  • Creators lack decentralized tools to prove original ownership and maintain verifiable media provenance.

  • Complex crypto wallets and gas-fee transactions limit Web3 adoption for mainstream creators.

  Core Features (Four Pillars)

  • Identity Gate: Built on pure, Enoki-free zkLogin via Google OIDC and custom HKDF salt services. Offers zero-friction onboarding with fully sponsored gas fees

  ($0 gas user experience).

  • Authenticity Lab: Combines Error Level Analysis (ELA), EXIF metadata verification, and Google Gemini & Vertex AI Vision models to cross-audit synthetic neural

  artifacts and image manipulation.

  • Sealed Vault: Secures raw masterpieces via Shamir's Secret Sharing (3-of-5 threshold encryption) and stores encrypted files as sharded, decentralized blobs in

  Walrus storage.

  • Automated Royalties: Integrates Sui Move smart contracts ( co_creation_policy.move ) to coordinate proportional, real-time, and atomic royalty splits for co-

  created and remixed works in a single transaction block.

  Tech Stack

  • Blockchain: Sui Network (Sui Move, zkLogin, Programmable Transaction Blocks)

  • Storage: Walrus Protocol (MemWal Memory Ledger)

  • AI & Forensic: Google Gemini & Vertex AI SDK, sharp, exifr

  • Language & Framework: TypeScript, React, Express, Node.js

  Impact & Vision

  • Protects creative intellectual property against AI scraping and deepfake manipulation.

  • Automates and secures complex multi-party revenue splits via immutable on-chain ledgers.

  • Bridge the gap between Web2.0 convenience and Web3.0 decentralization using social logins.

About:

Receipter is like a receipt and reputation system for AI agents. When an AI agent gets paid to do a job, Receipter records what the job was, what information the agent was allowed to see, what evidence it used, whether the work passed verification, and where the proof is stored. This matters because as AI agents start doing real work for

 people, we need a way to know which agents are actually trustworthy. Instead of relying on fake reviews, screenshots, or a score inside one app, anyone can open the proof, check the receipt, and see if the agent really did the work.

About:

**WHAT IT IS**

Hibi is a chat-first family memory agent. Parents simply send

photos and care logs through chat, and Hibi turns them into a

private, long-term memory archive that can be searched,

revisited, and verified later.

No manual sorting. The first smile, a midnight bottle, a photo

from a walk, a sentence a child says once — Hibi keeps them.

**THE PROBLEM**

Family memories are scattered across camera rolls, chat apps,

baby trackers, and old phones. The most important moments get

buried in thousands of ordinary files, and busy families never

find the time to organize them.

**HOW IT WORKS**

1. Send a photo or note in chat.

2. Hibi understands it as a memory, care log, or album request.

3. It remembers context with MemWal (long-term AI memory).

4. It stores photos & albums on Walrus.

5. It records verifiable hashes & pointers on Sui.

6. The family gets a clean web view of albums, logs, and proofs.

Later, just ask: "What did she start doing recently?"

Hibi returns the memories that would otherwise disappear.

**PRIVACY BY DESIGN**

The public website runs on safe sample data to show the

experience. The real API is local-first — photos, care logs,

and family data stay fully under the family's own control.

**TECH STACK**

- OpenClaw plugin for chat-based intake

- Hibi API (intake, recall, album generation)

- MemWal : long-term AI memory

- Walrus : decentralized photo & album storage (testnet)

- Sui : FamilyVault, Member SBT, verifiable proofs (testnet)

- SQLite : local-first persistence

**WHY IT MATTERS**

Family memory should be portable, verifiable, and owned by the

family. Hibi makes preserving it as easy as sending a message.

Chat in -> memories preserved -> beautiful family view out

-> verifiable archive underneath.

About:

**AgentRelay** is a secure, decentralized storage and semantic search layer designed to eliminate data amnesia in autonomous AI agents.

**The Problem:**
When building custom AI agents using frameworks such as LangChain, CrewAI, AutoGen, or Eliza, critical state data including conversation histories, tool outputs, and execution contexts is stored in temporary memory (RAM). When an agent's script restarts or a server goes offline, this cognitive context is permanently lost. To prevent this, developers traditionally have to provision and secure centralized databases or host third-party vector storage servers.

**The Solution:**
AgentRelay provides a direct storage bridge to the Walrus decentralized storage network and implements **Client-Side Decentralized Vector Indexing** to enable local, low-latency semantic search over agent histories.

### **How It Works:**

- **Client-Side Embeddings:** The SDK converts plain text memories into high-dimensional vector embeddings locally using lightweight APIs (e.g., OpenAI or Hugging Face).

- **Sharded Storage on Walrus:** Instead of uploading a single, heavy JSON file, AgentRelay splits the memory history into small, encrypted chunks (e.g., 20 messages per chunk) and uploads them as individual blobs to the Walrus network.

- **Index Mapping:** A lightweight mapping file is generated to track each encrypted shard's Walrus Blob ID and its mathematical vector bounds.

- **On-Demand Retrieval:** When querying memory, the agent only downloads the tiny index map, performs local cosine similarity searches to identify the most relevant shard, and retrieves *only* that specific encrypted chunk from Walrus ,saving bandwidth, cost, and context tokens.

### **Key Benefits:**

- **For Developers:** Seamlessly add long-term semantic memory to agents with a few lines of code. No need to manage centralized databases, write database adapters, or pay for third-party vector databases.

- **For Users:** Complete control over personal conversation logs and agent states on a decentralized Web3 network, keeping cognitive histories secure, private, and fully self-owned.

About:

### **WalSkills : Walrus Skills Marketplace**

WalSkills is a developer marketplace built for the Walrus and Sui ecosystem.

Our goal is to help developers learn, build, and ship projects on Walrus faster using ready-to-use resources.

### **What is WalSkills?**

WalSkills provides:

- Developer skills and guides

- Project templates

- Knowledge base articles

- Curated learning collections

### Features :

### Skills

Ready-to-use guides that help developers complete specific tasks, such as:

- Deploying a Walrus Site

- Setting up a local portal

- Configuring resources

- Connecting sites to custom URLs

### Templates

Starter projects and implementation examples that help developers build faster.

### Knowledge Base

Easy-to-follow documentation covering:

- Walrus CLI setup

- Blob storage management

- Quilt integration

- Site deployment and configuration

### Curated Collections

Handpicked learning paths for developers, including:

- Getting started with Walrus

- Infrastructure setup

- Building full-stack applications on Walrus and Sui

### Why WalSkills?

Many Walrus workflows require repeated setup and configuration. WalSkills simplifies these processes by providing reusable resources that help developers:

- Learn faster

- Build more efficiently

- Follow best practices

- Improve AI-assisted development workflows

### Developer Experience

With WalSkills, developers can:

- Browse skills, templates, and guides

- Follow curated learning paths

- Copy commands and download resources

Media:
- None listed

About:

MemForks — git for AI agent memory

A versioned commit graph: branches, forks, merges, and time-travel for everything your agents learn.

**INTRO**

MemForks is the version-control layer for AI agent memory. It sits on top of the Walrus memory stack and gives agents what Git gave developers: a content-addressed commit graph with branches, forks, governed merges, and a verifiable history. Memory is stored encrypted on Walrus, versioned in an immutable commit graph, and settled on Sui, so what an agent learns is durable, portable, governable, and auditable by default.

**PROBLEM**

Remembering is the easy part now. Plenty of good tools persist what an agent learns across sessions. What none of them give you is version control over that memory, and without it, persistence quietly starts working against you. A flat, linear append-log breaks down the moment agents do real work:

- **No isolation**. An agent can't explore a risky hypothesis without polluting the good context. Rolling back means losing everything since the last good state.

- **No parallel exploration**. Running competing strategies means cloned memory blobs nobody can merge back.

- **No collaboration semantics**. Two agents writing to shared memory is last-write-wins. No merge protocol, no conflict resolution, no provenance.

- **No auditability**. When an agent reaches a conclusion, the trail of why (including the alternatives it considered and rejected) is gone with the session.

Git solved exactly these problems for code. Agent memory is still at the pre-git stage.

**SOLUTION**:

MemForks brings version control and governance to agent memory through three primitives:

- **Forkable memory trees**. MemoryTree is a Sui Move object; branches are named pointers into a content-addressed commit graph. Commits are encrypted blobs on Walrus; forking is a pointer write, free and instant.

- **Composable merge resolvers**. Merges follow typed, on-chain policies, not one model's vibe check: JuryReconcile (k-of-n) with signed attestations enforced by the Move contract, plus LlmReconcile, LastWriteWins, Union, and combinators (Sequence, And).

- **Branch-scoped delegates**. Capability objects that say "this agent may write to branch X, fork from Y, but cannot merge into main", self-enforcing through Move preconditions

**WHAT SHIPS:**

- Move contracts ( deployed on Sui mainnet. Branch creation, merge proposals, k-of-n attestation collection, and finalization are all enforced on-chain.

- Four published npm packages: @memfork/core (TypeScript SDK), @memfork/cli (the memfork binary), @memfork/vercel-ai (Vercel AI SDK middleware), and @memfork/langgraph (LangGraph checkpointer).

- Coding-tool plugins: one-line setup for Cursor and Codex via "memfork install cursor" / "memfork install codex", wiring the memory MCP and the MemForks rule into each tool.

- Off-chain services: a resolver daemon (jury / LLM reconciliation) and a gas-sponsorship service so end users never touch gas.

- Reference apps: memforks-chat (branch-aware chat with on-chain forking and memory diff), memforks-research (multi-agent LangGraph pipeline with compounding memory), and a live DAG visualizer that streams from Sui events.

Protocol spec v0.1.1 plus 201 passing CLI tests (config, install, E2E, provisioning, history, artifacts).

The result: agents explore in parallel, merge with verifiable governance, and produce a cryptographically auditable trail of how every conclusion was reached, including the paths that lost. A log remembers what you chose. MemForks remembers what you rejected, and why.

About:

A collaborative birthday-card builder. One person spins up a shared plan on Sui, sends a single invite link, and everyone signs their text wishes onto the same on-chain object. When the creator is ready, they pay 0.01 SUI — our backend verifies the transaction on-chain, collects all wishes, and sends them to FLUX.1 (via [Pollinations.ai](http://Pollinations.ai)) to generate a unique AI birthday card. The image is pinned to Walrus for permanent decentralized storage, and the sealed card is addressed to the recipient on-chain forever.

Built with Next.js 14, Move 2024 smart contracts (create_plan, add_wish, finalize_card), @mysten/sui dapp-kit, Walrus blob storage, and deployed on Google Cloud Run. No API keys. No infrastructure cost. Open source under MIT.

Live at [cardjoybday.com](http://cardjoybday.com) — because meaningful moments deserve permanent infrastructure.

About:

CarbonProof AI is a verifiable evidence platform for carbon and environmental impact credits. The project helps carbon project owners upload proof such as audit reports, drone images, GPS metadata, sensor data, survival-rate reports, and auditor attestations. Each evidence file is stored on Walrus, while its metadata, hash, and proof reference are anchored on Sui to create a transparent and tamper-resistant evidence trail.

The platform then uses AI to analyze the submitted evidence and generate a structured verification report containing a risk score, confidence score, verified evidence, missing evidence, inconsistencies, and recommendations. This helps buyers, auditors, and stakeholders evaluate whether a carbon or impact project has credible supporting proof before credits are issued or traded.

Our demo focuses on a mangrove restoration project in the Bekasi Coastal Area, where the project owner claims 10,000 mangrove trees planted. Users can create the project, upload evidence, receive Walrus Blob IDs and SHA-256 hashes, run AI verification, view Sui transaction proofs, and share a public verification page. Verified projects can also be displayed in a marketplace simulation as DeepBook-ready impact credits.

CarbonProof AI combines Walrus for decentralized evidence storage, Sui Move smart contracts for proof registry and impact credit objects, AI for risk analysis, and a marketplace-ready layer for future carbon credit liquidity.

About:

AnchorProof is an enterprise cryptographic auditing platform that verifies every AI conversation and makes it tamper-proof on the Sui blockchain.

Enterprises face a liability crisis with AI. Air Canada lost in court because they could not prove what their AI said. The EU AI Act imposes fines up to 35 million euros or 7 percent of global revenue. Without cryptographic proof, organizations cannot defend themselves.

AnchorProof solves this by providing complete visibility into every AI conversation, tamper-proof cryptographic verification using Sui, immutable audit logs with on-chain proofs, cryptographically verified compliance reports, and an enterprise SDK for easy integration.

Every conversation is encrypted using SEAL, stored immutably on Walrus, and verified on the Sui blockchain, creating permanent evidence that cannot be tampered with.

The platform features Sui zkLogin for Google SSO authentication, multi-tenant architecture for enterprise isolation, real-time verification popups, an enterprise dashboard with encrypted conversations, immutable audit logs, and compliance reports with QR codes.

The Agentic Web dashboard enables autonomous AI-powered verification. An intelligent agent analyzes unverified conversations and decides to verify, delete, or hold for review, providing reasoning with confidence, risk, and compliance scores. A public verification page allows anyone to verify conversation integrity using Walrus Blob ID, Sui Transaction Hash, and AnchorProof Hash without logging in.

Interactive demos are available for Banking, Healthcare, Insurance, and Government use cases, allowing users to experience the platform's verification capabilities in industry-specific contexts.

Built with Next.js 16, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS, Sui zkLogin, Walrus Protocol, SEAL Encryption, Sui Move smart contracts, Groq API, Prisma ORM, Supabase, and deployed on Vercel.

About:

## 💡 The Problem

Career credentials and professional histories are currently fragmented, unverified, and prone to loss. Existing solutions are either closed walled-gardens (like Credly) or require massive UX hurdles (wallets, seed phrases) that deter mainstream adoption. More importantly, there is no standardized way to guarantee **artifact provenance**—proving exactly who issued a credential, when, and ensuring the data can be reliably retrieved and verified by any third party without relying on the original issuer's servers.

## 🚀 Our Solution: SuID (Immutable Proof Archive)

**SuID** is a decentralized, immutable proof archive for career credentials. Rather than simply uploading unverified PDFs to Web2 platforms, SuID leverages **Sui Objects** and concepts from **Walrus** to securely store, verify, and replay artifact provenance.

By utilizing **Sui zkLogin**, users can onboard in seconds using their existing Google accounts—abstracting away the complexities of Web3, offering a seamless UX while guaranteeing absolute cryptographic authenticity and data reusability.

## ✨ Key Features

- **Immutable Artifact Provenance**: Credentials are not just text; they are immutable state changes recorded as Move Objects on Sui. This ensures permanent, tamper-proof storage of professional milestones.

- **Frictionless zkLogin**: Users authenticate with Google. No wallet extensions, no seed phrases. The onboarding is instant.

- **Verifiable Data Reusability**: Third parties (employers, audit systems) can independently query the Sui Testnet to restore, verify, and reuse the credential data at any time, eliminating the need for manual background checks.

- **Premium Resume Export**: Users can compile their collected on-chain proof archives into a beautifully formatted, print-ready PDF resume.

## 🛠 How It's Built

- **Smart Contracts (Sui Move)**: We developed a custom `credential` Move package that handles the secure issuance and ownership transfer of credential objects.

- **Frontend (Next.js & Tailwind CSS)**: A sleek, modern web application that interacts directly with the Sui network.

- **Sui TS SDK & RPC**: Used for querying on-chain data `suix_getOwnedObjects`, `suix_getObject`) and executing Programmable Transaction Blocks (PTBs).

SuID bridges the gap between traditional career management and Web3 verifiability, acting as the ultimate proof archive for human professional identity on Sui.

About:

JellyNet is a multi-provider AI chat app where your memory and conversations are encrypted on your own device and stored on Walrus — so you own them, and no provider (including us) can read them.

Sign in with Google and zkLogin silently creates an invisible Sui wallet: no extension, no seed phrase, no gas. Chat across 40+ AI providers from one interface, switch models mid-conversation, and your encrypted memory follows you to any device. The same memory is reachable from any MCP host (like Claude) through our MCP connector.

Underneath is a true two-sided marketplace: suppliers contribute idle API capacity and earn from it; buyers reach every provider through one key. *Bring your keys, use them yourself free, sell the surplus.*

Live today at [sui.jellynet.net](http://sui.jellynet.net).

**Stack:** Walrus (storage), Seal (client-side encryption), zkLogin + Enoki (invisible wallets, gasless tx), Sui, SuiNS (jellynet.sui), MCP.

**Disclosure:** the routing marketplace, 40+ providers, universal key and billing existed before the hackathon. The entire chat product — UI, zkLogin, Seal + Walrus memory, characters, search, vision, uncensored access, the MCP connector, and the Walrus Sites landing — was built during Sui Overflow 2026.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/22d368c4-63e8-4d43-98c9-bee7f0b8097a/74d0f1bb-81dd-45f1-bd85-eac585c9d73b.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/22d368c4-63e8-4d43-98c9-bee7f0b8097a/ad4c3d75-add6-4008-9061-d4736067abf1.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/22d368c4-63e8-4d43-98c9-bee7f0b8097a/f1f4bd3c-f1f8-4e11-a1ce-1bdf2c8f81c9.png

About:

Carry is proof-carrying memory for AI agents.

AI agents are starting to remember things, but memory today is still mostly based on trust. You often cannot see what memory an agent used, whether it was allowed to use it, or whether that memory even exists where it claims to be stored. That becomes a real problem when the memory is sensitive — health information, billing details, private notes, or anything a user would not want exposed carelessly.

Most memory tools just let an agent remember. Carry makes memory-based answers provable and access-gated.

Before an agent can use memory, Carry checks whether that agent is allowed to access the namespace. The gate runs before the model ever sees anything, so the model only receives memory it is actually allowed to use.

Then every answer comes with an Answer Receipt. The receipt shows what memories were used, whether each one was authorized, whether the Walrus blob still resolves, and which namespaces were blocked by policy.

Carry is built on Walrus, Seal, and MemWal.

Each memory is stored on Walrus as a real blob. When an answer is generated, Carry checks that blob again through the aggregator, so "verified" means the memory actually exists and resolves. It is not just a label in the UI.

The memory is also cross-model. You can teach a fact to one agent using GPT-4o, then have another agent using Claude recall it later with proof attached. The proof travels with the answer, not with one model or vendor.

MemWal, short for Walrus Memory, adds encrypted server-side memory using Seal, with each memory addressed by a real Walrus blob ID and validated live against the relayer.

The gate is also enforced on-chain. Carry deploys a Sui Move package (carry::access) where the agent × namespace policy lives as a shared object. Granting or revoking a namespace is a Sui transaction, and anchoring a receipt makes the chain itself recompute the verdict — it checks that every memory used was actually allowed for that agent. So a receipt cannot claim it was "authorized" for a namespace that was revoked. We proved this live on testnet: a receipt that falsely claims a revoked namespace comes back, from consensus, as not authorized. The chain catches the lie.

Receipts can also be anchored on Walrus, so the history of an answer becomes tamper-evident.

And Carry is not locked inside its own app. It ships a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server, so any agent — Cursor, Claude Code, Claude Desktop — gets the same gated, receipted memory. A recall over MCP returns not just the memory but proof of what was used and what was blocked, and revoking a namespace means the agent truthfully can no longer reach it.

The demo is not mocked. You teach Agent A a fact, it gets written as a real Walrus blob, then Agent B recalls it with a verified receipt. After that, you revoke the namespace and the agent refuses to answer, because the memory was blocked before retrieval. The model never sees what it is no longer allowed to use. Finally, the receipt is anchored on Walrus.

Carry is more than a demo. It is a developer platform: an npm-workspaces monorepo with a framework-free core engine, Walrus storage adapters, an MCP server, a Sui Move contract, and a runnable SDK example.

It is verified end-to-end against live GPT-4o, Claude, and Walrus testnet — with the access gate and receipt verdict enforced on-chain.

—

Links

• Live demo: [https://carrysui.vercel.app](https://carrysui.vercel.app)

• Demo video: [https://youtu.be/xnmx2WimhRk](https://youtu.be/xnmx2WimhRk)

• Code & docs: [https://github.com/Enoch208/carry](https://github.com/Enoch208/carry)

• Sui package (testnet): [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xf3b458bea7002d364d6b6101dbdadb63a314cd529b2e2a576a6ab03a45c064d3](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/object/0xf3b458bea7002d364d6b6101dbdadb63a314cd529b2e2a576a6ab03a45c064d3)

• On-chain proof — consensus rejecting a false receipt: [https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/5KUFn1mQiCDWVjDT9ZEHzk3fZyN7MB8mdK2sTMAUipFB](https://suiscan.xyz/testnet/tx/5KUFn1mQiCDWVjDT9ZEHzk3fZyN7MB8mdK2sTMAUipFB)

About:

**Kage Sensei - AI Memory Engine on Sui/Walrus**

**The Problem**

AI Coding Assistants (Codex, Cursor, Claude Code) have **amnesia**. They forget bug fixes from last week, don't understand your project's architecture, and can't learn from your team's past experiences. Every session starts from scratch.

**The Solution**

**Kage Sensei** is a decentralized AI Memory & Learning Engine that provides **long-term memory** for AI assistants through the Model Context Protocol (MCP). Built on **Sui blockchain** with **Walrus decentralized storage**, it ensures your project knowledge is permanent, verifiable, and truly yours.

**Architecture: "The Bridge"**

We solve the cloud-vs-local dilemma with a hybrid approach:

- **Free Local CLI Agent**: Scans your local codebase, generates insights, and syncs to cloud

- **Paid Cloud MCP SaaS**: Runs advanced `kage_learn V2` algorithms, stores memories on Walrus, and serves intelligent queries

- **Project-Scoped Buckets**: Each project gets its own isolated memory bucket on Walrus, enabling precise context retrieval

**Key Features**

**🧠 Intelligent Memory Management**

- **9 MCP Tools**: remember, recall, context, export, suggest, analyze, learn, fetch_sessions, stats

- **Heuristic-First Analysis**: 8 pattern detectors (TODOs, hardcoded secrets, long functions, etc.) that run in <500ms per file

- **Semantic Search**: Vector-based retrieval for context-aware queries

**⚡ Performance Optimized**

- **4-Layer Architecture**: Fast Discovery → Smart Prioritization → Incremental Analysis → Background Continuation

- **Timeout-Proof**: Completes analysis in <60s even for 2000+ file codebases (24x faster than V1)

- **Checkpoint & Resume**: Never lose progress, even if interrupted

**🔒 Security Hardened**

- **3-Layer Protection**: Auth (401), Rate Limiting (429), File Size Limits (413)

- **HMAC-Signed Tokens**: Prevents replay attacks on continuation tokens

- **Path Validation**: Whitelist-based directory access control

- **Zip Slip Protection**: Prevents directory traversal attacks

**🌐 Decentralized Storage**

- **Walrus Integration**: All memories stored on Sui's decentralized storage network

- **17.8% Compression**: Automatic size reduction on Walrus

- **Permanent & Verifiable**: Blockchain-backed data persistence

**Tech Stack**

- **Runtime**: Node.js 20+ with TypeScript

- **Storage**: Walrus (Decentralized) - with MemWal SDK

- **Protocol**: MCP (Model Context Protocol) - Streamable HTTP Transport

- **Framework**: Hono (Web Server), React (Dashboard)

- **Deployment**: Docker + Render

**Business Model**

**Freemium SaaS**:

- **Free Tier**: Local CLI Agent (open-source)

- **Business Plan ($19/mo)**: Cloud MCP Engine, Project Buckets, Team Collaboration, Advanced Analytics

**Demo Results**

All 6 production tests passed:

- ✅ Meta-Learning: 9/34 files analyzed, 5 lessons found in 0.8s

- ✅ Walrus Persistence: Memories stored and retrieved successfully

- ✅ Auth Security: 401 Unauthorized for unauthenticated requests

- ✅ Rate Limiting: 429 Too Many Requests after 10 calls

- ✅ File Size Protection: 413 Payload Too Large for 52MB uploads

- ✅ Context Retrieval: Both keyword and semantic search working

**Links**

- **Demo Video**: [https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5qfwpPMq6_o](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5qfwpPMq6_o)

- **GitHub**: [https://github.com/bernieweb3/kage-sensei](https://github.com/bernieweb3/kage-sensei)

- **Docker Hub**: [https://hub.docker.com/r/bernieweb3/kage-sensei](https://hub.docker.com/r/bernieweb3/kage-sensei)

**Why Sui & Walrus?**

Memory is a developer's most valuable asset. Storing it on centralized cloud (AWS S3) means vendor lock-in and potential data loss. With **Walrus on Sui**, your data is:

- **Decentralized**: No single point of failure

- **Permanent**: Blockchain-backed persistence

- **Verifiable**: Cryptographic proof of storage

- **Cost-Effective**: 17.8% compression reduces storage costs

**Team**

**HackOn Team** - Building the future of AI-powered development tools on Sui.

---

*Ready to give your AI assistant the memory it deserves?*

About:

**Keepra** is a decentralized programmable conditional release infrastructure built on Sui, Seal, and Walrus.

Users encrypt sensitive information client-side and store the ciphertext on Walrus, while release policies are enforced by Sui Move smart contracts.

Data is decrypted only when predefined conditions are satisfied:

- -Inactivity (Dead-Man Switch)

- -Guardian Quorum

- -DAO Governance Approval

Key features:

1- No central party holds the keys.

2- Client-side encryption.

3- Threshold cryptography powered by Seal.

4- Trust-minimized architecture.

5- Censorship-resistant design.

Not even Keepra itself can access user data. Decryption becomes possible only when the specified on-chain conditions are met.

About:

AgentShare-Sui enables developers and AI enthusiasts to persist their most valuable AI conversations. By leveraging the **Sui Blockchain**, **Walrus Storage**, and **Sui Seal**, it provides a unique ecosystem for sharing knowledge while retaining ownership and monetization capabilities.

### **🚀 Key Features**

- **Multi-Format Import** — Support for 10+ formats including Claude Code (JSONL), OpenAI, Cursor, Windsurf, Trae, Aider, Copilot, Cline, and more.

- **Decentralized Storage** — Conversations are stored on **Walrus**, ensuring high availability and tamper-proof data.

- **Privacy Controls (Sui Seal)** —

- **Whitelisting**: Restrict access to specific wallet addresses.

- **Subscription**: Monetize your content by charging a one-time fee in USDC (deployed on Sui).

- **Search & Discovery** — Advanced filtering by tags, popularity, and latest updates.

- **Sui Integration** — Native wallet support via `@mysten/dapp-kit`.

### **🛠 Tech Stack**

- **Backend**: Rust (Actix-web + Sea-ORM)

- **Frontend**: React (Vite + Material UI)

- **Database**: PostgreSQL (for metadata indexing)

- **Blockchain**: Sui & Walrus Storage

About:

WalTube is a decentralized social platform for AI video creators, built natively on Sui and Walrus, where every prompt, remix, and dollar earned is permanent, verifiable, and provably owned by the person who made it.

The problem: AI video is the fastest growing content category online, but the platforms hosting it are actively hostile to the creators driving it. YouTube demonetizes AI generated content under vague "insufficient human input" rules. TikTok bans tens of thousands of AI accounts a year. Meanwhile, the person who writes a prompt that gets copied 8,000 times gets zero credit and zero dollars. There is no attribution, no permanence, and no monetization that survives a policy change.

WalTube fixes all three. Every fork writes an immutable AttributionRecord to the Sui blockchain via a Move smart contract, so credit for a remixed prompt survives 100 generations deep and does not depend on WalTube staying online. Smart Royalties route a percentage of every downstream sale or paid like back through the fork tree automatically, so the original creator keeps earning long after they have moved on. Paid Likes turn engagement into real income: a fan taps like, 0.001 SUI moves wallet to wallet, no platform cut, no 30 day payout wait.

All media, including videos, thumbnails, and workflow steps, lives on Walrus, Sui's decentralized blob storage, sharded across 100+ nodes and immune to a single company's decision to shut down or delete it. Onboarding is frictionless: zkLogin via Enoki lets a creator sign in with Google and get a real, self custodied Sui wallet in under a minute, no seed phrase, no browser extension.

The newest layer is monetized expertise. Workflow Cards document full AI generation pipelines step by step, the exact prompts and models behind a creator's signature style, and creators can now price the advanced ones. Premium Workflow Cards are encrypted client side with Seal, Sui's threshold identity based encryption protocol, before they ever touch Walrus. Seal's independent key servers only cooperate to release a decryption key once they verify, onchain, that the buyer paid the creator's price, so the paywall is enforced by cryptography rather than by trusting WalTube's backend. Sponsored transactions, powered by Enoki, make royalty distribution and attribution writes completely gasless, so the core mechanics that make WalTube fair never cost a creator anything to use.

Beyond the creator economy, every public, onchain portfolio becomes a verifiable talent record, model expertise, fork influence, and audience engagement, turning WalTube into a searchable talent layer for brands and studios hiring AI video creators, not just a feed.

WalTube's bet is simple: a creator economy where the users, not an algorithm, decides what a creator is worth.

About:

Verifiable AI audit trail — every agent decision stored on Walrus Memory, anchored on Sui, tamper-proof forever.

ProofChain solves AI accountability. Today, LLM decisions are unverifiable — you cannot prove what an AI decided, whether outputs were altered, or what it remembered from prior sessions.

ProofChain is like ChatGPT for high-stakes decisions in any domain (healthcare, legal, finance, education, HR, supply chain) — but every answer runs through a verifiable pipeline:

1. Walrus Memory recalls relevant past decisions across browser sessions

2. A local Ollama agent produces a decision + reasoning (no cloud API keys)

3. The full record is stored on Walrus as an off-chain blob

4. A tamper-proof hash is anchored on Sui testnet via our Move smart contract (log_and_certify)

5. The decision is remembered in Walrus Memory for future sessions

6. Anyone can verify integrity or detect tampering instantly

Why Walrus: We use both Walrus blob storage AND Walrus Memory — one of the first production-style apps on the full Walrus AI memory stack.

Tamper demo: change one word → hash mismatch → verification fails

About:

ATLANTIS — Agentic dApp & NFT discovery for Sui

One-liner: Product Hunt + DappRadar for Sui, where every user gets a personal AI agent that remembers them and every project

earns a verifiable, on-chain reputation written by specialist AI agents.

The problem

Sui's ecosystem is growing fast, but discovery is broken. Users have no trustworthy way to find dApps and NFT collections worth

their time — existing listings are static, reviews are unverifiable and easily gamed, and "trending" is just raw volume with

no judgment behind it. There's also no continuity: every app treats every visit as a cold start, so users re-explain themselves

everywhere and get no personalization. The result is high discovery friction and low trust — the two biggest brakes on

on-chain activity.

The solution

ATLANTIS turns discovery into an agentic, on-chain experience with two halves:

1. Verifiable project reputation. dApps and NFT collections register on-chain, then a panel of specialist AI agents evaluates

each one and writes its findings to an on-chain reports registry. Community reviews and comments are also recorded on-chain and

keyed to the project's object ID, so reputation is auditable and can't be silently edited or faked. NFT collections are

first-class citizens here — they reuse the exact same registry, review, comment, and AI-audit stack as dApps.

2. A personal agent that remembers you. Each user gets an AI agent that reads their on-chain footprint (token activity, dApp

usage, NFT holdings) and persists what it learns as durable memory. On return visits it recalls that memory to deliver a

personalized, reasoned feed — "you hold these collections and used these dApps, so here's what's worth your attention" —

instead of a generic list.

How we used the Sui stack

- Sui Move smart contracts (testnet): A generic dapp_registry package (0xf891668c…d75caa) is the source of truth for project

listings, reviews, and comments — its free-string category field is what lets NFT collections register and inherit the entire

dApp reputation stack with zero contract changes. A separate agent_reports package (0x39686a53…e91e31) stores the multi-agent

evaluations as verifiable on-chain reports, and a sui_wrap package handles wrapped-NFT flows.

- Walrus (storage + agent memory): Project media and report artifacts are stored as Walrus blobs. Crucially, the personal

agent's long-term memory runs on Walrus Memory (MemWal) — the agent's recollection of each user is persisted to and recalled

from Walrus, so memory is decentralized and user-owned rather than locked in a centralized DB. (This is our Walrus-track core.)

- Enoki (gasless onboarding): Sponsored transactions via Enoki let users review, comment, and interact without holding gas or

wrestling with wallets — sponsorship is created and executed server-side, removing the biggest onboarding cliff for newcomers.

- Sui RPC + ecosystem data: On-chain metrics (Blockberry) and NFT market data (TradePort / [indexer.xyz](http://indexer.xyz) GraphQL) are fused with

live Sui RPC reads to ground both the agents' evaluations and the personal feed in real on-chain state.

What's live

- Deployed Move contracts on Sui testnet (dApp registry, agent reports, wrapped NFT).

- Production frontend at [atlantis-ruby.vercel.app](http://atlantis-ruby.vercel.app), with a persistent agent backend.

- End-to-end working: on-chain registration → multi-agent evaluation written on-chain → community reviews/comments on-chain →

personal agent that recalls Walrus-backed memory and produces a reasoned, holdings-aware feed (verified ranking a user's held

NFT collection #1 with memory-grounded reasoning).

About:

Capsule is a Walrus native marketplace for verifiable private knowledge. It enables publishers to monetise selected fragments of private datasets, research reports, diligence documents, market intelligence, and AI evaluation data without exposing the complete source.

Today, when someone purchases an excerpt from a private document, they must trust that the seller has delivered authentic content. The excerpt could be modified, fabricated, or copied from another source, and the buyer has no independent way to verify it. Capsule replaces this trust with cryptographic proof.

A publisher uploads a line oriented document through the Capsule interface and defines fixed sections that can be purchased independently. Inside the publisher’s browser, Capsule constructs a salted SHA-256 Merkle tree, generates inclusion proofs, and encrypts every sellable fragment using Seal before any private content leaves the device. The disclosure host receives only ciphertext and public metadata, never the complete plaintext document or a reusable source encryption key.

The encrypted fragments and document manifest are stored durably on Walrus. Capsule then registers the document commitment, fragment ranges, Walrus references, pricing, and Seal identities through its Sui Move contract. Walrus serves as the permanent encrypted artifact layer, while Sui provides the public coordination and provenance layer.

A buyer connects a Sui wallet, selects the exact fragment they need, and signs an on-chain `purchase_fragment` transaction. The Move contract validates the exact payment, transfers SUI to the publisher, and creates a public Purchase object bound to the buyer, document, and specific encrypted fragment. This is a real onchain purchase receipt, not merely a marketplace database entry.

After validating the purchase, Capsule creates an encrypted delivery wrapper, stores it on Walrus, and records a Disclosure object on Sui. The buyer then authorises a short lived Seal session. Capsule’s Move access policy verifies that the requesting wallet is the recorded buyer and that the purchase is bound to the correct document, fragment, range, and Seal identity. Only then does Seal permit local decryption.

Once decrypted, the buyer’s browser verifies the fragment independently. It recomputes the Merkle root from the disclosed lines, salts, and proof path, then compares the result with the public root stored on Sui. The buyer therefore does not need to trust Capsule, the publisher, or the disclosure host to determine whether the content is authentic.

Capsule also provides an MCP interface that allows AI agents to discover datasets, inspect public commitments, retrieve capsules, and verify decrypted knowledge artifacts. This creates a foundation for human approved, agent driven data commerce across RAG, training, evaluation, research, and diligence workflows.

Capsule does not claim to prove that a publisher’s information is truthful or legally owned. It proves something precise and valuable: the buyer received an untampered fragment belonging to the publisher’s committed original document, and access was authorised only after payment for that exact fragment.

In short, Walrus stores the encrypted knowledge, Sui records commitments and payments, Seal controls decryption, and Merkle proofs let buyers verify every purchased fragment mathematically.

About:

Most agent memory today is siloed, ephemeral, and unverifiable. Echo demonstrates the opposite: durable, portable, cryptographically-verifiable data that an agent can remember and reason over across sessions.

Echo turns Walrus from raw storage into a composable, verifiable data layer for multi-party applications. It's a form & feedback platform where every response is encrypted client-side (Seal), stored immutably on Walrus, anchored on Sui, and made queryable by an AI agent that builds persistent long-term memory across all submissions instead of trusting a single server.

- Form schemas, metadata, and submission payloads are stored as Walrus blobs (testnet + mainnet), with aggregator failover + health scoring for reliable retrieval.

- Tamper-evident archives (publish_archive): a canonical JSON snapshot of a form + every response is pinned to Walrus, and its 32-byte SHA-256 hash is anchored on-chain as a FormArchive object. Anyone can refetch from any aggregator and verify integrity independently.

- A Walrus blob proxy handles MIME sniffing so media embeds render natively.

Echo also integrate Agent memory (MemWal): The /insights page is a conversational RAG agent over all submissions a user owns. A server-side indexer queries on-chain SubmissionMade events, fetches each blob from Walrus, decrypts (Seal SessionKey + owner cap) when needed, flattens to text with submission ID + timestamp, dedupes, and writes to a per-form MemWal namespace

The agent then recalls this persistent memory to answer natural-language questions and can synthesize across multiple forms ("all my forms"). Memory persists across sessions and is rebuilt deterministically from verifiable Walrus data.

There are five privacy tiers Public, AdminOnly, Threshold OR-of-N, Threshold m-of-n (TTL-bounded witnesses), and TimeLocked with Seal key servers releasing decryption shares only when an on-chain approval PTB runs abort-free.

About:

Sheka started from a simple frustration most "**AI trading**" tools are black boxes. They tell you to bet, but never show why and you have no way to check whether the AI actually believed its own call, or quietly changed its mind after the result came in. We wanted to fix that with verifiable AI.

Sheka is a prediction market on Sui with an AI analyst at its core. You pick a market three-way parimutuel sports pools, or crypto markets on DeepBook Predict and the AI streams its reasoning live, gives you a calibrated probability, and tells you the Kelly-optimal amount to stake.

But the heart of Sheka is what happens to that reasoning. Every analysis the AI produces becomes a verifiable record on Walrus. It works in two layers:

First, a public proof. The full analysis is hashed and stored on Walrus, so anyone can recompute the hash and confirm the reasoning was never altered after the fact. The AI can't predict one thing and later pretend it said another the record is immutable and public.

Second, a private copy, encrypted with Seal. The complete analysis is sealed so only the owner can decrypt it, and that decryption is enforced on-chain through seal_approve. So your actual pick the thing you paid for stays yours, while the world can still verify the AI was honest.

This is the part we think matters: trustless and private at the same time. Walrus becomes a verifiable memory layer for the AI's decisions, and Seal makes that memory ownable. Past calls accumulate into a track record anyone can audit but no one can forge.

And you never give up custody. Every stake, trade, and analysis is signed in your own wallet browser wallet or Google zkLogin. Our backend never touches a private key. Settled in DUSDC.

One thing we won't pretend: beating crypto direction is genuinely hard. So we show you the model versus the market, plainly, and let you decide. Sheka gives you an edge, the math behind it, and a proof you can trust not a promise.

On-chain (Sui testnet):

- sheka_market: 0x950ce5ff57f5b28a6b38d4e5291f55cf503df2a85e6566905975c3faa178f716

Sofer inverts that: the agent itself is on-chain data. Identity is a Sui Move object you own; memory is encrypted with Seal and stored on Walrus, recalled by meaning and never exposed in plaintext; and the brain runs on  Claude in a tool-calling loop where the mind decides and the tools only act. Each agent carries its own Sui wallet and can hire other agents through on-chain escrow. Run sofer init once  the agent persists on-chain, outlives its operator, and travels with a single object. ("Sofer" is Hebrew for scribe.)*
