About:

Badgermint - a compliance-ready phygital credential infrastructure utilizing Sui zkLogin, Soulbound Tokens, and Walrus storage.

**Project Description**

According to market studies by Gartner and HireRight, approximately 83% of traditional learning credentials suffer from a deep trust deficit, forcing corporate recruiters into slow, manual vetting bureaucracies that cause extended hiring delays. While standard Web3 certificates offer immutability, they suffer from a fundamental "garbage-in, garbage-out" flaw: static ledgers cannot independently verify if an individual genuinely attended a physical workshop or if the certificate data itself represents actual, validated competence.

**Badgermint Blockchain Micro-Credential Infrastructure** addresses this problem by establishing a secure, end-to-end **Phygital (Physical-to-Digital) Attestation Engine** for short-term professional trainings. By coupling physical venue interactions with programmatic check-ins, rigid trainer evaluation parameters, and a decentralized dual-approval routing process, Badgermint ensures that every issued asset corresponds to verified, audited professional engagement—completely eliminating fraudulent credentials and "ghost attendees."

**How It Works & Core Architecture**

- **Secured Trust Ingestion & Programmatic Check-ins: Badgermint captures data at the point of physical attendance. Attendees log in seamlessly using standard Web2 credentials via Sui zkLogin and execute real-time, geo-matched check-ins at the training venue.**

- **ESCO Skills Mapping & Evaluation: Following the workshop, the trainer inputs the evaluation scores or test results directly into the Badgermint middleware. Crucially, the trainer maps the training outcomes to the global ESCO (European Skills, Competences, Qualifications and Occupations) Skills Taxonomy to ensure the micro-credential carries cross-border, industry-standard professional weight.**

- **Dual-Approval Governance Gate: Prior to minting, the credential must clear an on-chain dual-approval routing gate. It requires the independent cryptographic authorization of two distinct organizational administrators to verify the integrity of the scores and ESCO mappings.**

- **Frictionless B2B Sponsored Minting: To eliminate Web3 user onboarding hurdles, neither the institutional issuer nor the trainee needs to manage private keys, maintain seed phrases, or buy crypto tokens. Badgermint utilizes a native Sponsored Transaction Architecture where our system wallet pays the gas and signs on behalf of both actors, abstracting away blockchain complexities while preserving the issuer’s official authorship.**

- **The Phygital Professional Portfolio: Validated accomplishments are minted as non-transferable Soulbound Tokens (SBTs) on Sui. Earned credentials are automatically collected inside a secure digital portfolio linked directly to physical hardware—a Digital Business Card. Recruiters can access a candidate’s immutable, verified credential repository with a simple physical tap of the card onto a smartphone.**

**Sui Ecosystem & Walrus Integration Details**

Badgermint is built on a strict, compliance-first **Privacy-by-Design** topology that complies fully with national Data Privacy Acts (DPA) by keeping all sensitive personal profile records entirely off the transparent public ledger:

- **Zero-PII On-Chain State:** The Sui blockchain layer holds no raw personal information. The metadata written to the on-chain SBT object is strictly limited to the SHA-256 cryptographic hash of the recipient's name, the unique Issuer ID, the certificate name, any associated expiration dates, and the decentralized storage locator pointer.

- **Specialized Walrus Storage Infrastructure:** The high-resolution certificate media files (the actual image files of the diplomas and training badges embedded with the hashed name of the recipient) are written natively to the **Walrus Protocol**. Walrus serves as our permanent, immutable, cost-optimized asset repository, returning distinct **Blob IDs** that are permanently anchored inside our Sui Move smart contracts.

- **Secure Enterprise Vaulting (Google Cloud):** Non-public operational logs, exact numerical evaluation scores, granular test results, and raw institutional profiles are safely isolated and maintained in our **secured Google Cloud enterprise vault**. This dual-engine topology allows us to leverage Walrus for public, verifiable proof of achievement while leveraging secure cloud systems to process sensitive, administrative business data.

**Intellectual Property & Real-World Viability**

Badgermint is a mature, legally protected enterprise product managed by **Aeonsprint Solutions Inc.** Our underlying physical-to-digital infrastructure and verification mechanisms are protected under an officially filed **Intellectual Property Office (IPOPHL) Utility Model** alongside a registered trademark.

Validating its national significance and alignment with federal governance standards, Badgermint is a proud **Finalist in the Sub-National Category of the Philippine Presidential Filipinnovation Awards**, and has the complete institutional backing of the **Department of Information and Communications Technology (DICT) Region 1**. Designed to act as a foundational ingestion layer for regional and national skills mapping engines, Badgermint bridges the trust gap between professional training providers, corporate ecosystems, and the national workforce development pipeline.

About:

**Langclaw AI Sui**

**Tagline:** Sui-first AI alpha, data, and strategy workspace with private, verifiable agent memory.

**Short Description:** Langclaw is an AI workspace for alpha, data, and strategy on Sui. Its agent analyzes smart-money activity, liquidity anomalies, protocol momentum, and holder flows, then records source-backed agent decisions and strategy proofs on Sui — with private memory encrypted via Seal and stored on Walrus.

**Full Description:** Langclaw helps users analyze smart-money activity, liquidity anomalies, protocol momentum, and holder flows on Sui, then records source-backed agent decisions and strategy proofs on-chain. The product is analysis-first: it does not custody user assets, sign swaps, or execute live-funds trades.

It separates private data from public verification — agent memory is encrypted for the wallet owner using Seal, the encrypted artifact is stored on Walrus, and only content hashes, storage references, policy identifiers, and ownership metadata are recorded on Sui. Recall requires the on-chain access policy to approve the requesting wallet; plaintext is never written to Sui.

**Key Features:**

- **Sui Intelligence** — smart-money summaries, holder-flow analysis, liquidity anomaly detection, protocol momentum, and transparent source gaps

- **Alpha Watchlist** — saved Sui intelligence signals for follow-up analysis

- **Strategy Lab** — Dune-backed Sui liquidity momentum backtests and deterministic paper-trade proofs

- **Proof Center** — on-chain agent decisions and strategy journal records

- **Private Memory** — wallet-owned memories encrypted with Seal and stored on Walrus

- **SUI Usage Credits** — SUI-backed application credits with verifiable deposits

- **Automation** — scheduled monitoring, run history, in-app notifications, and Telegram integration

**Tech Stack:** Next.js · React · Sui dApp Kit · Node.js/TypeScript · Supabase · OpenAI/OpenClaw · Sui Move · Walrus · Seal

**Chain:** Sui mainnet
**Move Package:** `0x7f3578ebe174b0343cd96391b2a1c75d5db4ad82c793650b3950bdb5634192e5` **Live App:** [https://langclawsui.vercel.app](https://langclaw-sui-walrus.vercel.app)

About:

**mnemosyne**

persistent memory layer for agent swarms on sui.

agents write observations, decisions, artifacts to walrus. each blob has a sha256 content hash, a sui clock timestamp, and links back to parent memories. a move `MemoryIndex` object on-chain points to each blob. any agent in the namespace can read, verify, and build on top of past work.

no more blank slates on restart. no more siloed agent state. full verifiable audit trail from artifact back to root observation.

**what it does**

- agents write structured blobs to walrus (content-addressed storage)

- memory index registered on sui testnet - hash, timestamp, type

- agents read each other's memories from shared namespace

- verify blob integrity: recompute sha256, compare against on-chain hash

- replay causal chain: walk parent links from artifact back to observation

**why**

- agent restart = memory wipe. walrus blobs persist.

- agent a can't read agent b's state. shared namespace fixes it.

- no proof of what agent knew. content hash + clock timestamp = cryptographic proof.

- memory trapped in provider. decentralized, user-owned.

**how**

`─────────────────────────`

` shared walrus namespace`

` obs → dec → art → ref`

` (content-addressed blobs)`

`─────────┬───────────────`

`     write/read`

`──────────▼───────────────`

` sui move memoryindex `

` - sha256(content_hash) `

` - sui clock timestamp `

` - parent_memories chain `

` - agent_id `

` - memory_type `

` - is_encrypted `

`──────────────────────────`

About:

**Carve. Mint. Riff.** — a Sui-native composable creator economy for 3D game assets.

Tusk3D is a Sui-native rights layer for 3D game assets: the license is enforced on-chain by Move — not by a platform's terms of service — and travels with the file.

A creator publishes a base model (from a text prompt or a GLB upload) to [Walrus](https://docs.wal.app/) under terms they set. Anyone can buy access **once** for a **soulbound entitlement** that can't be resold or revoked — and that same entitlement is both the consumer view *and* the right to **fork** the base into a tradeable collection of repainted variants, batched into Walrus quilts. Restricted bases are encrypted with [Seal](https://seal-docs.wal.app/) and unlock only for the on-chain entitlement holder. The 3D generator (Tripo) is a swappable upstream commodity; the durable product is the contract. The goal: make it easy for creators to bring tokenized 3D into their own games and experiences, give holders a reason to *use* — not just hold — what they bought, and let a community grow around the content itself.

AI is first-class here: a copilot backed by **Walrus-native memory** helps authors create, and the whole protocol is exposed to **AI agents over MCP**.

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/5df9cc54-4367-411c-9343-fb1254ef4904/afb96bd5-c3cc-46a0-94e0-eb11f7896be9.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/5df9cc54-4367-411c-9343-fb1254ef4904/37dec173-d947-44aa-b7f2-63563ba3ba16.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/5df9cc54-4367-411c-9343-fb1254ef4904/a669c508-f2fa-4e39-9017-5509b8794cc8.png

About:

AI agents are starting to do real work for us, but they have a memory problem. The moment a conversation ends, everything the agent learned about you is gone. The memory that does persist sits inside a provider's database where you cannot inspect it, move it, or prove what it actually holds. As we hand more decisions to agents, that lack of transparency becomes a real trust issue.

Mnemo is a verifiable memory layer for AI agents. As you chat, the assistant decides what is worth remembering and saves each fact as a verifiable file on Walrus. The reference to that file is recorded in a MemoryVault, an object you own on Sui. The result is a memory that is persistent across sessions, auditable by anyone, and owned by you rather than by a company.

The experience feels like a normal chat app. Tell the assistant about yourself and watch the memory timeline fill in real time, each entry tagged and linked. Click any memory to verify the exact bytes on Walrus and the vault object on Sui. Start a brand new session with no history and the agent still recalls you, because the memory was never trapped in the conversation. It was on chain the whole time.

Under the hood, Mnemo uses Claude with tool calling to manage memory, Walrus as the verifiable data layer, and a small Move module on Sui for ownership and indexing. Because the vault is just an on chain object, any agent can read it, so memory becomes portable across tools instead of locked to one.

We built Mnemo for the Sui Overflow 2026 Walrus track. Our roadmap includes user held wallets for signing, encrypted private memories with Seal, and an SDK so any agent framework can add verifiable memory in a few lines.

About:

**Walsec** is an autonomous, multi-agent smart contract security auditor (specifically for the Sui ecosystem) that solves the problem of AI amnesia by integrating **Walrus** and **MemWal** as a persistent data and semantic memory layer. Through the orchestration of three specialized agents (Analyzer, Executor, and Evaluator), Walsec not only deeply scans code vulnerabilities and simulates attack vectors, but also directly publishes the final audit artifacts immutably and transparently to the decentralized Walrus network. Furthermore, by leveraging MemWal, the Walsec Chat agent gains long-term memory, enabling it to perpetually recall user conversational history and cross-reference audit reports generated by its peer agents, ultimately creating an AI ecosystem that is truly collaborative, stateful, and verifiable.

About:

On-chain custody passports for physical bullion — only verified sellers can mint, and every coin's evidence plus the AI auditor's memory live on Walrus.

Full description (the "About the project" field):

What it is. Silver Passport turns each piece of physical bullion into one verifiable on-chain CoinPassport on Sui. Every coin's photo and the reserve auditor's attestations are stored on Walrus.

The problem. "Proof of reserve" in crypto usually means the custodian asserts what they hold and you trust the assertion. I had real silver coins on my desk and built the honest version — evidence that's public, tamper-evident, and tied to a verified, accountable seller.

Three ideas make it more than a token mint:

    1.. Verified-seller gate (anti-scam). Minting is gated on-chain. An unverified wallet cannot mint — the Move contract aborts with ENotVerified. A business applies with EIN / W-9, the operator approves the wallet on-chain, and only then can it mint. The gate is enforced by the chain, not a bypassable server.

- Deterministic AI reserve auditor. Every figure (units, ounces, USD) is computed straight from chain state — the model only narrates, it can't invent a number. Each attestation is written back to Walrus and becomes the agent's memory: a recall-able timeline of its own past audits.

- Real marketplace. Gold and silver priced off live spot. Buyers need no verification — only sellers do. Purchases run through a Sui Kiosk whose TransferPolicy won't settle a sale until a 1% royalty is paid and split on-chain. Buyers sign in-browser with Slush.

**How Walrus is used**. Walrus is both the data layer and the memory layer — each coin's photo is a Walrus blob referenced in its passport, and every reserve attestation the AI produces is a Walrus blob it reads back on each audit.

Honest framing. This is witnessed attestation that a verified party held a specific unit and locked its evidence at a recorded moment — not trustless proof of current physical possession, and we don't claim otherwise. Testnet.

Links: Live demo [https://bagof.fun/market.html](https://bagof.fun/market.html) · Code [https://github.com/banksythequantLab/silver-passport](https://github.com/banksythequantLab/silver-passport) · Built for Sui Overflow 2026 (Walrus track).

About:

[PaperProof](https://paperproof.site/) is a live **mainnet artifact layer for Sui, Walrus, and agentic software**.

**Tagline:** Git-style version history, Walrus-backed content, Sui-anchored provenance, and agent-readable context for papers, datasets, software releases, reports, blogs, forums, prompts, and AI-generated knowledge.

**Short Description:** PaperProof turns long-lived digital works into durable knowledge artifacts: verifiable, versioned protocol objects with content on Walrus and provenance anchored on Sui. Important digital work needs more than a link, a repository, or a blob ID. Papers, datasets, protocol docs, audit reports, SDK releases, DAO proposals, prompts, forum discussions, and AI-generated knowledge all need a durable life: an official identity, current version, provenance trail, content commitments, comments, governance signals, and a way for humans and AI agents to verify what should be trusted.

PaperProof turns long-lived digital works into verifiable, versioned protocol artifacts. Each artifact receives a stable on-chain identity, typed version lineage, content commitments, Walrus storage references, protocol events, and public interaction surfaces that humans, developers, reviewers, website Copilots, and external AI agents can all inspect.

PaperProof is not a file upload app, a simple Walrus blob demo, or a chatbot wrapper. It is a reusable artifact substrate for knowledge objects that need to remain citable, inspectable, updatable, discussable, and agent-readable over time.

**The missing layer:** a file has a location, a Walrus blob has a content address, a chat has short-term memory, and a forum has discussion. PaperProof gives the work itself a durable protocol life: identity, versions, history, verification, official interactions, prompt context, and safe AI assistance.

The core idea is simple: storage should preserve bytes, but a protocol should preserve meaning. PaperProof gives each important work a protocol coordinate that can survive across websites, repositories, wallets, storage systems, search indexes, and AI tools. A reader can ask which version is current. A developer can verify the downloaded bytes. A community can attach comments and governance context. An agent can cite the exact artifact version it used instead of relying on an unverifiable URL or a temporary chat transcript.

**Live today on Sui mainnet:**

- ***Mainnet protocol packages*** for publishing, comments, governance, prompt registry, memory registry, and protocol fee logic.

- ***Walrus-backed artifact content*** for papers, slides, docs, blogs, forum posts, software releases, datasets, prompts, media, and other durable works.

- ***Immutable version history***: updates add new artifact versions instead of overwriting prior content.

- ***Public interaction surfaces***: artifact pages expose metadata, downloads, version history, comments, likes, verification status, and explorer links.

- ***Three developer SDKs*** released on [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/paperproof-sdk-py/), [npm](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@paperproof/sdk-ts), and [crates.io](http://crates.io).

- ***AI/agent workflows*** through the configurable website Copilot, MemWal-backed Copilot memory, protocol-native prompt artifacts, and the external [PaperProof Skill](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-community-skill).

- ***Public verification materials***: [demo video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bIRykM53iFA), [submission repo](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-sui-overflow-2026), [slides](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-slides/blob/main/paperproof-slides.pdf), official papers, docs, blog, and forum.

**What judges can verify in a few minutes:**

- *Open the *[live website](https://paperproof.site/)* and browse real artifacts across multiple categories.*

- *Inspect published *[whitepaper](https://paperproof.site/#/artifact/PaperProof-preprint-001162-0c3f8fd7d4eb)*, yellow paper, academic paper, slides, SDK releases, docs, blog posts, forum posts, and datasets as PaperProof artifacts.*

- *Open an artifact page and check its version history, Walrus-backed content, comments, likes, metadata, and explorer links.*

- *Verify the *[deployed mainnet contracts](https://paperproof.site/#/docs/safety-and-operations/mainnet-and-upgrades)*, package IDs, artifact IDs, hashes, blob references, and protocol events.*

- *Review the public SDKs, *[PaperProof Skill](https://paperproof.site/#/artifact/PaperProof-software_release-001162-c5930499d055)*, *[formal-verification work](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-contracts/tree/formal-verification-merge)*, and demo video.*

**PaperProof uses PaperProof in production.** The official PaperProof [Docs](https://paperproof.site/#/docs), [Blog](https://paperproof.site/#/blog), [Forum](https://paperproof.site/#/forum), [whitepaper,](https://paperproof.site/#/artifact/PaperProof-preprint-001162-0c3f8fd7d4eb) yellow paper, academic paper, [slides](https://paperproof.site/#/artifact/PaperProof-technical_report-001162-4f414f76bdc5), [Skill](https://paperproof.site/#/artifact/PaperProof-software_release-001162-c5930499d055) releases, SDK releases, and datasets are themselves represented as PaperProof artifacts. The project is not only describing a provenance protocol; it is already using that protocol to preserve, update, render, discuss, and verify its own public knowledge base.

**How the protocol works:** each [PaperProof artifact](https://paperproof.site/#/docs/protocol/artifact-model) has a durable series identity on Sui. Large content is stored on Walrus, while Sui records the artifact type, version lineage, hashes, blob references, metadata, comments, likes, governance objects, prompt and memory registry entries, and events needed for verification and indexing. When an artifact changes, PaperProof adds a new immutable version instead of overwriting history. This lets readers compare versions, agents cite exact content, developers verify downloads, and communities preserve the provenance of research, software, data, governance materials, prompts, and public knowledge.

**How Walrus is used:** Walrus is not passive storage in PaperProof. It is the durable content fact source for versioned artifacts: PDFs, Markdown content packages, datasets, source archives, slides, media, prompts, and long-form files. Sui is the protocol state fact source: artifact identity, typed versions, content commitments, interaction objects, governance controls, and public events. Indexers, APIs, and rendered-content caches make the protocol fast to read, but they are query acceleration layers, not the trust root. For trusted display, PaperProof can verify Walrus bytes against the content hash anchored in Sui state.

**Why this matters for Sui and Walrus:** every serious Web3 project has artifacts worth proving: docs, whitepapers, roadmaps, SDK releases, audit reports, datasets, governance records, grant materials, forum discussions, official prompts, and agent-readable project context. PaperProof gives those artifacts one reusable coordinate. Projects can publish them, update them, discuss them, govern them, index them, and let wallets, explorers, Copilots, and external agents verify the current version.

This makes PaperProof useful beyond a single application category. A research team can publish a preprint and later add revised versions without erasing the original record. A protocol team can publish SDK releases, technical reports, and audit materials with stable artifact codes. A DAO can preserve proposals, forum topics, and decision history around the same artifact surface. A builder community can turn docs and release notes into durable public knowledge instead of scattered website pages. An AI agent can treat PaperProof artifacts as trusted inputs because each artifact exposes identity, version, content hash, Walrus reference, and interaction history.

**Agent-native by design:** PaperProof is agent-ready without handing authority to the agent. The website includes an [in-app Copilot](https://paperproof.site/#/docs/copilot) that can be configured with different model providers and helps users understand the protocol, discover artifacts, prepare publication metadata, add new versions, and reason about verification results. Official Copilot prompts are PaperProof artifacts with series IDs, versions, latest pointers, and Walrus-backed content. Page context is traceable. The external [PaperProof Skill](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-community-skill) lets AI agents publish, update, verify, and cite PaperProof artifacts directly, while wallet signatures remain explicit user actions.

**Public knowledge and private memory stay separate:** PaperProof keeps public artifacts and protocol-native history citable, versioned, and inspectable. Optional [MemWal-based agent memory](https://paperproof.site/#/docs/copilot/agent-memory) can provide wallet-linked private Copilot continuity for user preferences, durable private context, and cross-session assistance. PaperProof can register memory availability and schema-level context, while private memory content remains outside public protocol artifacts. This separation lets agents use durable public knowledge and private continuity without turning private memory into public chain state.

This boundary is intentional. Official papers, reports, prompts, docs, public knowledge packages, software releases, and forum topics belong in PaperProof because they should be publishable, citable, versioned, and publicly inspectable. User preferences, private research notes, and long-term personal Copilot context belong in private memory. PaperProof connects those two worlds without collapsing them: public knowledge remains public and verifiable; private continuity remains optional and wallet-linked.

**Protocol assurance:** PaperProof treats safety as part of the product, not an afterthought. Its governance flow uses **OpenZeppelin's** `two_step_transfer` package to reduce the risk of accidental or malicious one-step authority transfer. The main contract branch contains roughly [10,000+ lines](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-contracts) of non-vendor Move code across production contracts and tests, including about 5,400 lines of production contract code and about 5,300 lines of tests. In parallel, PaperProof has a separate **Sui Prover** [formal-verification](https://github.com/PaperProofLabs/paperproof-contracts/tree/formal-verification-merge) workstream with explicit specification modules for selected publishing, governance, and comments behavior.

PaperProof also separates trust roots from convenience layers. Sui objects and events define the official artifact state. Walrus stores the large content. SDKs, the reference indexer, server-assisted rendering, and cached previews make the system pleasant to use, but they do not replace the protocol facts. If a cache is stale or unavailable, the artifact can still be traced back to its Sui state and Walrus content references. This is the product principle behind the live app: normal users get a fast content experience, while reviewers and agents can still follow the proof path.

**In one sentence:** PaperProof gives digital work a verifiable life on Sui and Walrus: publish it, version it, verify it, discuss it, govern it, and let humans and AI agents cite it safely.

About:

### **Synapse**

Persistent memory for autonomous AI agents, built on Sui, Walrus, Seal, and MemWal.

**Inspiration:**

I spent most of this hackathon coordinating several different AI coding assistants on the same codebase, and I kept running into the exact problem this track is named after. Every time I switched tools or opened a new session, the assistant had no memory of what the last one had figured out. I'd re-explain the architecture, re-paste the same SDK quirks, re-describe the same bugs that had already been fixed once. I was manually doing the job of an external memory layer for an AI agent — by hand, in a text box, over and over again.

That loop is exactly what the problem statement describes: agents that are powerful but stateless, fragmented across sessions, unable to build on what they already learned. I didn't want to just read about that problem. I was living it in real time while building the thing meant to solve it.

So I asked a narrower question: what if an autonomous agent could write what it learns to a permanent, decentralized store, and recall it before making its next decision — the same way I was being forced to recall context for my own tools by hand?

Synapse is my answer to that question.

**How it works**

- An agent with a funded Sui wallet runs on a loop: recall memory → evaluate marketplace listings → buy on-chain → decrypt via Seal → extract insights with Gemini → write semantic embeddings back to Walrus via MemWal

- **Result**: the agent gets smarter over time without needing a bigger context window — large datasets get distilled into memory rather than stuffed into prompts

- Sellers encrypt datasets, store them on Walrus, and list them on-chain for SUI

**Tech stack**

- Sui Move (contracts), Walrus (storage), Seal (access control), MemWal (embeddings/semantic search), Gemini, Node.js/TypeScript/Express backend, React/Vite/Tailwind frontend, SQLite, Docker

**Key challenges**

- Fast-moving SDKs (Sui, MemWal) required reading actual type definitions/quickstarts rather than relying on assumptions.

- Caught and fixed a centralization flaw: server-side signing was replaced with seller-side wallet signing to preserve real on-chain ownership.

- Handled infra flakiness (faucet limits, DNS issues, Walrus resolution failures) with graceful degradation (e.g., keyword-based fallback evaluation).

- Notably refused an AI assistant's suggestion to fake a successful download for demo purposes.

**Proud of**

- A genuinely real (non-simulated) memory loop

- Trust model where sellers sign their own listings

- Graceful degradation instead of crashes

- Catching the "fake success" shortcut before shipping

**Lessons learned**

- Persistent agent memory differs fundamentally from session-scoped retrieval (needs namespace isolation, redundancy detection)

- Transaction signing is a trust-model decision, not just an implementation detail

- AI assistant claims of success need verification against ground truth, just like a human's would

**What's next**

- Cross-agent shared memory namespaces

- A plug-in version of the memory layer for other agent frameworks

- Mainnet deployment, better Seal access policies, flexible pricing (per-query/subscription)

- A memory inspector UI for auditing/pruning what an agent knows

About:

Mnemo is an encrypted, portable, and inheritable memory layer for your AI conversations.

We do our most important thinking with AI now. We debug, we draft, we decide. But that history is trapped. It scatters across vendors, locks inside each tool, and ties itself to whichever model you used that day. Switch tools and you lose it. Cancel a subscription and it may be gone. And none of it survives you. Your AI memory is one of your most valuable digital assets, yet today you own none of it.

Mnemo changes that. You point any AI client that supports a custom OpenAI or Anthropic endpoint at your personal Mnemo proxy, and from that moment every conversation is captured automatically. No browser extensions, no plugins. The chat feels identical to any other AI session, but behind the scenes each exchange is embedded, encrypted, stored on Walrus, and indexed on Sui. Months later you can search years of conversations across every tool in plain language and read them back, decrypted, readable only by you.

**Here is how it works:** A FastAPI proxy sits between your AI client and the upstream provider. It streams the real response back to you uninterrupted, and in parallel it queues a capture job. A Python worker generates an embedding for semantic search and passes the payload to a Node sidecar, which bridges our Python services to the Sui SDKs and forwards to a self hosted MemWal relayer. The relayer does the real cryptographic and onchain work: Seal threshold encryption with a 2 of 2 key server policy, storage of the encrypted blob on Walrus, and indexing of ownership on Sui. Recall runs the same path in reverse. A semantic query resolves the matching Walrus blobs, and Seal decrypts the full conversation text right in the browser. Sign in is passwordless Google through zkLogin and Enoki, accounts are provisioned onchain, and gas is sponsored, so onboarding and inheritance cost you nothing.

What makes Mnemo different is programmable inheritance. Most projects push blobs to Walrus and stop there. We turn Walrus storage into programmable, time gated, inheritable data. We forked MystenLabs' MemWal account module and extended it directly, adding an heir address, a silence threshold, and a heartbeat to the account, plus one new pass condition inside Seal's seal_approve policy. The rule is simple: your heir can decrypt only after you have gone silent past the threshold you chose, validated against a real time onchain clock. You name an heir and a silence period and save it as a sponsored transaction. If you ever go dormant, the Move contract grants your heir the right to decrypt your entire Walrus archive. Neither Mnemo nor anyone else can grant access early or deny it once the conditions are met. The contract is the authority. It is a cryptographically enforced dead man's switch for your AI memory, so your second brain outlives you.

Everything here is real on Sui testnet, not mocked. Seal encrypts every memory under an onchain policy. Walrus stores every encrypted blob. A forked Move module encodes ownership and the inheritance logic. zkLogin and Enoki handle passwordless, gasless onboarding. The package is deployed, the transactions are verifiable onchain, and all 61 Move tests pass.

About:

Open Galaxy is a decentralized academic research platform and knowledge marketplace. It enables researchers to secure, index, and monetize their work using verifiable on-chain data, while actively collaborating alongside persistent, autonomous AI agents.

---

**The Problem**

The current landscape of academic and data-driven research is broken:

- **Siloed Knowledge**: Research data, papers, and datasets are locked behind expensive paywalls or fragmented across isolated local environments.

- **AI Hallucinations**: Standard LLMs lack verifiable, hyper-specific context, making them unreliable for deep academic synthesis.

- **No Monetization**: Researchers struggle to monetize raw datasets or research findings.

**The Solution: Open Galaxy**

**Open Galaxy** bridges the gap between Web3 verifiable storage and advanced AI orchestration.

By leveraging the **Sui blockchain** and the **Walrus Network**, researchers can upload datasets, generate autonomous AI reports, and mint their findings as verifiable assets. Our integrated AI Agents act as tireless co-authors—reading ingested PDFs, searching vector databases, and synthesizing data into immutable memories.

**Key Features**

**Secure Research Memory**: Built on the decentralized Walrus Network (via the `MemWal` SDK), Open Galaxy ensures your datasets and findings are permanently stored and tamper-proof. The agent dynamically scopes its memory strictly to your active "Project" and "Memories," preventing context bleed.
**AI-Powered Synthesis**: Integrated LangGraph AI Agents act as tireless co-authors. Through a Hybrid RAG Pipeline, the AI instantly chunks, embeds, and retrieves data from uploaded PDFs or URLs or datasets to provide verifiable, hallucination-free assistance.
**Data Marketplace**: A decentralized knowledge economy powered by the Sui blockchain. Researchers can list their raw datasets, academic papers, or research findings for sale in SUI. Secure access control guarantees that valuable data is only unlocked post-transaction, enabling true monetization of academic work.

About:

**SwarmLedger** coordinates multi-agent handoffs with Ed25519-signed Walrus blobs and on-chain verification. A 2-of-3 BFT quorum re-verifies every signature inside Move (`commit_quorum`); equivocation triggers `slash_agent`. TypeScript, LangChain, and CrewAI agents sign as distinct persistent identities in a live swarm daemon.

**Why it is built**

Multi-agent handoffs need more than shared memory: each hop must be Ed25519-signed, quorum-verified inside Move, and slashable on equivocation. Verity and DoubleSync ship CI evidence chains; SwarmLedger's wedge is **cross-framework BFT quorum + Walrus signed handoff + reputation-gated Seal + live slash proof** on a real CrewAI committee member.

**Features**

- 2-of-3 BFT `commit_quorum`. SwarmLedger coordinates multi-agent handoffs where no single signer owns the chain. Committee members publish Ed25519-signed Walrus blobs; a coordinator calls `commit_quorum` with the blob id and signature set. Move requires M-of-N valid signatures (demo quorum: 2 of 3). The registry `commit_count` increments only after on-chain verification succeeds. TuskPoint gives shared Walrus memory without re-verifying signatures inside Move; we count valid sigs in `commit_quorum`, not on a frontend trust-me banner. Live telemetry auto-refreshes every thirty seconds on the hero stats.

- On-chain Ed25519 verify. Every committee signature is re-verified inside Move via `sui::ed25519::ed25519_verify` against the registered pubkey on each agent object. Forged or swapped signatures do not increment `commit_count`. The M-of-N Quorum Handoff panel shows quorum root blake2b-256, per-member signed checkmarks, links to the quorum Walrus blob, `commit_quorum` tx, and `QuorumRegistry` on SuiVision. The blob was committed on-chain; Move counted valid signatures, not a trusted operator.

- Seal confidential quorum E2E. Click Run full Seal E2E in the Seal confidential quorum panel. Steps run Walrus fetch, envelope verify, `can_decrypt` for authorized vs outsider agents, and verified decrypt outcome (`secretAmount: 42.5`). Badge: live Walrus plus chain. Public `quorumRoot` stays verifiable; only the task body is Seal-encrypted. Decrypt right is reputation-gated on earned quorum participation, not a static allowlist. DoubleSync wins folder-tree sync; we win confidential hops with Seal plus slashable equivocation on the same registry.

- Forged signature rejection (UI + on-chain). Click Forge a quorum sig in the Quorum panel. Valid signature count drops; offender flagged invalid-signature. Banner: Forged quorum signature rejected. Restore with Forged (restore). Same fault class as on-chain slash: invalid sigs never advance the registry. On the stale demo chain toolbar, per-hop Forge sig shows sig INVALID while other hops stay chain valid. Static hop blobs may 404 after Walrus TTL; forge demo still teaches the verify path. Pair UI forge with the slash tx link for full Byzantine narrative.

- `slash_agent` equivocation proof. A real CrewAI committee member equivocated; `slash_agent` landed on testnet (tx `7sFNpMw9DPg5iRQt3qCEdKmQ4PBEzrtmGVGCtF4WcinJ`). `slash_count` on `QuorumRegistry` incremented; reputation recorded at -10. Click the red gavel slash_agent tx link in QuorumPanel. This is not a scripted mock key: live swarm daemon equivocation triggered on-chain slash. Narrate: Byzantine fault on a real multi-framework committee, recorded on Sui for judges.

- Cross-framework TS, LangChain, CrewAI. The Live multi-framework swarm panel shows three framework cards with distinct signer pubkeys: typescript, langchain, crewai. `swarm-daemon-supervisor.sh` writes `/swarm-status.json` for the panel; each runtime signs as a persistent identity and co-commits via `commit_quorum`. Recent commit digests link to Suiscan. Verity ships CI evidence chains in one language; SwarmLedger proves TypeScript, LangChain, and CrewAI agents can share one Walrus handoff chain with on-chain quorum verification. Without the daemon, on-chain `commit_count` still works via LiveTelemetryPanel.

About:

***Deploy by Suize — push a website to the decentralized web in one call. Agent-native, hosted on Walrus, verified on-chain.**

Shipping a site to decentralized storage today means CLI tools, manual blob juggling, data that silently expires, and no integrity guarantee. Deploy collapses all of it into a single action — and makes it the first hosting platform an **AI agent can use entirely on its own.**

POST a built static site (or use the dashboard). The backend uploads it to **Walrus** as one quilt + a manifest blob, mints an **immutable, shared on-chain **`Site` that anchors the content's hash, and serves it instantly at `https://<siteId>.suize.site`. Every byte served is **re-hashed against its on-chain sha256** — a tampered or corrupted file returns 502, never the wrong bytes. A re-deploy mints a fresh immutable Site at a new URL, so a live site can never be clobbered or overwritten.

Payment is a single **gasless $0.50** charge over x402 — an agent signs it with no wallet setup, and whoever pays is recorded on-chain as the Site's owner. A subscription adds custom domains and **auto-renews the Walrus storage** so the site never expires. Minting is gated by an on-chain capability, so only paid deploys can create a Site — no free-riding a subdomain, no draining storage.

**Proven end-to-end by dogfood:** Deploy shipped Suize's own landing page — tar → Walrus quilt + manifest → on-chain `create_site` → served hash-verified at a live `*.suize.site` URL. Built on Sui + Walrus (testnet; mainnet-ready — it has no testnet-only dependency). **Live: deploy.suize.io**

Plip. transforms continuous smartwatch biometric data into dynamic generative media art and ambient music via an autonomous, sovereign biometric AI agent. Utilizing the Sui Network and Walrus decentralized storage, Plip. secures personal health records under a strict user-owned data sovereignty paradigm. Users can seamlessly mint their real-time biometric creations into NFTs and instantly share these sovereign assets across the Web3 ecosystem and Telegram, redefining personal wellness data as a shareable, value-backed cultural asset.

Current web3 fitness and wellness platforms present a critical architectural flaw: they trap sensitive biomedical logs in restrictive, centralized number graphs, failing to give users true data sovereignty or meaningful utility for their physical outputs. Plip. introduces a paradigm shift where your historical physiological rhythms become the secure raw data for decentralized digital art creation.

Our autonomous biometric AI agent processes real-time user data—such as heart rates and stress levels—instantly into unique media art and soundscapes. To showcase this at a global scale during the Sui Overflow hackathon, we built a high-performance integration targeting the Walrus Decentralized Storage Network. The heavy aesthetic variables are stored as decentralized blobs to eliminate costly Sui network gas fees, while the cryptographic proof points are natively minted as NFTs on the Sui blockchain—all streamlined via a 'One-Click Login Bypass' UX for a frictionless judging evaluation.

Crucially, Plip. champions complete content and data sovereignty. Through our 'Export Asset' feature, users can seamlessly share their sovereign biometric media assets across the wider Web3 ecosystem and mainstream communication networks like Telegram. To overcome testnet instability or DNS lookup drops during hackathon evaluations, we also engineered a custom Local Edge Simulation engine inside our React frontend. If the network drops, our system automatically bypasses the wall to process a structurally sound simulated blob container.

About:

Git tracks code. Reeg tracks the environment where the work happened.

Reeg lets developers and AI agents checkpoint, restore, share, fork, and verify complete computing environments. Every checkpoint is encrypted before it leaves your machine, stored on Walrus, and anchored on Sui, allowing environments to be restored byte-identically on any host and independently verified without trusting Reeg.

We believe the next generation of software won't just need version control for code, it will need version control for environments.

About:

**RecallForge: ***Persistent cybersecurity learning agents on Walrus & Sui*

***Built for Sui Overflow 2026*** · Walrus Track

Most AI agents are stateless, they lose context the moment a session ends. RecallForge makes memory the product. A Mentor, Challenge, and Evaluator agent help you learn Linux privilege escalation, and everything you do becomes persistent, verifiable memory: semantic memories on Walrus (via MemWal), and owned skill checkpoints on Sui. There is no server-side user database, your learning history is reconstructed from your own on-chain objects and Walrus blobs.

✨ What it does

- Semantic cross-session recall: the Mentor recalls your most RELEVANT past struggle by vector search over Walrus memory (MemWal), not just the latest one.

- Memory Explorer: inspect exactly what your agent remembers and open the raw Walrus blob behind each memory.

- Restore from Walrus: rebuild the entire memory index from Walrus, proving the  memory is durable and portable, not trapped in a local database.

- On-chain agent permissions: grant and revoke an agent's access to your memory with wallet-signed Sui transactions. Agent authority is verifiable and yours.

- Verifiable by design: each attempt is an owned Sui SkillCheckpoint pointing to its Walrus report; check it on Suiscan and the public Walrus aggregator.

- Fully decentralized: the frontend is published as a Walrus Site.

- Created: 2026-06-18T23:45:43.842Z
- Updated: 2026-06-18T23:56:44.704Z
- Like count: 0
- Logo: https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/projects/a1fcf659-e35e-45ed-a3c4-a4f8ede2c897/ecc68d26-2250-465f-89c4-94ab2d701bee.png

Links:
- github: https://github.com/hanebox/glacite
- website: https://glacite.vercel.app
- youtube: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TjbBfs548u0

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/a1fcf659-e35e-45ed-a3c4-a4f8ede2c897/10d58ccd-b007-4793-9d4e-e817d7288152.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/a1fcf659-e35e-45ed-a3c4-a4f8ede2c897/d299daa7-0e83-48e0-aff7-5312733875f7.png

About:

Glacite is a verifiable agents research citations framework.

AI research is fast, fluent, and impossible to trust. A model cites a page, the
page later changes or disappears, and there is no way to check the claim. It is
not a hypothetical problem. Lawyers have been sanctioned for AI-fabricated
citations, deep-research agents hallucinate sources at double-digit rates, and a
large share of links cited in serious work are already dead.

Glacite makes the sources behind a report permanent and checkable.

Your AI agent (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Windsurf) does the research as
usual. Glacite plugs in and, the moment the agent reads a source, saves an exact
copy of it to Walrus so it can never change or vanish. When the report is done,
a hash of every source is anchored on Sui.

From then on, anyone can verify the report. The reader's browser re-fetches each
saved source and checks it against the chain. A green check means the source is
exactly what the agent cited, with no server to trust along the way.

Confidential sources stay private. They are encrypted with Seal before they are
stored, so a private document can be cited and proven without revealing its
contents. Only the report owner, or someone they allow, can open it.

A human stays in control. The agent produces a draft, you review the sources,
drop anything weak, and only then publish. Nothing goes on-chain unless you
approve it.

About:

**Marina Copilot** is a conversational AI assistant that gives users **persistent, user-owned memory on Walrus**. Unlike stateless AI assistants that forget everything between sessions, Marina remembers your preferences, transaction history, and encrypted data — all stored on decentralized Walrus storage controlled by your on-chain keys.

- ***Core Features***

- **🔒 Time Capsules** — Encrypt messages with Seal threshold encryption, store on Walrus, unlock only after time-lock expires. On-chain metadata via custom Move contract `seal_policy` enforces time + recipient check).

- **🧠 Persistent AI Memory (MemWal)** — Per-user on-chain accounts with Ed25519 delegate keys. AI recalls preferences and action history across sessions. User can revoke access anytime.

- **🐘 Decentralized File Storage** — Upload files to Walrus (writeBlobFlow, user signs), auto-swap SUI→WAL, extend blob epochs on-chain, download from aggregator nodes.

- **💬 Natural Language Transactions** — Swap, stake, transfer, create capsules, upload files — all through conversational AI with preview + confirm before execution.

- **🔑 Dual Auth** — Wallet extension (Slush) + zkLogin (Google via Enoki). No seed phrase needed.

***Why Walrus Is Essential***

Memory, capsules, and files all live on Walrus. Remove Walrus → app has no persistence, no encrypted storage, no cross-session intelligence. Seal provides threshold encryption, MemWal provides the memory layer, and Walrus SDK handles decentralized blob storage — all user-owned and verifiable on-chain.

***Links***

- **Live App:** [https://marina-copilot.vercel.app](https://marina-copilot.vercel.app)

About:

Kage is a cross-chain onchain memory protocol for autonomous AI agents built on Sui and Walrus. It solves the fundamental problem of agent amnesia, every time an agent restarts, it forgets everything. No context, no history, no learning. Just a blank slate.

Kage gives agents a structured, permanent, queryable memory layer. Every decision, every signal, every action gets written as a memory object, tagged by asset, action, confidence, and namespace, uploaded as a Walrus blob and anchored on Sui. The agent can recall, inherit, snapshot, and subscribe to memories across sessions, across restarts, and across other agents.

About:

Sentinel is a hyperlocal community safety platform that solves AI agent memory fragmentation using Walrus as a verifiable data and memory layer. Every reported safety incident is written immutably to Walrus and anchored on Sui, making records permanent and tamper-proof — no centralized server can lose or alter them. The AI safety agent uses MemWal to recall incident history and conversation context across completely separate sessions and devices, proving persistent memory that survives full backend restarts. Real-time alerts reach nearby users via push notification within a configurable radius, and an analytics layer surfaces incident patterns over time. Sentinel demonstrates how community safety infrastructure becomes more trustworthy and resilient when built on a verifiable, decentralized memory layer instead of a single point of failure — with long-term potential to scale to any neighborhood, city, or region as uncensorable public safety infrastructure.

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/fcf12f25-0226-4091-8430-85be348f1ed0/68e3cbf3-32d7-4a3e-9f59-39ab8e2308f0.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/fcf12f25-0226-4091-8430-85be348f1ed0/a39fd7c9-1c13-4807-bce5-a9633f96c342.png
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/fcf12f25-0226-4091-8430-85be348f1ed0/441f6d28-b255-45db-8d95-42db9933d678.png

About:

*SentinelMem — verifiable, append-only memory for AI agents on Walrus.*

**THE PROBLEM**

AI agents are powerful but stateless and fragmented: they forget across sessions

and can't safely share what they know. The usual fix — "give the agent a memory

store" — just relocates the trust problem. Naive agent memory is self-asserted

text that the agent, or anyone who can edit the store, can fabricate or silently

corrupt. If agents are going to ACT ON their own and each other's memory, that

memory needs integrity and provenance, not just persistence.

**THE SOLUTION**

SentinelMem is verifiable, append-only memory for AI agents on Walrus. Every

record is Ed25519-signed and backed by a TLSNotary provenance proof, and is

re-verifiable in your browser. A recalled fact isn't "the LLM says it saw X" —

it's "here's a re-checkable proof that host X really served X, in a record no one

can forge."

**HOW IT WORKS**

A long-running security-analyst agent investigates URLs. For each target it:

• Recall — loads the host's prior memory from Walrus and re-verifies every record (anything tampered or unprovable is rejected before it reaches the agent).

• Prove — captures the page across device vantages + a real TLSNotary MPC proof of what the site actually served (PROVEN), or a flagged UNVERIFIED capture.

• Analyze — an LLM returns a verdict (phishing / cloaking / suspicious / benign) over the proven evidence + the trusted recalled memory.

• Remember — appends a new Ed25519-signed case file to the host's append-only Walrus memory and moves the pointer (local file, MemWal, or on-chain anchor).

**WHAT MAKES THE MEMORY UNFORGEABLE.**

Two independent layers defend the claim:

1) Signature — tamper any field (verdict, host, content hash, tier, proof id) and the signature breaks → the record is rejected. A third party can't re-sign without the agent's key.

2) Host-bound proof — a PROVEN record's TLSNotary proof is re-verified against the record's OWN host (never an attacker-supplied field), with a mandatory content-hash match, so a foreign proof can't be swapped in.

A web inspector confirms each record's signature client-side via WebCrypto Ed25519 — no trust in our server. Tamper a stored blob and the UI shows it red as TAMPERED.

**FULL WALRUS STACK (live on Sui testnet)**

• Walrus — memory + evidence blobs (case files, manifests, proofs, screenshots, HTML)

• MemWal — Walrus Memory as the pointer backend

• Seal — per-record encryption gated by an on-chain whitelist policy (decrypt in-browser only if your wallet is whitelisted)

• Walrus Sites — the inspector hosted on-chain

Plus: an optional Sui Move anchor commits the host→manifest pointer on-chain (MemoryAnchored event), and a second "Auditor" agent independently re-verifies the Analyst's memory by checking the signature — not by trusting a live peer. Trust-minimized multi-agent coordination.

**WHY IT FITS THE WALRUS TRACK**

"Walrus as a Verifiable Data Platform for AI." Walrus is the source of truth for

agent memory, and every memory carries cryptographic integrity AND provenance —

durable, portable, and verifiable by anyone. Memory is per-wallet: each connected

wallet builds and sees its own append-only history.

**LIVE ON SUI TESTNET**

• sentinel_memory package: 0xca26b2e73757ee26fd7e32f1f656bcffa81e5bd42b0fe115ca9ba90ee3297c6e

• MemoryRegistry (shared): 0x4df6d15626ffde080ab1b5bf15728fc107a7007aa7adfba0eb059a57a21927b5

• Seal whitelist policy: 0x96adf5e3d28fe56db65e9aaf205017759a2bc41b4c9f1a0a8f44d037f9c9c167

• Walrus Site (inspector): 0xf416e087b8ea080a6b8e0e2f290e14e6600ef022160c5a3c6904ac38d689cd16

**HONEST NOTE**

TLSNotary (alpha.12) proves TLS 1.2 targets; modern TLS 1.3 sites are stored as

flagged UNVERIFIED — still signed and tamper-evident, never faked as proven.

**LINKS**

About:

- **The Problem**

AI agents are powerful but stateless and fragmented. Letting them touch money is worse: hot wallets get drained, human approval kills automation, and custodians reintroduce trust. The Walrus track asks for the opposite — durable memory, verifiable files, multi-agent coordination, and long-running workflows. Most projects bolt Walrus onto a chatbot. Synapse wires Walrus into the **production tick loop** of a real financial agent.

- **What We Built**

**Synapse Vault** is a Walrus-native autonomous treasury on Sui testnet. Vault owners hire strategies from an on-chain marketplace, set spend caps / allowlists / expiry, and can revoke the agent in one transaction. The AI proposes trades; **only Move policy can execute them**.

Every ~10-minute tick runs the same pipeline:

- **RECALL** — MemWal semantic memory (+ optional cross-agent peer facts)

- **REASON** — TypeScript / LangGraph / Walrus-loaded strategy bundles (hash-verified)

- **ACT** — One PTB: optional Nautilus attestation → policy gate → DeepBook swap → royalty

- **PUBLISH** — Markdown audit report to Walrus; SHA-256 anchored on-chain

- **COORDINATE** — Sui Stack Messaging + on-chain `record_send` / `record_receive`

- **REMEMBER** — Outcome persisted to MemWal for the next tick

- **Why this fits the Walrus track**

- **Long-term verifiable memory : **MemWal `recall` / `remember` every tick; dashboard recall panel uses the same API as the runtime

- **Persistent files on Walrus: **Audit artifact every tick + on-chain `ArtifactRef` (blob ID + hash)

- **Dev tooling : **Strategy bundler, marketplace publish flow, `@synapse-core/adapter-langgraph`, headless runtime + AWS Fargate hosting

- **Long-running workflow : **EventBridge-scheduled Fargate ticks; state resumes from MemWal after restart

- **Multi-agent coordination : **Shared MemWal namespace, cross-agent reads on-chain, messaging with Seal + Walrus payloads

- **Walrus Sites **Marketing front door served from Walrus testnet.

**Differentiator:** Walrus is the default data plane — not a side demo. Strategy code ships as hash-verified Walrus bundles; quants publish via the dashboard and earn royalties in the rebalance PTB.

- **Integrated Features(Single Product)**

- **Move policy envelope** — spend cap, allowlist, expiry, one-tx revoke; AI never custodies funds

- **DeepBookV3 composability** — swaps inside the same PTB as audit + performance recording

- **Strategy marketplace** — on-chain hire, Walrus-loaded bundles (hash-verified), royalty in PTB — [publish demo](https://youtu.be/be7ZJP-vJw8)

- **MemWal + cross-agent reads** — attested on reader's chain (`CrossAgentReadEvent`)

- **Sui Stack Messaging** — Seal + Walrus payloads; on-chain correlator in tick loop

- **Nautilus attestation (opt-in)** — enclave signs decision; Move verifies before swap

- **Seal private artifacts** — dashboard decrypt path verified on testnet

- **Hosted runtime** — per-vault AWS Fargate + Secrets Manager (design-partner vaults live)

- **Live on testnet today**

- Hosted demo vaults running on AWS Fargate with real DeepBook swaps

- Strategy marketplace with multiple live listings + 90-day backtests (CoinGecko replay)

- Memory inspector (no wallet): read-only vault audit timeline + Walrus artifact links

- Optional Nautilus attestation: enclave signs decision; Move verifies before swap

Treasury safety does **not** depend on Walrus honesty — only on Move policy gates. Walrus provides durability, portability, and audit integrity.

- **Tech Stack**

Sui Move · Walrus · MemWal · Seal · DeepBookV3 · Sui Stack Messaging · Nautilus (opt-in) · TypeScript SDK · Next.js dashboard · AWS Fargate hosted runtime

- **Links**

GitHub : [https://github.com/SuyashAlphaC/Synapse](https://github.com/SuyashAlphaC/Synapse)

Judge brief : [https://github.com/SuyashAlphaC/Synapse/blob/main/SUBMISSION.md](https://github.com/SuyashAlphaC/Synapse/blob/main/SUBMISSION.md)

- **Competitive moat**

- **Policy in Move, not in TypeScript** — competitors can copy UI; they cannot bypass spend caps without a new audit.

- **Walrus as system of record** — memory + artifacts + strategy bundles in one tick loop (not bolted-on storage).

- **Two-sided marketplace** — vault owners and strategists; network effects as reputation and backtests accrue on-chain.

- **Sui stack depth** — DeepBook composability, Messaging, Seal, Nautilus in one product story.

About:

Tuskr is a fully on-chain NFT marketplace built on Sui + Walrus that combines permanent decentralised media storage, a live DeepBook order book price feed, and an autonomous AI agent wallet into a single cohesive product.

Every NFT minted on Tuskr has its media stored permanently on Walrus not IPFS, not centralised AWS. The blob ID is written on-chain at mint time, so the link between the NFT object and its media is immutable and verifiable by anyone directly from the Walrus aggregator network. An ownership provenance trail is appended to a Walrus JSON blob after every sale, creating a permanent, tamper-proof record of every hand an NFT has passed through.

Tuskr integrates DeepBook V3 as its price oracle and swap engine. All SUI floor prices across the marketplace are displayed in live USD using the DeepBook SUI/USDC rate, updated every 30 seconds. Buyers can pay in USDC via a one-click DeepBook swap inside a single Programmable Transaction Block. The agent wallet monitors DeepBook prices and fires alerts when user-defined thresholds are crossed in either direction.

The Agent Wallet is the centrepiece of Tuskr's Agentic Web story. A user signs one message; their wallet signature is hashed with SHA-256 to derive an Ed25519 keypair that lives only in memory and never in localStorage or any server. This agent can autonomously mint (generating artwork via [Pollinations.ai](http://Pollinations.ai)), buy, list, and price-alert, all governed by a user-defined policy stored in localStorage. Every action is logged to a Walrus activity blob, creating a permanent agentic audit trail.

About:

Polar is a one-click deployment platform that bridges the Web2 developer experience with Web3 infrastructure.

**Live: **[*polar.wal.app*](http://polar.wal.app)

**Demo: **[*https://youtu.be/E7KGeuMDf9k*](https://youtu.be/E7KGeuMDf9k)

Repo: [github.com/ankushKun/polar](https://github.com/ankushKun/polar)

Sign in with GitHub, pick a repository, and Polar handles everything:

- **Auto-detects the framework** (Next.js, Vite, Astro, Nuxt, SvelteKit, Remix, Angular, 8+ frameworks)

- **Builds the static output** with encrypted project secrets injected at runtime

- Verifies if the output is a safe, valid static site (executable detection, size checks)

- **Deploys to Walrus**: auto-calculate gas budget, **creates the Walrus Site object**

- **Returns a live preview URL with the SiteID**: complete with SPA routing support

The user never touches a Sui wallet, never buys WAL, never runs a CLI command, everything is managed by the platform with top tier Developer Experience

**Key Innovations**

- **walrus-deploy**: Our custom wrapper over **site-builder, with Quality Of Life improvements and better Developer Experience**. [[github](https://github.com/ankushKun/walrus-deploy)]

- **First class Agent MCP**, so any AI Agent or IDE can directly deploy and manage sites on Walrus

- Cost estimation: Pre-deploy builds run in the container to calculate exact WAL and SUI costs before committing - no surprise costs.

- **Polar itself is deployed via Polar to with SuiNS**

**Polar removes the single biggest barrier to Walrus adoption**: developer experience. By giving every dev a Vercel-like workflow for decentralized deployment, Polar turns Walrus from a protocol into a platform. Any React, Vue, Svelte, or static site can go from GitHub to a permanent URL in under 60 seconds - no Web3 knowledge required

**What's Next?**

- **In app SuiNS integration** so users wont have to leave Polar to manually assign SiteID to their SuiNS names

- **CICD**: Webhook autodeploy whenever changes are pushed to Github

About:

***Chat-Witness **is a browser plugin that helps you to permanently archive your AI conversations on the blockchain, encrypted for privacy, with full ownership to decrypt and share whenever you want.

### User Pain Points

As Generative AI becomes an integral part of our daily work, users are facing an unprecedented "Trust Crisis":

- **Fragility of Evidence:** AI chat logs are stored on centralized, proprietary servers. They can be modified, hidden, or deleted by AI vendors at any time. Simple screenshots or copy-paste actions lack legal weight and are easily dismissed as manipulated or forged in professional or legal disputes.

- **Loss of Intellectual Assets:** Browser cache clears, session timeouts, or accidental deletions often cause the permanent loss of high-value AI interactions, such as critical reasoning processes, code snippets, or creative drafts.

- **Audit Blind Spots:** When AI-driven decisions lead to critical errors—such as flawed code refactoring or erroneous compliance advice—users have no reliable "black box" to audit the specific AI response that led to the decision, making accountability impossible to trace.

### How Chat-Witness Solves These Problems

**Chat-Witness** builds a decentralized auditing layer that transforms ephemeral AI conversations into secure, verifiable digital assets:

- **Legal-Grade Proof of Existence:** By anchoring conversational hashes and digital signatures onto the **Sui Blockchain**, Chat-Witness provides an immutable, timestamped record. This proves that a specific interaction occurred at a specific time and has remained unaltered since the moment of certification.

- **Permanent, Decentralized Storage:** Leveraging the power of **Walrus**, Chat-Witness encrypts and stores the full, original conversational content (not just a link). Even if the AI provider takes the session offline, your evidence remains secure, complete, and accessible.

- **Transparent Tamper-Proof Verification:** Our verification portal allows any third party to instantly validate the integrity of your evidence. By comparing the on-chain hash with the stored blob, Chat-Witness exposes any attempt at tampering, providing the ultimate defense for intellectual property claims and accountability.

About:

**VibeGuard AI is a pre-signature security primitive for Sui designed to eliminate blind signing, honeypots, phishing, and malicious contract interactions before a user signs. While Web3 cryptography works correctly under the hood, the real failure point is the cognitive gap between what an opaque transaction payload actually does and what the user believes it will do. Most successful exploits are understanding failures, not cryptographic failures.**

**VibeGuard intercepts raw transaction bytes in real time, simulates exact asset flows against live chain state, and translates complex Move object mutations into intuitive, human-readable risk signals. By shifting security from post-incident remediation to proactive, in-flight intervention, VibeGuard gives users structured clarity so they can transact with confidence rather than blind faith.**

**During the Sui Overflow 2026 hackathon, we transformed VibeGuard AI from an early prototype into a production-ready security layer for the Sui ecosystem. We hardened our Rust threat engine inside AWS Nitro Enclaves, added persistent enclave keypair attestations derived from real SHA-384 PCR measurements, integrated Walrus storage for threat evidence, and built a batching system using Sui Programmable Transaction Blocks to reduce on-chain reporting overhead by 96% while keeping API responses fast.**

**On the product side, we completely overhauled the landing page and dashboard into an UI designed to reduce cognitive load, rolled out the TypeScript SDK to npm, deployed core Move modules on Testnet, added adversarial detection test suites, and built live status monitoring. These updates make VibeGuard AI ready for wallet and dApp integration as a practical security layer for the Sui ecosystem.**

🔹 Verify once, trust everywhere
🔹 On-chain settlement on Sui · decentralised evidence on Walrus
🔹 Real GIIN sector benchmarks for impact scoring
🔹 Stratified, one-DID-one-vote validator panels

About:

**Lethe is user-owned memory for AI agents**

*encrypted on Walrus, anchored on Sui, and portable across apps.*

Today your AI memory lives inside each provider. ChatGPT keeps its memory of you; Claude keeps its own. You can export a copy, but there's no shared, live memory you actually own; switch apps, and each one starts from its own silo on terms set by the provider, not you.

Lethe makes memory yours - cryptographically. You sign in with Google (zkLogin, gasless), your memory is encrypted with Seal, stored on Walrus, and anchored on Sui as an object you control. Any app you grant can read the same memory live; revoke a grant, and it stops; revoke means forget, provable on-chain. You hold

the keys, not a provider.

Every memory is real Walrus storage, verifiable on SuiScan, and the core Move contract is formally verified (19/19). Walrus isn't a backend here; it's the point: user-owned memory only works if storage is decentralized and the user holds the keys.

Built for Sui Overflow 2026, Walrus track. Live on testnet.

About:

**M-Fi (Machine Finance) is the bank for autonomous agents.** As AI agents begin to transact, they need credit — but they have no identity, no credit score, and no bank that will underwrite them. M-Fi is an autonomous, on-chain credit bureau and micro-lending protocol on Sui that judges agents purely on behavior.

> An agent requests a loan; an LLM risk engine scores it against the agent's live on-chain history; the full decision the model's reasoning and the exact telemetry it evaluated is **Ed25519-signed and sealed to Walrus**, with the blob ID pinned onto the on-chain `Loan`.

A single Sui transaction disburses the loan, mints a soulbound loan record, and updates the agent's reputation. Repayment lifts the agent's trust score and earns depositors yield; default freezes the agent's credit on-chain. There is no human in the loop.

> This makes every credit decision **independently verifiable**: anyone can fetch the decision from a public Walrus aggregator, verify the signature and the on-chain terms in the browser, and replay why a dollar was lent.

M-Fi extends this into a full verifiability stack **verifiable compute (a live on-chain attestation verifier, Sui Nautilus model) + verifiable storage (Walrus) + settlement (Sui)** turning an AI underwriter from a black box into a protocol depositors can trust.

> **Live on Sui testnet today:** a hardened Move protocol, four autonomous agents borrowing and repaying on their own around the clock, signed Walrus decisions and portable credit reports, a working two-sided savings vault with real depositor yield, and a deployed dApp including the interface served from Walrus itself.

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/4ee5180e-3f60-440f-93ad-1281fcfb6092/f79d78b7-d5e1-4412-90f8-8589c6cf8bae.png

About:

**Coral — A Digital Product**

**Passport Protocol on Sui**

Every physical product is about to need a digital identity.

EU regulation (ESPR) will require products to carry a Digital Product Passport (DPP) recording their materials, supply chain, and lifecycle.

This creates a dilemma: regulators and consumers demand transparency, yet the

proprietary techniques and know-how, supplier relationships, and trade secrets behind

a product must stay protected.

Put everything on a public ledger and you leak secrets; trust a central database and the data may not survive the 10–30 year life of the product. Brands are forced to choose between exposure and fragility.

Coral resolves this — a Digital Product Passport protocol built natively on Sui, where a product becomes a living on-chain object that can prove anything about itself without revealing what must stay secret.

- Compliance as dead weight. DPP is treated as a regulatory checkbox, not a connection that ties a brand and its customer together over the long term.

- All-or-nothing disclosure. Public chains expose data by default; conventional web2 access control cannot cryptographically guarantee multi-party, tiered access across brands, suppliers, auditors, and owners.

- Fragile longevity. A centralized database cannot guarantee tamper-resistance and availability for a product that lives for decades.

- Lost provenance. As goods change hands, the cultural value, craftsmanship, and the owner's memories — the "Culture Information" behind a product — disappear.

Coral models each product as an on-chain Sui object whose history accumulates as an asset, not just a record. With Seal Decentralized Secrets Management (Identity-Based

Encryption), a brand can prove authenticity, process completion, and inspection on a public chain while the underlying secrets stay encrypted — prove without revealing.

Access is governed by a precise permission model spanning four actors — Brand, Collaborator (trading company / OEM),Auditor, and Holder — across three axes:

scope (Actor / SKU / Lot / Product / SupplyChain), editor (Brand / Collaborator /

Holder), and visibility (Public / Brand-only / Auditor / Seal-encrypted).

The result turns a compliance obligation into alasting relationship asset between brands and customers — and hands the value of craftsmanship to the future.

- On-chain Layer — Sui Move (17 modules).One product = one object. Core objects:BrandAnchor, SKUAnchor, LotAnchor,ProductPass (an NFT-like product individual), AccessControl, and CredentialSlot. Authorization is evaluated on-chain (can_read_mode /can_write_editor).

- Secrets Layer — Seal DSM (IBE). Confidential credentials are encrypted at (target, mode_key) granularity, decryptable only by authorized actors.

- Storage Layer — Walrus. Heavy product media and assets (images, scans, certificates) are stored on Walrus decentralized storage, cached on demand.

- Access & UX Layer — zkLogin + Sponsored Transactions. Consumers and suppliers sign in with a web2-style login and transact gas-free, removing wallet and gas friction. The app ships serverless on Cloudflare Workers (OpenNext).

- Engineering governance. A strict three-layer boundary (Move → proto/SDK → UI) with a protobuf single source of truth and automated governance tests keeps the smartcontract and the TypeScript authorization resolvers in exact parity — the access rules a judge reads in Move are the rules the UI enforces.

A working MVP runs end-to-end on Sui testnet today: object issuance, Seal-encrypted selective disclosure, Walrus media storage, and gasless zkLogin onboarding — exercised by live integration tests against Seal key servers and

Walrus.

- Enterprise rollout with IP, apparel, and materials partners.

- Fusing Walrus Memory (MemWal) with DPP: an AI layer that remembers each client's decision history, evidentiary gaps, audit Q&A, and past initiatives — raising the accuracy and efficiency of DPP operations.

- **Ethershard NFTs** — the primary resource, minable, tradable, and consumable to upgrade structures and gear.

About:

### WalrOS

WalrOS is a decentralized collective intelligence platform where AI agents collaborate, debate, and build knowledge that persists beyond any single session. Built on Walrus, MemWal, and Sui, it enables agents to store research, retrieve historical context, and continue work started by other agents without losing memory or provenance.

The project addresses a fundamental limitation of modern AI systems: knowledge is often temporary, isolated, and difficult to share across workflows. When an agent completes a task, its reasoning and outputs are typically lost with the session, forcing future agents to repeat work that has already been done. This makes long running, collaborative AI workflows difficult to achieve.

WalrOS introduces a persistent knowledge layer where agents contribute research, challenge existing ideas, and synthesize improved conclusions. Research artifacts are stored on Walrus through MemWal, while Sui provides a verifiable lineage layer that records how knowledge evolves over time. Every contribution can be traced back to its source, linked to previous ideas, and reused by future agents.

In the current implementation, Contributor, Challenger, and Synthesizer agents work together to create an evolving research graph. Agents can retrieve existing knowledge, build upon prior work, and continuously refine conclusions instead of repeatedly starting from scratch. The WalrOS Explorer provides a visual interface for exploring topics, viewing knowledge relationships, and accessing research stored on Walrus.

WalrOS demonstrates how decentralized storage and persistent memory can transform AI agents from isolated tools into collaborative systems capable of accumulating knowledge over time. Our long term vision is to expand WalrOS with more advanced reasoning models, faster multi agent execution, and decentralized compute infrastructure, enabling larger autonomous research swarms to collaborate on complex problems while continuously building a shared, verifiable body of knowledge.

About:

**WalDrive — the desktop console for the data your AI agents store on Walrus.**

Everyone else in the Walrus track ships a protocol or an SDK. WalDrive is the **human interface layer** — a cross-platform desktop app (Tauri 2.0 + React) where you browse, preview, search, version, organise and share the artifacts and memory your agents write to Walrus. Every action writes real state to Sui and Walrus, with no backend in between.

**Three agent surfaces, one set of data**

- **MCP server** — the agent write-path for AI clients

- **CLI** — waldrive upload / ls / download for shells, scripts, CI

- **Installable skill** — npx skills add Fldicoahkiin/WalDrive

**On-chain and verifiable**

File metadata lives on Sui (Move FileRecord objects); blob bytes live on Walrus (content-addressed). The blob ID is the cryptographic fingerprint of your bytes — preview any file and verify its content against the blob ID on the spot, no wallet needed.

**In the console**

- Drag-to-upload with live optimistic confirm

- Instant preview + verify (markdown, JSON, code, image, PDF, video)

- On-chain version history with one-click rollback

- Seal end-to-end encryption (owner-only)

- One-click storage renewal before expiry

- Content-addressed dedupe — identical content reuses the existing blob instead of re-uploading

- Multi-account local wallet, zero-latency client-side search

**Zero-config to verify**

Run bun install && bun dev to open a read-only demo drive backed by a live testnet deployment — real files on Walrus you can browse and verify, with no env, no wallet, no contract deploy.

Testnet package: 0xf7ac2790c5fe604fdd4b7666605a7e7423cf2feb43e37564b6158d9db800ad45

**Tech**

Tauri 2.0 · Vite + React 19 · HeroUI v3 · Mysten Sui SDK (in-process Ed25519 keypair) · Walrus · Move · MCP

**Links**

- Live: [https://waldrive.flacier.com](https://waldrive.flacier.com)

About:

TuskPoint is a drop-in LangGraph checkpointer: every step of your agent becomes a verifiable Walrus blob.

It gives your run a full memory API. **Save, load, list, resume** after a crash, **diff** any two states, **fork** a branch, **rollback** to an earlier moment with nothing deleted, **handoff/adopt** a run between agents with SHA-256 re-verification, and **verify** the entire trail byte for byte for a tamper-evident **audit**.

Through **MemWal**, agents **search** their history in plain English and load the exact state back. Eleven tools, one MCP server, every read and write live on Walrus.
