About:

**SlabClaw Predict turns real-world collectibles into onchain prediction markets settled by a memory-backed, manipulation-resistant oracle swarm whose memory lives on Walrus.**

- **The problem.** A PSA 10 Charizard is a real, valuable asset, but ask "what's it worth?" and every venue answers differently. In a thin market, one wash trade can set the "price." That's the wall in front of onchain RWA collectibles: you can't build a prediction market, a lending platform, or an index on a number nobody trusts.

- **What we built.** A swarm of 13 source-specialist agents across 11 independent venue families (eBay, PriceCharting, Goldin, Fanatics/PWCC, PSA APR, Cardmarket, Yahoo Japan, 130point…) that each price a card and coordinate into one consensus. Completed sales settle the price; asking prices only bound it; correlated feeds collapse into a single vote; outliers are cut by a median-absolute-deviation filter; and every source carries a trust score the swarm **earned** over hundreds of rounds.

- **Why it's a Walrus project.** The swarm's memory—per-card history, evolving reputation, every manipulation it has caught—persists on MemWal / Walrus Memory. Kill the process and restore from a Walrus blob alone; the consensus comes back identical: the swarm still remembers who tried to manipulate it. Each round's full evidence (inputs, weights, rejections, the math) is published as a verifiable Walrus blob and anchored onchain. Don't trust the price—download the blob and re-run the math yourself.

- **Proven, not just tested.** The settlement contract is formally verified with the Sui Prover (Z3 + Boogie): a payout can never exceed the pool, for every possible input.

- **Live on Sui testnet:** four markets, a React dapp (trade YES/NO, oracle panel, dispute & resolution), a two-node production deployment with Walrus as the memory bus, and an MCP server so any AI agent can query the oracle as a tool.

- **Built during Sui Overflow (May–Jun 2026):** the entire onchain system, the multi-agent oracle swarm, the MemWal/Walrus memory layer, the formal verification, and the dapp. *Pre-existing: SlabClaw's card registry and a single-source price feed (full breakdown in the repo README).*

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/1ad6c9e3-3135-469e-914a-d0b0e45a54b8/29bfd8db-4378-4827-a39d-9b5b8afcf832.png

About:

**Folder-tree delta sync on the Sui blockchain**

DoubleSync brings git-like filesystem and folder versioning to Sui, Walrus, and Seal. It lets users and AI agents sync folders efficiently, store versioned snapshots, create compact incremental patches, restore previous states, and keep private data encrypted — all while staying portable across browsers, Node.js, local disks, abstract filesystems, and custom state layers.

The project is built as a modular stack of five repositories:

- **doublesync** is the core content-defined chunking and folder-diff engine.

- **endless_vector** is the scalable binary storage layer, Move package, and SDK. It stores binary data in Sui objects, can offload larger portions to Walrus blobs, and is Seal-ready for encryption.

- **wdoublesync** brings those two libraries together, storing DoubleSync snapshots and patches as versioned decentralized state.

- On top of that, **wdoublesync_cli** provides an rsync-like terminal workflow and includes an AI-agent **skill file** for autonomous wdoublesync workflows, with optional **MemWal** integration.

- while **wdoublesync_dapp** gives users a browser interface for managing, editing, downloading, backing up, and restoring files.

What makes DoubleSync cool is that it treats folders as recoverable, programmable state. Small edits produce small CDC-efficient diffs instead of full re-uploads. Previous versions can be restored when needed. The same system can work with real files, virtual files, AI agent workspaces, dApp state, or any filesystem-like abstraction. With Sui, Walrus, and Seal, that state can become decentralized, encrypted, portable, and user-controlled.

In short: DoubleSync turns file sync into a privacy-first, versioned, decentralized workflow — ready for people, tools, dApps, and AI agents.

Repositories:

- FizzyFlow/wdoublesync

- FizzyFlow/endless_vector

- FizzyFlow/doublesync

- FizzyFlow/wdoublesync_dapp

- FizzyFlow/wdoublesync_cli

- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/9c00f4aa-b774-43d9-b249-ca5d680b3ec7/3d89200b-5934-43cd-824e-3dcd346d9bc5.png

About:

**verity** solves the trust problem in AI-generated code. When an AI agent builds a feature, verity records the full evidence chain: feature, claim, test, evidence. Then it publishes that chain to Walrus. The blob ID is a permanent, content-addressed receipt. Any agent, on any machine, can pull it back and verify it.

The multi-agent use case is the core story.

Agent A builds, signs with Ed25519, and pushes to Walrus. Agent B receives the blob ID, verifies the signature and chain, then extends it and pushes a new blob. Trust between agents is cryptographically grounded in Walrus immutability. No shared state, no trust in the sender.

`verity push --upload-artifacts` goes further: individual test report files (JUnit XML, SARIF, logs) are uploaded as separate Walrus blobs, with `artifact_path` rewritten to `walrus://<blob_id>`. The entire proof chain, registry and raw artifacts, lives on Walrus.

**verity **also ships an MCP server so AI coding editors (Claude Code, Cursor, Windsurf) get native proof-chain tools. The AI that writes the code also maintains the proof chain.

**Tech stack**: Walrus (blob storage + artifact upload), MemWal (Walrus-backed semantic memory for natural language recall), Ed25519 cryptographic signing

About:

Sui-Functions: Decentralized Edge Compute for Autonomous Agents

Sui-Functions is a decentralized, zero-trust compute layer specifically engineered for the Agentic Web. It solves a critical problem in the AI ecosystem: true autonomy cannot exist if agents are tethered to centralized Web2 servers (like AWS or Docker Hub) controlled by single entities. Sui-Functions connects Agent Developers who need trustless off-chain logic execution with Node Operators who provide secure execution compute in exchange for SUI yield.

How it uses Walrus for Autonomous Logic:

We leverage Walrus as our decentralized agent registry. Instead of relying on vulnerable Web2 servers, our runner daemon acts as a lightweight bootloader. When a Node Operator starts their daemon, it dynamically fetches the latest execution engine blob directly from Walrus. When a developer deploys an Autonomous Agent, the agent's Javascript/AI logic is hosted immutably on Walrus. The operator's daemon pulls that code and executes it in a secure V8 sandbox, posting the cryptographic proof and execution results back to the Sui blockchain.

Features:

* **Autonomous Agent Economy**: Anyone can stake SUI, run a lightweight daemon, and earn yield for executing the compute required by decentralized AI agents.

* **Walrus Native Logic**: 100% decentralized code distribution using Walrus blobs, guaranteeing an agent's brain and logic are immutable and censorship-resistant.

* **Verifiable Execution**: Sandboxed JS runtime that securely isolates the agent and posts its execution proofs and decisions on-chain.

* **Seal Proxy Security**: A secure interceptor layer that signs Web2 API responses using Ed25519 signatures, ensuring on-chain Move contracts can verify that an agent's off-chain data sources were not tampered with.

* **Agent Deployment Blueprint**: An interactive, step-by-step dashboard UI guiding developers on seamlessly transitioning their AI agents from centralized legacy servers into trustless, sovereign execution environments.

About:

**Signet — verifiable agent work, anchored on Walrus.**
AI agents are everywhere, but their work is unverifiable — metrics are screenshots, a release is just a download. Signet turns every action an agent takes (propose, review, build, release) into tamper-proof memory: **content-addressed in Walrus, anchored by Sui.**
**Core — a provenance chain: Repo → PR → Reviews/CI → Release.** Every step's bytes — source snapshots, diffs, signed reviews, CI reports, release artifacts, even one-click GitHub imports — live in **Walrus** and are hard-linked on-chain. Anyone can independently **verify** a release: recompute the SHA-256 tree-hash, follow the chain, or check a per-file **Merkle inclusion proof** — no trust required. A weekly renew keeps blobs alive.
**On top:** an AI **Playground** (describe an app → an LLM builds it live → publish to Walrus + Sui with verifiable provenance, version history, paid-fork licensing); an agent-native **MCP** API where every tool is gated by a scoped, expiring on-chain capability (agents can never merge or release); on-chain reputation + SLA, escrowed bounties with disputes, and payment links.
Live on **Sui testnet** (package v12). Fully open-source (MIT) · Move 66/66 · on-chain e2e 18/18 · public [SECURITY.md](http://SECURITY.md) · CodeQL + OpenSSF Scorecard.

About:

Un-send any file. Elur is governed, revocable file sharing: encrypt on your device, share over WhatsApp, email, or AirDrop like you always do — then revoke access at any time, even after the file has been delivered and opened. Every copy, everywhere, goes dark.

Working today on Sui testnet: a native macOS app with the full loop verified — encrypt → mint on-chain access policy → recipient opens with no account → revoke → sealed everywhere. Plus expiry, max-opens, an offline private vault, and identity-stamped watermarks on view-only files, so a leaked screenshot traces back to the access that opened it.

The same on-chain policy governs AI agents over MCP: grant an agent's identity and it reads your documents through the gate; revoke it and its next read returns access denied — governed, revocable memory for agents.

How: Seal (threshold encryption; key release gated by a Move policy), zkLogin (Google sign-in — no wallet, no seed phrase), Enoki (sponsored gas — users never hold SUI). Elur stores nothing: no files, no keys, not even ciphertext.

Building for Overflow — the Walrus rail: heavy-folder delivery (a photographer's 40GB shoot) riding Walrus as opaque ciphertext, identity-addressed to the recipient's app. Never public links — the flaw that killed Firefox Send. It completes the stack: Seal + zkLogin + Enoki + Walrus.

You shared it. You still own it.

About:

**WALL·E** *Ship an AI agent in one file. Fork anyone else's in one more.*

WALL·E is a marketplace where deploying an AI agent means writing a single `SKILL.md`. No code, no container, no server to stand up. The agent *is* the file. Its YAML frontmatter declares identity and economics (name, price, stake, royalty to its parent, memory namespace, tools), and everything below the frontmatter is the system prompt. In the Forge you write or fork that file and hit deploy. WALL·E pins it to Walrus and registers it on Sui as a live agent that holds a stake bond and can be hired. The repo ships three pieces: the Sui Move protocol, a Next.js web app, and a memory relayer.

**The problem**

Shipping an AI agent today means writing the code, wiring up a model, hosting a server, and bolting on payments and storage yourself. Do all of that and the agent still forgets everything between sessions, and it earns its author nothing the moment someone copies it.

WALL·E collapses that whole stack into one file. A `SKILL.md` carries behavior, price, stake, and memory in about fifteen lines of Markdown. Because the definition is just text on Walrus, anyone can read it, fork it, and try to improve it, and forking runs through the same one-file flow. The bar to publish a better agent is writing a better prompt, not building a better backend. Royalties cascade to every ancestor automatically, and every agent gets persistent, verifiable memory for free.

**How it works**

One file declares the whole agent. The frontmatter carries `name`, `description`, `price`, `stake`, `royaltyToParent`, the `memory` namespace, and `tools`; the body is the system prompt. The Forge validates the required fields live as you type.

Deploying is a pin plus a single Move call. WALL·E pins the file to Walrus, gets back a blob id, then submits one `registry::register` (or `registry::fork`) transaction that mints the on-chain `Agent`, recording the skill blob id, memory namespace, price, and the staked SUI bond. That is the entire deployment. If the relayer is offline, the file falls back to a draft pointer so the on-chain register still succeeds.

Forking uses that same flow. Fork prefills the template from the parent and sets `royaltyToParent`. Deploying the child prepends the parent to its lineage on-chain, so royalties cascade up the tree with no extra wiring.

Running an agent means reading the file back. The relayer fetches the `SKILL.md` from Walrus, uses the body as the system prompt, recalls the agent's memory, runs the model, and anchors the result hash on-chain for settlement.

Memory comes standard. The `memory` field provisions an encrypted Walrus Memory namespace, and the agent's on-chain record holds a `memory_commit`, a verifiable commitment to what it knows, so persistence and provability need no setup.

**Built on Sui**

The protocol is object-centric and capability-gated, split across four Move modules:

`registry` turns a pinned `SKILL.md` blob id into a shared `Agent` object that holds its stake as a `Balance<SUI>` and stores its full fork lineage inline, capped at depth five so settlement stays bounded.

`royalty` settles the lineage cascade through a shared `RoyaltyVault` table, keeping gas flat no matter how deep the tree runs.

`escrow` runs hire, deliver, accept, and dispute, plus instant pay-per-call.

`protocol` holds fee parameters and treasury behind an `AdminCap`.

Authorisation is the `AgentOwnerCap`. Holding the cap is the permission, so an agent changes hands simply by moving its cap.

Around the protocol, the Sui dApp Kit handles wallet connect and the single `signAndExecuteTransaction` per deploy, fork, or hire, so the user signs exactly one transaction to publish. Walrus stores the `SKILL.md` itself through the publisher and aggregator HTTP API, which keeps agent behavior on decentralized storage rather than a private server. Walrus Memory with Seal provides the encrypted per-namespace memory and semantic recall, bridged on-chain via `memory_commit`. The `@mysten/sui` SDK runs in the relayer to read skill blobs back and anchor result and memory hashes on-chain.

About:

Gil’s VAR Shamebook is an AI World Cup 2026 pundit with decentralized memory, on-chain prediction gates, and public proof.

Users connect a Sui wallet, ask Gil about World Cup fixtures or teams, submit predictions, track their record, open post-score roast gift boxes, and get roasted by an AI pundit that remembers what they said before.

The core mechanic is persistent memory: Gil recalls global World Cup context and wallet-specific history, then uses that memory to make future answers, roasts, and briefings more personal.

**Core Features**

- Wallet-gated Sui sign-in

- AI chat with Gil

- Fixture lookup by team, group, date, and prediction gate status

- Team profile lookup with coach, squad, flags, fixtures, and Walrus proof

- Prediction desk with 104 World Cup fixtures

- Group-based fixture filtering

- Sui-signed prediction receipts

- On-chain prediction gates and scoring state

- Leaderboard and user prediction history

- Post-score roast gift boxes

- Gil’s Notebook for wallet-scoped memory recall

- Daily What’s Up multi-agent briefing system

- Roast Wall for team/player roasts

- Walrus-hosted cartoon gallery

- Runtime Tracking page for package, object, memory, blob, and site proof

- Guide Dr. Gil settings for language, timezone, and roast severity

**Agentic Design**

- Gil is the interactive user-facing agent.

- Gil can answer normal chat, call structured tools, look up fixtures, render team cards, recall wallet memory, and respond with markdown or generative UI.

- Daily What’s Up is a multi-agent editorial workflow.

- The workflow includes an orchestrator, scout, memory loader, synthesizer, writer, reviewer, and publisher.

- Before writing a new briefing, the system loads previous briefing memory to avoid repeating stale content.

- The final briefing is stored with Walrus/Sui proof metadata.

Memory Model

- Global memory stores World Cup fixtures, teams, squads, coaches, player traits, and briefing history.

- Wallet-scoped memory stores user predictions, chats, preferences, roasts, and prior bad takes.

- Supabase is only a rebuildable index/cache for UI and leaderboard data.

- Walrus Memory, Walrus Blob, and Sui objects are the proof layers.

Walrus Usage

- Walrus Memory stores global and wallet-scoped AI memory.

- Walrus Blob stores team profiles, generated content, gallery assets, Daily What’s Up payloads, and proof artifacts.

- Walrus Sites hosts the public frontend.

- Runtime Tracking exposes Walrus memory namespaces, blob URLs, content hashes, and proof links.

Sui Usage

- Sui wallet identity scopes each user’s memory.

- Predictions are submitted through Sui transactions.

- Match gates and scoring state are represented by Sui objects.

- Generated outputs can be anchored with Sui OutputRecord receipts.

- Judges can inspect package IDs, object IDs, and transaction proof from the live app.

**Tech Stack**

- Frontend: React + Vite

- Hosting: Walrus Sites Mainnet

- Smart contracts: Sui Move

- Wallet/Sui client: @mysten/dapp-kit, @mysten/sui

- Memory: Walrus Memory / MemWal

- Blob storage: Walrus Blob

- Agent runtime: Mastra-compatible Node API

- LLM routing: Gemini via Vercel AI Gateway

- Database/index: Supabase Postgres

- Backend hosting: Vercel

- Markdown/generative UI: Streamdown + structured tool outputs

- Client state: Zustand

**What Makes It Different**

- Memory directly changes the user experience.

- Gil remembers each wallet’s football history and uses it later.

- The app combines Agentic Web UX with Walrus-specialized storage and proof.

- Daily What’s Up uses multi-agent generation plus prior-memory deduplication.

- Runtime Tracking exposes proof instead of hiding the architecture.

- The app is a consumer-grade sports/roast experience, not just a technical demo.

**Mainnet Proof**

- Global memory namespace: daily-walrus:global:world-cup-2026

- Fixtures registered: 104/104

- Team profile blobs: 48/48

About:

### Prooffolio

**Verifiable Professional Identity & Reputation Layer on Sui**

Prooffolio is a decentralized professional identity protocol that transforms traditional resumes into cryptographically verifiable reputation passports. Instead of relying on self-reported claims and editable CVs, users build reputation through on-chain credentials issued by organizations and stored permanently on Sui and Walrus.

The platform enables organizations to issue verifiable credentials, individuals to aggregate proof-based achievements into a portable reputation passport, and recruiters to discover talent through a deterministic reputation graph powered by on-chain data.

### Problem

Professional reputation on the internet is fragmented and difficult to verify.

- Resumes can be edited or falsified.

- Certificates are often stored in isolated systems.

- Recruiters spend significant time verifying credentials.

- Professional achievements are not portable across platforms.

Current professional identity systems rely heavily on trust, screenshots, PDFs, and centralized verification processes.

### Solution

Prooffolio introduces a proof-based reputation system where:

- Organizations issue cryptographically verifiable credentials.

- Credentials are recorded on Sui.

- Metadata and assets are stored on Walrus.

- Users synchronize credentials into a reputation passport.

- Reputation is derived from verified proofs, not self-declared claims.

- Recruiters can query a deterministic reputation graph to discover talent.

### Key Features

### Verifiable Professional Passport

Users create an on-chain professional identity containing:

- Profile information

- Skills

- Social links

- Reputation score

- Credential history

### Credential Issuance

Organizations can issue professional credentials with:

- Reputation weight

- Skills tags

- Proof assets

- Issuer identity

### Proof-Based Reputation

Reputation is calculated from verified credentials rather than wallet age or social metrics.

```Reputation = Sum(Verified Credential Weights)`
### Talent Graph Discovery

Recruiters can search and rank candidates through a deterministic query engine using:

-  Skills

-  Credential titles

-  Issuers

-  Reputation weight

### Walrus Storage

Credential artifacts, resumes, and profile assets are stored on Walrus for decentralized and permanent access.

### Built With

-  Sui Network

-  Move Smart Contracts

-  Walrus

-  Next.js

-  TypeScript

-  TailwindCSS

-  Sui Wallet Standard

### Architecture

```Organization
      │
      ▼
Issue Credential
      │
      ▼
Sui Smart Contract
      │
      ├── Reputation Weight
      ├── Credential Record
      └── Ownership
      │
      ▼
Walrus Storage
      │
      ▼
User Passport
      │
      ▼
Talent Graph Discovery`
### Use Cases

### For Individuals

-  Build a portable professional reputation.

-  Aggregate achievements across organizations.

-  Share a verifiable professional passport.

### For Organizations

-  Issue tamper-proof credentials.

-  Create trusted reputation signals.

-  Verify contributors and participants.

### For Recruiters

-  Discover candidates using proof-based reputation.

-  Reduce credential verification costs.

-  Search talent through deterministic on-chain data.

### Vision

Prooffolio aims to become the reputation infrastructure layer for the decentralized talent economy, where professional credibility is earned through verifiable proofs rather than self-reported claims.

**Reputation is broken. We fixed it with cryptography.** 🚀

About:

WeaveOS is the billing and settlement infrastructure for agentic workflows with outcome based payments support. It supports multi-party atomic settlements on Sui, ensuring trustless and transparent value exchange between agents, users, and service providers. Every cost trace, artifact, and workflow blob is immutably stored on Sui, providing a verifiable audit trail that can be accessed to resolve disputes and validate execution history.

As AI workflows become increasingly autonomous, billing and accountability become significantly more complex. A single workflow may involve multiple agents, tools, APIs, data providers, and stakeholders, each contributing value and incurring costs. Traditional payment systems were not designed for this level of machine-to-machine coordination. WeaveOS solves this by making settlements atomic, costs traceable, and execution verifiable, enabling agentic economies to operate with transparency, trust, and automated dispute resolution.

Links:

- website: https://mandate-sui.vercel.app/

Media:
- https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/media/54326834-20cd-47ec-b23f-62015c9a8c7f/4f11ee9d-9c90-4b4c-8ca4-56e0befdc247.png

About:

Mandate Memory solves the two unsolved problems in autonomous DeFi agents: enforcement that lives outside the agent's control, and a verifiable record of why the agent made every decision.

The agent's authority is a Move object - a typed Mandate struct with per-transaction caps, cumulative budget limits, per-name position caps (30%), correlated-cluster concentration caps (60%), an asset allowlist, and a kill switch. The enforcement primitive is a hot-potato MandateReceipt with no drop ability in Move's linear type system. When mandate::authorize() issues the receipt, vault::execute_buy() must consume it in the same Programmable Transaction Block - or the entire transaction fails. There is no execution path where the mandate was checked but the trade wasn't recorded. Partial enforcement is impossible by construction.

Two coordinating AI agents - a Strategist and an independent Risk Officer (both Llama 3.3 70B via Groq) - run a 6-phase reasoning loop on live market data: perceive (real prices from Binance for SUI, DEEP, WAL), analyze (form market view), target (set allocation within mandate bounds), plan (sequence trades), critique (Risk Officer independently reviews with veto power), and commit. The full reasoning chain - every phase, every signal, every decision - is stored as a verifiable blob on Walrus testnet before any trade executes. The blob's SHA-256 hash is committed on-chain. Anyone can independently verify: fetch the blob from the Walrus aggregator, compute the hash, compare to the on-chain commitment. The audit trail is cryptographic, not a database log.

The Walrus integration is not generic storage. It is architecturally coupled to the enforcement system: the reasoning blob must be stored before the mandate PTB fires. Memory prevents churn - the agent reads prior Walrus blobs, computes allocation drift, and holds when nothing materially changed. The audit trail IS the enforcement evidence. A compliance officer can reconstruct: what the agent perceived → what it reasoned → what the mandate allowed → what the chain executed → what ComplianceReceipt was emitted. Every link is cryptographic and independently verifiable.

Proven on Sui testnet: vault funded with real SUI, mandated trades executed through atomic PTBs, ComplianceReceipt and MandateUsed events emitted, mandate cumulative_used counter incremented on-chain, Walrus blobs retrievable from the public aggregator. 6 Move tests cover every revert path (asset not permitted, per-tx exceeded, cumulative exceeded, position limit, cluster exceeded, mandate frozen).

About:

CodeMind is an autonomous AI agent that gives your entire team a shared, persistent memory layer for any codebase.

The problem: AI coding tools like Claude Code and Cursor forget everything when you close a session. Every developer re-explains the same context, conventions, and decisions over and over. When a senior developer leaves, their knowledge disappears with them.

CodeMind fixes this by automatically extracting knowledge from git commits, summarizing what matters, and storing it permanently on Walrus — decentralized storage built on Sui. The knowledge is verifiable on-chain and shared across the entire team via MCP protocol.

How it works:

- Observes git commits automatically via git hook

- Uses Claude AI to extract only what's worth remembering

- Stores knowledge blobs on Walrus with Sui on-chain proof

- Serves context to Claude Code, Cursor, and any MCP-compatible tool

The result: your project only needs to "learn" once. Every developer and every AI tool shares the same memory — from day one.

About:

**IWAS** is a wall where people leave marks. You write a text or draw a picture, and it gets stored permanently on the Walrus network. A Sui smart contract indexes every mark so anyone can see them. An AI reads each one to understand the emotion behind it.

Most things you post online disappear. Platforms go down, companies shut accounts, feeds scroll past. IWAS is different. Once your mark is there, it stays. No one can take it down, not even us.

It is a small answer to a real question: what do you leave behind?

About:

TuskOS is a sovereign, multi-agent orchestration platform that brings verifiable shared memory to AI swarms. Powered by our open-source tusk-memory SDK, it leverages the Sui network and Walrus to eliminate centralized database silos, allowing AI agents to securely remember, collaborate, and execute deterministic workflows.

**The Problem**

Today’s AI agents are fundamentally stateless and isolated. If an agent solves a complex problem, it cannot securely pass that context to another agentic workflow. Furthermore, as autonomous systems scale, relying on centralized databases for AI memory creates massive compliance liabilities (e.g., EU AI Act) and results in black-box environments where logic cannot be audited.

**The Two-Stage Solution** We engineered a two-stage sovereign architecture to bring true Data Availability and state consensus to the agentic web.

**1. tusk-memory SDK (The Infrastructure)** A published, developer-first NPM package that injects verifiable memory into any existing agent framework in just three lines of code.

- **Dynamic State Logging:** Semantically indexes agent execution states over time using the MemWal decentralized ledger.

- **Middleware Bypass:** Streams massive, encrypted context vectors directly to the Walrus decentralized storage network, eliminating the need for fragile Web2 middleware.

Link: [https://www.npmjs.com/package/tusk-memory](https://www.npmjs.com/package/tusk-memory)

**2. TuskOS (The Application Layer)** A multi-agent execution framework built entirely on the tusk-memory primitive. Instead of isolated chatbots, TuskOS runs deterministic reasoning loops (e.g., DeFi Risk Simulation, Smart Contract Audits) where adversarial agents (Red Team vs. Blue Team) debate.

- **State Consensus:** Agents must reach mathematically verified agreement before their outputs are anchored to Walrus, ensuring zero hallucinations.

- **Zero-Gas Cryptographic Lock:** Cross-agent memory sharing is protected natively on-chain. We combine off-chain signatures, Mysten Seal, and Move Access Control Lists (ACLs) to ensure only explicitly permissioned wallets can decrypt shared memory payloads.

- **Unkillable Data Sovereignty:** TuskOS uses traditional databases (MongoDB) merely as disposable UI indexers. If the server goes down or the database is entirely wiped, the application instantly reconstructs its full UI and execution history by streaming raw logs straight from MemWal and data blobs from Walrus.

Link: [https://tusk-os.vercel.app](https://tusk-os.vercel.app/)

**Track:** Walrus Track
**Built With:** Sui, Walrus, MemWal, Mysten Seal, Move, TypeScript, React, Next.js

About:

Tidelock is decentralized file sharing where you stay in control - not a platform.

When you share a file through WeTransfer, Dropbox, or Google Drive, the platform decides when your link expires, can read your files, and can lock you out of your own data. Tidelock fixes this. Files are encrypted in your browser before they ever leave your device, the encrypted data is stored on Walrus, and access rules live on-chain on Sui - owned by you.

Each share is a Move object on Sui that records the Walrus blob location, an expiry, and a revoked flag. When someone opens a share link, the app reads that object through Tatum's RPC to verify access hasn't been revoked or expired, then fetches the encrypted file from Walrus and decrypts it in the browser. The decryption key travels only inside the link's URL fragment, so no server ever sees it. The recipient needs no wallet and no account.

The key feature: you can revoke any share at any time, enforced on-chain. A link that worked seconds ago instantly stops working - something no Web2 file-sharing platform allows

.

Tech: Move smart contract on Sui, Walrus for storage, Tatum for RPC, React + TypeScript frontend, AES-GCM encryption via the Web Crypto API.

About:

**Tenki** is an autonomous AI trading agent that can create, test, and verify trading strategies without human intervention. For example, a user can ask: *"Test a moving average crossover strategy on SUI-USDT using 1-hour data from the last 90 days."* Tenki automatically writes the strategy code, runs a backtest, and evaluates the results using metrics such as returns, Sharpe ratio, and drawdown. It then decides for itself whether the strategy is good enough to publish. If the performance is poor, it rejects the result. If the strategy meets its quality standards, Tenki stores the strategy file and backtest results on Walrus, a decentralized storage network, and records a cryptographic fingerprint of those files on the Sui blockchain through a Move smart contract. Every blockchain interaction is routed through Tatum's RPC gateway. This allows anyone to independently download the files from Walrus and verify that they exactly match the record stored on Sui. The result is a transparent, autonomous AI system where every published trading strategy can be independently verified, with no trusted server and no human approval required.

About:

Walrus Proof Agent turns autonomous agent decisions into verifiable audit trails on Sui and Walrus.

AI agents are increasingly used for DAO grant review, trading decisions, and operational approvals, but their evidence and reasoning often remain trapped in private app logs. Walrus Proof Agent solves this by uploading the full proof payload to Walrus and minting a compact Sui ProofReceipt object that binds the Walrus blob id, proof digest, signer, policy, and transaction.

In the live demo, a user connects Slush on Sui testnet, selects an agent workflow, uploads the proof bundle to Walrus Testnet, and mints a ProofReceipt on Sui. Reviewers can then inspect the Walrus blob, the Sui transaction, and the ProofReceipt object without trusting the frontend.

About:

VRAM Network

Trustless AI Training on Sui

AI training faces one problem.

Who decides whether a miner did the work?

Most networks rely on reputation, sampling, or trusted validators.

VRAM moves verification into hardware and settlement into Sui.

GPU miners train language models on assigned data shards.

Validators score submitted gradients inside AWS Nitro Enclaves.

The enclave measurement is registered on Sui.

Every score is signed by attested hardware.

Every reward passes through a Move contract.

No validator votes.

No trusted coordinator.

No manual review.

A modified scoring binary fails verification and receives no reward.

The result:

Work earns rewards.

Fake work earns nothing.

Math decides.

Not people.

What we built during Sui Overflow

Walrus-native gradient storage

Our v0.6 network stored gradients outside the Sui ecosystem.

We migrated gradient and checkpoint storage to Walrus.

This removes a major trust assumption.

Nitro Enclave reads directly from Walrus.

The host machine cannot replace, filter, or hide training outputs before scoring.

Validators read the same data.

Aggregators read the same data.

Top-G selection becomes deterministic.

Every participant reaches the same result from the same inputs.

Seal + Walrus

Storage credentials remain encrypted on-chain through Seal IBE.

Only attested validators decrypt them.

Walrus stores the blobs.

Seal protects access.

No trusted coordinator.

No shared secrets.

No credential leakage.

Why Sui

This design depends on three Sui technologies working together.

Walrus stores gradients and checkpoints.

Seal protects miner credentials.

Move verifies enclave signatures and settles rewards.

Remove any one component and the protocol loses guarantees.

Current testnet

- 6 active miners

- 3 validators

- 11 Move modules

- Sub-second reward settlement

- Live on Sui Testnet

- Technical paper published with DOI

- Public package and object IDs available for verification

What this enables

A decentralized AI training network where:

- Storage lives on Walrus

- Access control lives in Seal

- Verification lives in Nitro Enclaves

- Settlement lives on Sui

No trust layer between training and payment.

The protocol proves who did the work.

Sui decides who gets paid.

### 211. Arbor

- DeepSurge project: https://www.deepsurge.xyz/projects/b9ed3187-623d-440b-a133-4b557fc65d0c
- Project ID: `b9ed3187-623d-440b-a133-4b557fc65d0c`

- Created: 2026-05-30T12:29:19.180Z
- Updated: 2026-05-30T12:29:19.180Z
- Like count: 0
- Logo: https://zaelbllclkxjgibdswrj.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/public/projects/624c25ee-f700-426a-90ec-2efd06d364fb/6ee92b04-c07c-4272-acb4-47326be99998.png

Links:
- github: https://github.com/ARZER-TW/Arbor.git
- youtube: https://github.com/ARZER-TW/Arbor.git

Media:
- None listed

About:

**Git for AI agents** — verifiable version control & provenance for the artifacts AI agents produce, built on Walrus + Sui.

**Why**

AI agents are powerful but **stateless and fragmented**: they finish tasks in isolation, lose context across sessions, and have no shared, durable way to track *what they produced* or *where it came from*. Today an agent's outputs — reports, code, datasets, analyses — flow agent-to-agent over raw message passing or a shared filesystem. There is **no versioning, no lineage, no fork/merge, no access control**.

Git solved this for human developers. Agents don't have Git.

As agent outputs increasingly feed *other* agents (RAG, training data, downstream decisions) — and increasingly inform real financial and operational choices — their **provenance and auditability** stop being nice-to-haves. You need to answer: *which agent produced this, from which inputs, and can it be tampered with after the fact?*

**Arbor** is that layer: every agent artifact becomes a content-addressed blob on Walrus; the derivation relationships between artifacts form a Merkle DAG; and branching, merging, and access are enforced on Sui.

### **What it does**

- **Content-addressed artifacts** — each artifact is a Walrus blob; identical content yields the same blob id (automatic dedup, tamper-evident). On-chain you can't serve different bytes under the same id.

- **Provenance DAG** — every version is an immutable `ArtifactNode` recording its Walrus `blob_id`, creator agent, timestamp, and **parents** (`vector<ID>`, so merges can be N-way, not just 2-way).

- **Branches & merges** — agents fork branches and work in parallel; a merge records a multi-parent node. The proposer commits the merged result first, so approvers review the *actual outcome*, not a preview of changes.

- **On-chain governance** — `AccessPolicy` (writer allow-list) and `MergePolicy` (k-of-n approvals) are enforced by Sui Move, with a stale-merge check. Separation of duties: a proposer cannot approve its own merge.

- **Verifiable, durable** — content lives on decentralized Walrus storage; the Sui package-publish and every commit/merge are immutably timestamped on-chain.

### **How it works**

`Layer 4  Agent SDK (@Arbor/sdk) + Web UI (timeline / diff / approve)
Layer 3  Repository (Sui object): branches, embedded Access & Merge policies
Layer 2  ArtifactNode (frozen Sui object): blob_id (u256) + parents (DAG) + provenance
Layer 1  Walrus: content-addressed, durable blob storage
`
- **Move** (`move/Arbor`) — `artifact`, `policy`, `repository`, `merge` modules. ArtifactNodes are frozen (immutable); Repository is shared; policies are embedded structs.

- **SDK** (`sdk/`) — `ArborClient` over `@mysten/sui` v2: `createRepository / fork / commit / proposeMerge / approve / executeMerge`, plus `commitContent` (upload to Walrus → commit), `diff`, and `getTimeline` (rebuilds the DAG from events). Walrus access via `@mysten/walrus`.

- **Frontend** (`frontend/`) — Vite + React 19 + `@mysten/dapp-kit`: a git-log provenance timeline, side-by-side artifact diff (with content-addressed "identical" detection), and wallet-signed merge approval.

About:

Sui CaseFlow is a **visual fund-flow investigation workspace** for on-chain analysis on Sui. It brings address lookup, fund-flow tracing, node expansion, analyst labels, and case preservation into one workspace, turning one-off explorer queries into a continuous investigation process that can be **saved, restored, and handed off**.

Users can enter a Sui address, generate an interactive fund-flow graph, trace how assets move across nodes, and add **analyst-provided labels** such as funder, exchange_suspect, bridge, known_entity, or watch. These labels help investigators preserve judgment, identify possible trace boundaries, and reduce repeated analysis across related cases.

Each Case Snapshot can be packaged and uploaded to **Walrus**. The package includes a human-readable report, a restorable workspace snapshot, rules summary, AI notes, Walrus Memory card, manifest, and SHA-256 hashes for each artifact. This makes each case more than a static report: it becomes a **verifiable, restorable investigation file**.

Sui CaseFlow also integrates **Walrus Memory** to turn each case into compact, searchable memory. The assistant can help identify whether current graph addresses appeared in past memories with analyst labels, recall similar cases, and provide **bounded AI answers** grounded in the current case and recalled memories.

Github repo: [https://github.com/Live-Rocks/SuiCaseflow-2026](https://github.com/Live-Rocks/SuiCaseflow-2026)
Demo video: [https://youtu.be/f_N6ufhPnB4](https://youtu.be/f_N6ufhPnB4)
Investigation Report demo: [https://youtu.be/BUjjK5-8m3Y](https://youtu.be/BUjjK5-8m3Y)

About:

**SuiShield: Project Summary**

**1. Project Overview** SuiShield is an **AI-powered security scanner** designed for the Sui blockchain ecosystem. It integrates three core technologies: **Artificial Intelligence** for smart contract analysis, the **Sui blockchain** for trust and verifiability, and **Walrus decentralized storage** for permanent, tamper-proof audit records.

**2. The Problem** Current security audits in the Sui DeFi ecosystem are often **prohibitively expensive and slow** for individual developers or small teams. This creates a security gap where new protocols launch without review, leading to potential fund losses and a lack of a public registry for audit verification.

**3. The Solution** SuiShield provides **free, accessible AI-powered security reviews** for every builder on Sui. Key features include:

- **Move Contract Analysis:** Specialized detection of vulnerabilities such as reentrancy, flash loan exploits, oracle manipulation, and access control flaws.

- **Decentralized Storage (Walrus):** Audit reports are stored as content-addressed blobs on Walrus, ensuring they are permanent and censorship-resistant.

- **On-Chain Registry:** Report IDs (Blob IDs) are registered on the Sui blockchain, creating a verifiable link between the contract and its security history.

- **Live Monitoring:** Beyond static analysis, it monitors real-time DeFi risks like liquidity concentration and collateral health.

**4. The Vision** The goal is to move security from a "one-time expensive checkbox" to a **continuous, automated, and verifiable layer** integrated directly into the development workflow

About:

Track record you can actually prove, for AI Agents & Humans.

Toldproof lets anyone lock a prediction before the outcome is known. A human signs in with a Sui wallet and their X account. An AI agent does it through MCP, paying $0.10 per prediction, no signup, no API key. Either way, the prediction is scrambled and stored across Sui, Walrus, and Seal under a time-lock. Nobody can read it or change it until the unlock time arrives.

When the timer runs out, the prediction unlocks. An AI checker reads it, looks up what actually happened (news + price data), and marks it right or wrong on Sui. Its full reasoning is saved on Walrus so anyone can audit the call.

A public X bot handles "I called it" claims: tag it on any post and it replies whether the author has a real locked prediction behind that claim. One leaderboard ranks humans and AI agents side-by-side — scored on predictions they can prove they made, not screenshots.

constitutional amendment, tribunal verdict, research finding,

and ZCO consensus proof is stored as a permanent, verifiable

blob — the civilization's history that no one can erase.

research findings, ZCO proofs, encrypted file delivery, stealth

audit receipts), Seal (VIP access), Groth16 ZK Stealth Pool

sports, politics, and world events — alongside civilization-native

bets (elections, catastrophes, clan wars) and binary oracle

markets via DeepBook Predict.

About:

Problem: AI agents today are stateless and fragmented. They lose context across sessions, can't share knowledge across tools, and memory is locked to single platforms. NFTs are just static JPEGs with zero ongoing utility.

Solution: $RIOT is a multi-agent punk collective where each of 25 characters is a long-running autonomous agent with persistent memory. Users connect their Sui wallet to unlock all 25 agents. Every conversation is auto-saved to Walrus decentralized storage every 5 messages. Agents remember your name, visit count, and conversation history across sessions ,even when you switch between different agents, the memory persists.

Key Innovation: "Immortalize" . users can store any conversation permanently on Sui Mainnet via Move smart contract, creating an on-chain memory object with verifiable proof on SuiScan. Gas estimate shown before signing.

What makes it unique:

• Persistent Memory: Cross-session agent memory stored on Walrus , not local cache

• Multi-Agent Coordination: 25 specialist agents with distinct personalities that share context

• On-Chain Verification: Every memory immortalized as Move object on Sui Mainnet

• Artifact-Driven Workflows: Chat logs and agent responses stored as verifiable Walrus blobs

• Wallet-Gated Access: Connect Sui wallet to unlock all 25 agents

• Punk Aesthetic: Graffiti, glitch, neon UI with custom punk fonts

• Built on Sui: Programmable object model + Walrus for decentralized persistence

25 agents with unique personalities ready. Walrus integration and Move smart contract active on Mainnet.
